# UnaOS Security — model, threat framing, hardening ledger

> Engineering companion to the design vision in
> [`dev/OS/04_SECURITY_IMMUNITY/`](dev/OS/04_SECURITY_IMMUNITY) — the
> permission model there ("no root; apps start with zero permissions; the OS
> passes single file handles, not folder access") is the direction; this
> document tracks the concrete threat model, the implementation chain, and the
> per-arch hardening state. Arc sequencing lives in [`ROADMAP.md`](ROADMAP.md).
>
> Rule: **an arc that lands a ledger item checks it off here in the same
> commit.**

## Threat framing

UnaOS is being built to hold *direct authority over physical hardware*:
G-code streams to a 3-D printer, PWM to a vehicle's throttle, GPIO, SDR. A
compromise is not just data loss — it has physical consequences. Security is
therefore foundation work, not a retrofit (BeOS deferred multi-user and paid
for it; we won't repeat that).

Exposure classes, honestly stated:

1. **Ring-0-resident parsers.** The network stack (Ethernet/ARP/IP/TCP/DHCP),
   USB descriptor parsing, the FAT reader, and (since UNAFS-K3, aarch64) the
   unafs mount parser (partition table / superblock / inode / catalog decode
   in `fs/unafs.rs` + `unaos/libs/fs/unafs`) all parse attacker-influenceable bytes with
   full kernel privilege. As of UNAFS-K4 the unafs mount is read-WRITE (the
   kernel commits blocks to the medium through it — see the K4 write-path scope
   at the bottom of this ledger). Until the privilege boundary exists everywhere, these
   are the practical attack surface, and they remain kernel-resident even after.
   The unafs parser joined on the same basis FAT shipped on, and is now the
   first of the four hardened against a crafted volume (BEFS-HARDEN,
   2026-07-13): every disk-derived size/count/offset is bounded against the
   volume span at the parse boundary (`Superblock::validate`), disk-sized
   allocations are fallible (`try_reserve` → graceful `Err`, never a capacity
   panic or OOM abort), extent arithmetic is `checked_*`
   (profile-independent), hole-fill/free walks are bounded, and record decodes
   carry bincode byte budgets so a crafted length prefix fails BEFORE
   allocating. Residual, honestly stated: for extent-backed records the decode
   pre-allocation is capped (4 MiB record ceiling — the panel lowered it from 64 MiB, which exceeded the 48 MiB kernel heap and so was itself an abort vector; a passing bincode claim allocates infallibly, so the ceiling IS the max forced allocation), not eliminated. The FAT
   reader's equivalent audit is still open — see the checklist below.
2. **Privilege boundary not yet load-bearing (both arches).** The ring-3/EL0
   boundary now exists on BOTH architectures (x86 U1a/U1b: ring-3 round-trip +
   per-page perms + fault→task-kill; aarch64 M6a/M6b, the pioneer). x86 U2 runs
   the FIRST real program loaded FROM DISK in it (a flat ring-3 binary read off
   the FAT volume) — **metal-confirmed on the real 2012 rMBP (2026-07-03): a
   Realtek USB3 SD reader → FAT16 card → `HELLO.BIN` (72 B) read + executed in
   ring 3 (`hello from disk` -> PASS)**. But that program is still trusted only by
   ring-3 isolation (no signature/allowlist check yet — U2's untrusted-loader note
   below), and the kernel's own subsystems (drivers, FS, net) remain ring-0.
3. **No identity or authorization layer yet** — no principals, no grants;
   any code that runs can do anything. This is what the U-chain builds.

## The model: capabilities first, POSIX-layerable

Decision (2026-07-02): UnaOS targets a **single primary human with
capability-isolated software principals**, not classical login/uid multi-user.

- Every vessel, handler, and agent is a **principal** with an explicit grant
  set (e.g. Comscan: `serial:acm0`, `gpio:*`; Vug: `fs:/models rw`; an
  untrusted download: nothing).
- **Handles are capabilities.** The per-process handle table is the enforcement
  point: every syscall that touches a resource goes through it. Grant, attenuate,
  revoke are first-class operations; handle transfer over the bandy bus is
  unforgeable. *(Status: arc **U4** built the table STRUCTURE — child-process
  handles, keyed by ASID, with reaping owner-scoped by construction — on aarch64;
  arc **U5** (2026-07-05, aarch64) turned it into the CHECK — handles now carry a
  rights bitmask, checked at the single lookup point (`handle_resolve`); grant/
  attenuate/revoke are first-class (`SYS_CAP`, with the monotonic-decrease
  attenuation invariant enforced); `sys_write` routes through the table (a
  `CONSOLE` handle with `CAP_WRITE`, endowed at spawn — no ambient stdout); and a
  process's handles are cleared on ASID teardown. Arc **U6a** (2026-07-06, aarch64)
  generalized the descriptor into a **general object table** — a handle is now
  `(kind, target, rights)` with the kind (`Child`/`Console`/`File`/`Socket`) in a
  parallel sidecar, first-free-allocated for ALL kinds; the fixed `CONSOLE_FD` pin
  is retired (the reserved-index allocator closes the print-AND-spawn collision).
  The x86 twin **U6x** (2026-07-06) mirrors it (keyed by address-space SLOT rather
  than ASID). Arc **U6b** (2026-07-06, aarch64) made the `File` kind REAL —
  `SYS_OPEN`/`SYS_READ` route a disk read through a `File` handle gated by
  `CAP_READ` (the first resource syscall on a non-Console object, backed by a
  per-task open-file table; the hook UnaFS `grants:*` enforcement will ride).
  Its x86 twin **U6bx** (2026-07-07) brings the arches to File-handle parity,
  serving reads from a **BSP-staged source** (the IF-masked x86 SYSCALL path
  cannot drive the `hlt()`-ing xHCI read; the staged-set constraint was
  RETIRED by the STOR-1 S1–S7 arc — the interrupt-driven storage service task —
  which routes reads live and, at S7, resolves an open of ANY on-disk file).
  Arc **U7** (2026-07-07, aarch64) landed the cross-process CORE: an
  inbox-mediated `SYS_XFER`/`SYS_RECV` (attenuated, owner-scoped, the
  single-writer-per-row invariant preserved by construction) + a sender-owned
  single-level revoke that reaches the received cap at its next resolve.
  Its x86 twin **U7x** (2026-07-07) restores full parity (keyed by
  address-space SLOT; the shared kernel window is refused as a transfer
  endpoint) and folds both U6bx ledger notes (atomic offset claim;
  File-revoke descriptor release).
  Arc **U8** (2026-07-07, aarch64) closed U7's two documented escapes:
  revocation TREES (a derivation ledger — revoke chases re-grants and
  re-transfers, and `CAP_REVOKE` gained its real subtree semantics) and
  generation-tagged inboxes (the sys_xfer TOCTOU residue).
  Arc **U9** (2026-07-07, aarch64) gave `CAP_WRITE` on a `File` real meaning —
  EMMC2 sector writes, an in-place-only `fat::write_at`, `SYS_SEEK`, and a
  File+`CAP_WRITE`-routed `sys_write` (the same `handle_resolve` CHECK, so a
  revoked write-cap is `-EACCES` for free) — the last rung before UnaFS
  `grants:*` on open. Its x86 twin **U8x** (2026-07-07) restores full parity (a
  slot-keyed derivation ledger + per-slot inbox generations; the shared kernel
  window stays refused as a transfer endpoint), so the whole capability chain —
  U4→U8/U8x — is now arch-symmetric through revocation trees.
  Arc **U10** (2026-07-08, aarch64) gave the FAT writer an ALLOCATOR: file
  **growth** (`fat::write_grow` — allocate + zero-fill + chain new clusters, dir
  `size` bumped LAST), **create** (`SYS_OPEN` `O_CREAT` → `fat::create_in_root`),
  and **delete** (`SYS_UNLINK` → `fat::delete_located`). Grow, create, AND delete
  all ride `sys_write`'s ONE `handle_resolve(asid, fd, CAP_WRITE)` CHECK (create
  via an O_CREAT open that endows the write cap), so an RO-opened / U8-revoked /
  wrong-kind handle can never mutate the volume — no new enforcement code. The
  FAT-safety invariants are load-bearing: every FAT mutation writes ALL `num_fats`
  copies; the free search is bounded and returns `-ENOSPC` when full; a new cluster
  is **zero-filled before it joins a chain** (no stale-byte disclosure into a grown
  region); the directory `size` (the reader's truth) is written last so a crash
  mid-grow leaves a consistent smaller file; and delete marks the dir entry `0xE5`
  BEFORE freeing the chain, so a crash can never leave a live entry aliasing freed
  clusters. Additive on the shared `fat.rs` — x86 never calls the writers.
  Its x86 counterpart **U9x** (2026-07-08) brings the arches to File-WRITE parity:
  `SYS_SEEK`, an RW `SYS_OPEN` mode bit, and a File+`CAP_WRITE`-routed `sys_write`
  (the same single `handle_resolve` CHECK, so a revoked write-cap is `-EACCES` for
  free). Because the IF-masked x86 SYSCALL handler cannot drive the `hlt()`-ing
  xHCI write pump, the write is **STAGED** — an in-place overwrite of a
  per-descriptor in-memory buffer a read-back witnesses through the same cap (the
  write twin of U6bx's staged read); M1 is in-memory only, the BSP-side disk
  write-back is M2. Its ALLOCATOR twin **U10x** (2026-07-08) then landed
  grow/create/delete on x86 — every FAT mutation DEFERRED out of the IF-masked
  handler to a launcher IF=1 drain (a separate op-queue; the shared `fat.rs` writers
  reused VERBATIM, x86 adds only a read-only `cluster_size()` accessor). It rides the
  SAME single `handle_resolve(row, fd, CAP_WRITE)` CHECK (grow and unlink are
  mutations; create via an O_CREAT open that endows the write cap), plus a scaffold
  guard that refuses `SYS_UNLINK` on an immutable STAGED file (HELLO.BIN is EL0 code —
  only a runtime-created descriptor is unlinkable, a flag reset on every slot free) and
  a per-descriptor `FILE_OPNAME` binding so a deferred op ALWAYS names the descriptor's
  OWN file (no confused-deputy: a RW SCRATCH.BIN holder can never mint a GROW.BIN op).
  x86 divergences are ledgered honestly: the deferred DELETE is a launcher-side replay
  (weaker causal fidelity than pi4's in-handler unlink), its sibling-`-EACCES` proves
  gen-invalidation not a freed-chain aliasing fail-safe (no on-disk chain exists
  pre-drain), and grow is one-page-bounded. **U11x M2** (2026-07-10) then brought the
  arches to unlink-defers-free parity: a GLOBAL per-name open refcount defers the
  deleted file's op to the LAST close ACROSS processes (teardown counts as close),
  so the delete now FIRES at the correct lifecycle point even though the disk half
  remains a replay (the causal-fidelity gap closes at the lifecycle layer). UnaFS `owner`/`grants:*` enforcement on the
  by-name namespace — the last cap gap — landed on the **aarch64** track as **U6**
  (owned-by-default at `SYS_OPEN` + `SYS_FGRANT` delegation, an in-kernel seam the
  on-disk attributes will feed; see the aarch64 ledger); the **x86 twin (U6x)**, the
  bandy Ring-3 delegation wrapper, subdirs/LFN/rename, and write-back caching remain
  future. See both ledgers below.)*
- **UnaFS stores the metadata natively**: `owner` and `grants:*` are ordinary
  typed attributes — queryable ("show every file the midi-agent can write"),
  and no on-disk format change is ever needed.
- **POSIX hedge**: if human multi-user is ever wanted, a "user" becomes a
  named bundle of capabilities layered on the same attributes. Nothing in the
  chain below has to be redone.

Implementation chain: **U1a → U1b → U2 → U3 → U4 → U5 → U6** (x86 leads, Pi
ports, Jetson joins — full table in `ROADMAP.md` §1).

## Hardening ledger

### x86_64
- [x] Ring-3 boundary: user GDT segments (DPL-3 code/data, SYSRET-compatible STAR), `TSS.RSP0`, SYSCALL/SYSRET path (`EFER.SCE`, `LSTAR`, `STAR`, `FMASK`) (U1a, 2026-07-02, **QEMU-verified; metal pending**)
- [x] EFER.NXE enabled; NX on all user data/stack pages; user **code page W^X enforced across cores** — mapped ring3-RX **read-only from the start** at `USER_BASE` (the blob is copied through the identity alias, never through `USER_BASE`), so no core can ever cache a writable mapping of it; supersedes the U1a single-core `invlpg` flip (U1a/**U1b B4**, 2026-07-02, **QEMU-verified + metal-confirmed** on the real 2012 rMBP: `u1b-code-write` faulted with `err=0x7` (Write bit set) on the real page tables)
- [x] User fault → task kill, never kernel halt — a ring-3 fault (`CS.RPL == 3`) on #PF/#GP/#UD/#SS/#NP/#DE/#BR/#AC `swapgs`es, logs `(task, vector, cr2)`, and kills the task via `sched::exit()`; a CPL-0 fault stays fatal (kernel bug never hidden). (**U1b A**, 2026-07-02, **QEMU-verified + metal-confirmed** on the real 2012 rMBP: 3 kills at `vec=14 err=0x7/0x7/0x15`, `exited=1 killed=3 -> PASS`, **kernel continued past all three kills** — proving `swapgs`/GS restore work on silicon)
- [x] SYSRET boundary hardening: caller-saved GPR scrub (`rdi/rsi/rdx/r8/r9/r10` zeroed before `sysretq` — no kernel-dispatcher pointer leaks to ring 3) + canonical-`rcx` guard (a non-canonical return RIP — the CVE-2012-0217 shape — is refused and the task killed, never `sysret`ed with the user rsp loaded) (**U1b B1/B2**, 2026-07-02, **QEMU-verified + metal-confirmed for the pass-through** — the ring-3 round-trip returns cleanly through the scrub+guard tail on real silicon. The guard's *refusal* logic is now **logic-tested** (U2 Part-0c, 2026-07-02: the callable twin `rcx_canonical` refuses `0x8000_0000_0000_0000`, QEMU-verified — `:: U2-0c: canonical-rcx guard refuses … -> PASS ::`); the end-to-end refusal — ring-3 code actually returning to a non-canonical RIP, which is unreachable from our 1 TiB window — is still future)
- [x] NMI on a dedicated IST stack — an NMI landing in the pre-`swapgs`/pre-stack-switch window of `unaos_syscall_entry` switches to a kernel stack unconditionally, so its frame can never be pushed onto the ring-3 stack; the handler stays GS-free (**U1b B3** + **U2 Part-0c**, 2026-07-02, **QEMU-verified, fire path now exercised**: a real self-NMI — `apic::send_ipi(<own apic id>, 0x4400)`, level-assert | NMI delivery mode — is confirmed **taken on the NMI IST stack** (the handler compares its RSP against the IST bounds): `:: U2-0c: self-NMI taken on IST -> PASS ::`. This positively demonstrates the unconditional IST switch, upgrading the earlier "slot installed" claim. Still future: metal re-confirmation of the self-NMI, and a fixture that lands the NMI *specifically* mid-syscall-entry — the IST switch is CPL-independent, so the self-NMI is representative, but the exact window has not been targeted)
- [x] CR4.SMEP set — code gates it on `CPUID.7:EBX.SMEP` and sets it when present; **metal-confirmed active on the real 2012 rMBP** (`:: SMEP on ::` photographed 2026-07-02 — TCG `qemu64` does **not** expose SMEP, logging `SMEP unsupported`, so this is the one line QEMU cannot show). A SMEP-*violation* functional test (supervisor fetch from a ring-3 page → #PF) is still future. **SMAP unavailable pre-Broadwell** — compensate with explicit `copy_from_user` discipline
- [x] #DB (vector 1) + #MC (vector 18) each on a dedicated IST stack (**U2 Part-0a**, 2026-07-02). #DB closes a user-triggerable DoS: `RFLAGS.TF` is writable at any CPL (`popfq`), so ring 3 can arm single-step then `SYSCALL`; the pending trap lands on the first `LSTAR` instruction at CPL 0 with GS/RSP still ring-3, and with #DB absent from the IDT that escalated to a same-CPL #NP whose frame landed on the user-writable stack → misread as a kernel fault → halt. The GS-free #DB handler kills a ring-3 #DB, and for a CPL-0 #DB whose RIP is inside the `unaos_syscall_entry` stub clears TF and `iretq`s (resumes the syscall); any other CPL-0 #DB is fatal. #MC is fatal on its own IST. **QEMU-verified**: installed + boot-verified, `:: U2-0a: TF+SYSCALL survived -> PASS ::` (kernel not halted). **Fire path of the #DB *resume* branch is metal-only** — QEMU TCG does not model the TF-on-`SYSCALL` trap (`db_resumed=0`; the fixture exits cleanly instead), so the resume itself is exercised only on real silicon; the #MC fire path is untested (no machine-check fixture). **DR7 is now cleared per-CPU at syscall init (U2.5 Part 0-ii, 2026-07-03, QEMU-verified)** — `mov dr7, 0` beside the SMEP write, logged once as `:: U2.5-0: DR7 cleared ::` — so a firmware-left hardware breakpoint can no longer raise a #DB inside the entry stub: the resume policy's "TF is the only #DB source there" assumption is now *enforced*, not merely assumed
- [x] First-entry GPR scrub (`user_task_trampoline`) — every GPR but `rsp` is zeroed after the five `iretq`-frame words are pushed and before `iretq`, so no live kernel value (the Task Box pointer, kernel-stack top, entry VA) reaches ring 3 in a register at first entry. The x86 twin of the aarch64 M6d first-`eret` scrub; complements the U1b SYSRET scrub, which covered only the return half (**U2 Part-0b**, 2026-07-02, **QEMU-verified**: U1a/U1b/U2 ring-3 entry all still function — the scrub does not disturb the entry ABI, which takes no register arguments)
- [x] **W^X map audit — the live page tables are walked, classified, and the ring-3 half is ENFORCED (WXAUDIT, 2026-08-06, QEMU-verified; metal-pending — pure page-table reads, no device dependency).** The x86 twin of the aarch64 H1/M6c audit table below, which x86 had gone without since U1a: the ring-3 W^X claim above ("user code page W^X enforced across cores") was true *by construction* but **nothing checked it at runtime**, so a future edit to `build_slot`/`map_user_page` could have retired it silently — the same shape as the GR15 defect where `map_mmio_window` un-typed the framebuffer from WC to UC unnoticed for two weeks. **The fold, not the leaf.** Effective permission on x86-64 is the fold of every entry on the PML4→PDPT→PD→PT path — ring-3 reach is the AND of U/S, writability the AND of W, executability the NOT of the OR of NX — so `wx_fold`/`wx_violates` accumulate down the walk; a leaf-only checker would both miss violations and manufacture them. Huge leaves (1 GiB/2 MiB) are classified at their own level. **The vacuity leg (why this is not a decorative counter):** every NX conclusion is void unless `EFER.NXE` is actually set — with NXE=0 the hardware ignores bit 63 and every present page is executable — so the audit READS EFER.NXE, prints it, and when it is clear classifies with NX disregarded. The audit cannot report a clean map on a kernel whose NX is not armed. **What is ENFORCED (the ring-3 half — where the boundary actually lives):** `wx_check_slot` walks the slot's own user branch on **every** `alloc_user_space` and panics rather than hand out an address space containing a ring-3-reachable W∧X page — it checks the LIVE table bytes, not the constants `build_slot` meant to write; and `map_user_page` (the one ring-3 mapping seam that took `writable`/`nx` as independent arguments, with no guard) now refuses a W+X request, matching the assert `protect_user_slot_range` already carried. **What is REPORTED, not fixed:** see the x86 W^X map census below — the kernel's own identity map is coarse supervisor-RWX, exactly the aarch64 H1 finding, and splitting it into RO-executable text and NX data/heap/MMIO is a separate review-gated arc (the x86 twin of the deferred WXN refactor). **Falsifiability — both layers proven able to fire, not merely to print:** (1) `WXAUDIT-0` is a four-leg negative control on the pure classifier — it must flag a synthetic ring-3 W+X leaf, must NOT flag the real U1b B4 code-page shape (RO+X — a false positive there would have the audit failing every boot and being switched off), must let an NX **parent** veto an executable leaf (the OR-accumulation leg a leaf-only checker fails), and must re-flag that same page when NXE is clear (the vacuity leg); a boot whose classifier fails this panics before reporting anything. (2) The end-to-end path was falsified in-tree: dropping `PTE_NX` from `build_slot`'s data/stack flags made the boot panic at `wx_check_slot` with `slot 0 has 3 page(s) both writable and executable` — exactly the window's three data/stack pages, page 0 (RO code) correctly untouched — and the injection was reverted. Prior note, still accurate: `CR0.WP` is briefly cleared around the U1a/U1b/U2 page-table edits (firmware maps its tables read-only) and restored; scope is the mapper only. (The U2 loader writes the loaded program through the code frame's identity alias, NOT through `USER_BASE`, and needs no `CR0.WP` toggle — the frame is ordinary RW heap; the ring-3 mapping stays read-only, so W^X across cores holds by construction.)
- [x] **Kernel-half W^X, milestone 1 — the PDPT-level NX sweep, the first NX bit this kernel has ever put in its own map (WXN-x86 M1, 2026-08-06, ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED on the real 2012 rMBP — Boot W, 2026-08-06, kernel `7748d22c` @ `68370d6f`).** The census below reports the kernel's own identity map as coarse supervisor-RWX and names the split as a separate, review-gated arc. M1 is the first milestone of that arc, and it flew. `memory::wxn_sweep` ORs bit 63 into every present, supervisor-only **PDPT** entry except the one or two 1 GiB regions that must stay executable — the kernel image, and the low GiB holding the AP trampoline. **Parents, not leaves.** Hardware folds execute permission as the OR of NX down the whole PML4→PDPT→PD→PT path, and `wx_fold` folds it the same way, so ONE bit on a PDPT entry retires 512 (or 262144) leaves at once, in silicon and in the count: ~1022 entry writes instead of 66047. The decisive secondary benefit is blast radius, not arithmetic — every write is a read-modify-write that sets exactly one bit and nothing else, so the GR15 shape (a whole-PTE rewrite that silently dropped the panel's PAT bit and cost 8.7–9.1× on the blit path) is avoided structurally rather than by discipline. It runs in `arch::init` between `syscall::init()` (bit 63 is RESERVED, not ignored, with EFER.NXE clear — the sweep re-reads NXE itself and REFUSES rather than trusting the call site) and `wx_audit_report()`, so the existing WXAUDIT census line IS the success signature. **The metal readings:**

    ```
    :: WXN-x86: ehdr=0x7B233000 img=[0x7B233000,0x7B8E6DEA) gib_img=1 gib_tramp=0 spare_n=2 pdpt_seen=1024 nx_set=1022 huge_leaf_nx=0 skip_spare=2 skip_user=0 skip_pml4_user=0 skip_selfmap=0 already_nx=0 skip_fb_lock=0 skip_fb_base=0 skip_fb_walk=0 residue_leaves=1535 (1g=0 2m=1023 4k=512 pt=1) pge=0 flush=cr3-reload wp=0 -> SWEPT ::
    :: WXAUDIT x86: leaves=66047 user=0 user_WX=0 kern_WX=1535 (2048 MiB) tables=1028 nxe=1 walk=1720kcyc l1=0 l2=65535 l3=512 ::
    :: WXN-FBWC: fb=0x90020000 lvl=2 e=0x00000000900010E3 pat=1 pcd=0 pwt=0 w=1 fx=0 -> LEAF BIT-IDENTICAL ::
    :: WXAUDIT-NXE: cores=8 nxe=8 nxe_mask=0xFF wp=0 wp_mask=0x0 -> PASS ::
    ```

    **`kern_WX` 66047 → 1535 on metal — the identity map is now ~97.7 % supervisor-NX**, and the residue is exactly the two spared GiBs (`2048 MiB`): the trampoline's and the image's. **The k-family was decided ON THE BOOT, not argued.** The prior metal baseline (`leaves=66047`, `tables=1028`, byte total degenerate in k) does not pin the leaf mix — the whole family `n1 = k`, `n4 = 512(k+1)`, `n2 = 65536 − 512k − (k+1)` satisfies every printed aggregate for each k ≥ 0 — so the predicted residue was **1535 or 2046**. The O1 leaf histogram answered it in one reading: `l1=0` ⇒ **k = 0** (no 1 GiB leaves on this Apple EFI map), residue **1535**, the natural `GiB0 = 511×2M + 512×4K`, `GiB1 = 512×2M`. The family-invariant predictions `pdpt_seen=1024`, `nx_set=1022`, `skip_spare=2` all came in exact, and `huge_leaf_nx=0` — this sweep wrote no leaf on this platform. **The GR15 watch is clean:** `WXN-FBWC … pat=1 … -> LEAF BIT-IDENTICAL` says the framebuffer's leaf PTE is bit-for-bit what it was before the sweep (WC preserved) while its folded `fx=0` says the parent above it did change — one instrument, two independent claims. **The AP NXE fix is proven on all eight cores, and metal contradicted QEMU on WP.** `WXAUDIT-NXE: cores=8 nxe=8 nxe_mask=0xFF` — every core has EFER.NXE armed, so no core translates bit 63 as reserved; without this the sweep's NX conclusions would be void on seven of eight cores. The same line carries a **metal-only finding**: `wp=0 wp_mask=0x0` — **CR0.WP is clear on EVERY core, the BSP included.** QEMU's BSP reads `wp=1`, so the standing "the BSP has WP" reading was a QEMU artifact and never a statement about this hardware; **M3's WP work is therefore all-eight-cores, not AP-only.** NX enforcement does not depend on WP — it is EFER.NXE and bit 63 and nothing else — so M1 is fully real on metal; but the read-only half of W^X does not bind ring 0 yet, and `wp_mask` says so on the wire rather than leaving it to be assumed. **Cost:** `walk=1720kcyc` against the 1695 kcyc metal baseline — the O1 histogram costs ~25 kcyc on metal, negligible, and the figure is printed precisely so a future map change that inflates it is visible. **Next milestones, both open:** **M2** — the huge-leaf splitter plus a static page-table pool, which is what will let the two spared GiBs be reduced to the pages that actually need X; **M3** — `.text` RX (clearing W), `CR0.WP` armed per core, and trampoline isolation. The counter-hazard M1 hands its successors, recorded here so it is inherited rather than rediscovered: an NX'd non-leaf entry silently vetoes any FUTURE executable mapping created beneath it — nothing in the tree creates one today (`map_mmio_window` mints NX leaves; ring 3 lives under a separate PML4 slot at 1 TiB), but a later arc that maps executable memory under an identity GiB will find it non-executable for reasons no leaf can explain.
- [x] **Kernel-half W^X, milestone 2 — the huge-leaf splitter: ring-0 X is confined to the image's non-writable `PT_LOAD` union plus the one AP trampoline page (WXN-x86 M2, 2026-08-06, ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED on the real 2012 rMBP — Boot Y, 2026-08-06, media built at `776fb13c`).** M1 retired 64 512 of the 66 047 leaves with 1022 parent writes and left two whole 1 GiB regions — the AP trampoline's and the kernel image's — executable, because a parent bit is all-or-nothing over its subtree. M2 opens those two GiBs. The sweep reads the loaded ELF's program headers, takes the **union of the NON-WRITABLE `PT_LOAD` segments** as the executable window, and NX's everything else inside the spared GiBs: whole 2 MiB leaves where the window does not reach them, and a 2 MiB → 4 KiB **split** where it does, out of a static 16-table `.bss` pool whose sizing arithmetic is written at the site. A **read-only pre-pass** counts the tables the split will need before anything is written, so pool exhaustion is a printed `REFUSED` with an unmodified map rather than a half-split one. Each split copies the parent's attributes through `WXN_LEAF_CARRY`, which **excludes bits 7 and 12 and translates the huge-leaf PAT bit 12 into the 4 KiB PAT bit 7 by hand**, and refuses outright to split the framebuffer's WC leaf — the GR15 regression (a whole-PTE rewrite that silently dropped the panel's PAT bit and cost 8.7–9.1× on the blit path) designed out structurally, not avoided by care. New tables are populated with their final values **before** the single parent store, so no core ever walks a partially built table. **The metal readings:**

    ```
    :: WXN-M2: xseg=[0x7B21C000,0x7B34C000) xsegs=2 xpages=304 tramp=0x8000 spare_n=2 demote_1g=0 split_2m=1 pool_used=1/16 nx_pdpt=0 nx_2m=1022 nx_pt=0 nx_4k=719 keep_x=305 already_nx=0 skip_user=0 fb=0x90020000 fb_delta=0x0 pge=0 flush=cr3-reload -> SPLIT ::
    :: WXAUDIT x86: leaves=66558 user=0 user_WX=0 kern_WX=305 (1 MiB) tables=1029 nxe=1 walk=1721kcyc l1=0 l2=65534 l3=1024 ::
    :: WXN-FBWC: fb=0x90020000 lvl=2 e=0x00000000900010E3 pat=1 pcd=0 pwt=0 w=1 fx=0 -> LEAF BIT-IDENTICAL ::
    :: WXAUDIT-NXE: cores=8 nxe=8 nxe_mask=0xFF wp=0 wp_mask=0x0 -> PASS ::
    :: WXPROBE map: at=bss va=0x7B4A9000 lvl=2M e=0x800000007B4000E3 p=1 w=1 u=0 nx=1 g=0 pat=0 pcd=0 pwt=0 fw=1 fx=0 fu=0 ::
    ```

    **`kern_WX` 1535 → 305 leaves, `2048 MiB` → `1 MiB` of ring-0 W∧X — the identity map is now ~99.5 % supervisor-NX**, and the residue is no longer a pair of GiB-sized regions but 304 image pages plus the single trampoline page at `0x8000`. **Every prediction came in exact, and three independent derivations agree on the same number:** `keep_x = xpages + 1 = 305`, the census's own `kern_WX = 305`, and `leaves = 66047 + 511 × split_2m = 66558` (one 2 MiB leaf replaced by 512 4 KiB leaves), with `tables = 1028 + 1 = 1029`, `pool_used=1/16`, and the sweep's own arithmetic closing on the wire (`nx_2m=1022` of the 1023 whole 2 MiB leaves in the spared GiBs, `nx_4k=719` + `keep_x=305` = the 1024 4 KiB leaves). `demote_1g=0` and `nx_pt=0` are the two Apple-EFI-shaped predictions, both confirmed: `l1=0` (no 1 GiB leaves — M1's finding) means nothing needed demoting, and the low GiB already reaches `0x8000` through an existing 4 KiB PT. **The GR15 watch is clean on both arms** — `fb_delta=0x0` on the M2 line (the sweep's own claim that it wrote nothing at the framebuffer) and `WXN-FBWC … pat=1 … -> LEAF BIT-IDENTICAL` (the independent re-read). And `WXPROBE map: at=bss … nx=1 fx=0` is the positive reading no aggregate can give: a named kernel *data* VA, read back from the live tables, folded non-executable.

    **Boot Z re-reading (2026-08-06, metal, capture `rmbp-gr16-s73`, media built at trunk `3aa2b7a4`) — `kern_WX` 305 → 319, and the rise is CODE GROWTH, not lost coverage.** The Boot Z media carries CFU-2 (`user_range_leaf_ok` plus its witness) and the kepler decomposition instrument, and the sweep reads the bigger image:

    ```
    :: WXN-M2: xseg=[0x7B1FB000,0x7B339000) xsegs=2 xpages=318 tramp=0x8000 spare_n=2 demote_1g=0 split_2m=2 pool_used=2/16 nx_pdpt=0 nx_2m=1021 nx_pt=0 nx_4k=1217 keep_x=319 already_nx=0 skip_user=0 fb=0x90020000 fb_delta=0x0 pge=0 flush=cr3-reload -> SPLIT ::
    :: WXAUDIT x86: leaves=67069 user=0 user_WX=0 kern_WX=319 (1 MiB) tables=1030 nxe=1 walk=1729kcyc l1=0 l2=65533 l3=1536 ::
    :: WXN-FBWC: fb=0x90020000 lvl=2 e=0x00000000900010E3 pat=1 pcd=0 pwt=0 w=1 fx=0 -> LEAF BIT-IDENTICAL ::
    :: WXAUDIT-NXE: cores=8 nxe=8 nxe_mask=0xFF wp=0 wp_mask=0x0 -> PASS ::
    ```

    `xpages` 304 → 318 is 14 pages of new code inside the non-writable `PT_LOAD` union; the wider extent now straddles **two** 2 MiB boundaries, so `split_2m` goes 1 → 2 and `pool_used` 1/16 → 2/16 — the splitter handling growth exactly as designed, with the arithmetic still closing (`leaves = 66047 + 511 × split_2m = 67069`, `tables = 1028 + 2 = 1030`, `l3` 1024 → 1536, and `nx_4k=1217` + `keep_x=319` = the 1536 4 KiB leaves). **The discriminator between growth and regression is the three-way identity, and it holds exactly: `keep_x = xpages + 1 = kern_WX = 319`.** A coverage loss breaks it — the sweep would be keeping X on pages the ELF window does not claim, or the census would be counting W∧X leaves the sweep never visited — whereas code growth moves all three terms together, which is what happened. **Until M3b clears `W` from `.text`, every code page is W∧X by construction, so `kern_WX` measures the size of the executable image and not a leak**; the invariant to watch across boots is the identity, not the count, and the two decouple only when the `W` column closes. `pool_used=2/16` leaves 14 tables of headroom, and the GR15 framebuffer watch is clean on both arms again (`fb_delta=0x0` and the independent `LEAF BIT-IDENTICAL pat=1` re-read). (The bench `serial-analyzer --wxn` raised `WXN-KERNWX-ROSE` on the Boot Y+Z pair: **correct to fire, wrong in its diagnosis** — it read the rise as coverage having SHRUNK. The rule is being refined to test the identity rather than the sign of the delta.)

    **The finding that chose the design, and the debt it hands M3.** The first cut took X from the ELF's `PF_X` segments only. It booted and **died** — `PROTECTION_VIOLATION | INSTRUCTION_FETCH` at `switch_context`, which lives in `.rodata`: `smp.rs`'s trampoline `global_asm!` opens `.section .rodata` and never returns to `.text`, and rustc concatenates a crate's `global_asm!` blocks, so live ring-0 task-switch code sits in a `PF_X=0` segment. **`PF_W` is the honest bit for this image, not `PF_X`** — hence the non-writable union, which is wider than `.text` and says so. The consequence is recorded here so it is inherited rather than rediscovered: **`.text`-RO cannot be called W^X-complete while `switch_context` lives in `.rodata`**; that `.section` leak is an `smp.rs` fix owed by M3.

    **CLOSED (`eb6cd7c2`, 2026-08-06).** `.pushsection`/`.popsection` + an explicit `.code64` restore: the block now composes wherever the codegen partitioner puts it, and the trampoline bytes stay in `.rodata` deliberately (they are copied to `0x8000`, never executed in place). Adversarially reviewed and verified on the built ELF — `switch_context` moves from `Ndx 6` at `ap_trampoline_end`'s address to `Ndx 9` (`.text`) at `0x10fc84`, the trampoline blob is byte-identical by sha256, and the section/phdr lists, the QEMU `WXN-M2` numbers and the check warning multiset are all unchanged. **Two corrections to the model above, both from that review:** the concatenation unit is the **codegen unit, not the crate** — a default-feature build of the same commit puts `switch_context` in `.text`, so *which* block a leak poisons varies with the feature set and the compiler version, and the defect is properly read as "an arbitrary suffix of one CGU's asm" rather than as a bug about `switch_context`. And the invariant is **not** one every other `global_asm!` observes: **the aarch64 side has the same leak class, unfixed** — `main.rs:33` opens `.section .text.boot, "ax", %progbits` and never restores, with `pi-baremetal.ld` placing `KEEP(*(.text.boot))` first at the load address the VideoCore ROM jumps to, and `exceptions.rs:116` leaks `.text.exception_vectors` the same way. Same trigger profile, latent boot-brick; **the Pi track's files — reported here, not touched.** The durable guard is a post-link gate (no GLOBAL symbol but the trampoline's own may live in `.rodata`), which catches the class rather than the instance.

    **What is still open is the `W` column, and its current state is on the wire.** Every executable page remains writable (305 on Boot Y, 319 on Boot Z — the figure tracks the image; `WXPROBE map: at=ktext … w=1 fw=1 fx=1`) and **`CR0.WP` is clear on all eight cores** — `wp=0 wp_mask=0x0`, the metal-only finding M1 recorded and Boot Y reproduces (QEMU's BSP reads `wp=1`; that was an artifact, never a statement about this hardware). **M3** is therefore: `.text` RX (clearing W), `CR0.WP` armed per core on all eight, the `smp.rs` `.section` fix above, and trampoline isolation. Until it lands the kernel half enforces **NX but not RO** — the map constrains what may execute, not what may be written, and `wp_mask` says so on the wire rather than leaving it to be assumed. **The milestone order inverted deliberately, and the prerequisite has now landed on metal.** M3a (arming `CR0.WP` per core) was the named next step, but CFU-2 below is a kernel-writer-into-a-ring-3-RO+X-page bypass that WP would convert from silent corruption into a fatal CPL-0 `#PF` — a ring-3-triggerable halt — so CFU-2 had to precede it. It did, and Boot Z proves it on silicon (`CFU2-WGATE … -> PASS`, all five arms). Boot Z's `wp=0 wp_mask=0x0` is therefore the *expected* reading and not a miss: M3a is not aboard that media, by design. M3a has since landed (committed at `37fc0f3c`, staged for Boot AA — see its own entry below), and so has **M3b**, which clears the `W` bits themselves: on an armed boot the "NX but not RO" reading above is superseded — Boot AI, 2026-08-07, `kern_WX 337 → 1`. Everything in this paragraph describes Boots Y and Z and no armed boot since.
- [x] **Validated user-pointer access — the single kernel/user copy seam `copy_from_user`/`copy_to_user`/`user_range_ok` (CFU-1, 2026-07-15, QEMU-verified; metal-pending).** Every x86 syscall used to open-code the SAME ring-3 window predicate (`end = ptr.wrapping_add(len); end < ptr || ptr < LO || end > USER_BASE + window`) and then read/write the memory RAW (`from_raw_parts` / `copy_nonoverlapping`), leaning on "ring-3 VA == kernel VA in the live CR3" (the identity alias). Correct-by-review, but with **no single enforcement point** — every new syscall (SOCK-2..7 and STOR S2/S3/S8 each) re-open-coded it, so one future mistake would be a kernel read/write of arbitrary memory. CFU-1 mints the seam in `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs`: **`user_range_ok(ptr, len, UserAccess::{Read,Write})`** — the exact unified predicate, overflow-safe, `Read` admitting the whole window (page 0 is a legal RX read source), `Write` requiring the range past the read-only code page (`USER_BASE + PAGE_SIZE`); **`copy_from_user(dst, user_ptr)`** and **`copy_to_user(user_ptr, src)`** — validate-then-copy through the identity window, `#[must_use]` + `Result`-returning so a failed copy cannot be silently ignored. The open-coded sites are converted mechanically: **M1** the socket family (`sys_sendto`/`sys_recvfrom`/`sys_connect`/`sys_send`/`sys_sock_recv` — retiring the SOCK-2..7 ledgered `copy_from_user`-for-socket-buffers residual); **M2** the storage/file family (`sys_write` console, `sys_write_file` incl. the S8 dynamic/S3/S4 branches, `sys_write_grow`, `sys_open`'s name copy, all four `sys_read` write-out branches). The load-bearing **validate-BEFORE-CAS-claim** orderings in `sys_read`/`sys_write_file` are preserved exactly (the seam's `user_range_ok` sits where the inline predicate was, before the offset claim, so an `-EFAULT` still moves no offset and loses no bytes); validate-only sites that hand a borrowed user slice to smolnet (`sys_sendto`/`sys_send`/`sys_connect` headers) or the console (`sys_write`) use `user_range_ok` + the documented in-place borrow. **ZERO behaviour change** — same errnos, same clamps, same partial-write semantics, byte-identical serial on every existing gate (this is a refactor-with-a-seam plus one new witness). **Evidence — the M3 negative witness** (`cfu_efault_witness`, uncounted, drives the REAL dispatcher): three out-of-window `SYS_OPEN` name ranges — a WRAPPING range, a below-`USER_BASE` pointer, an above-window end — each return `-EFAULT` with no side effect (SYS_OPEN claims nothing before its name copy), plus positive controls (a valid in-window range validates `Ok`; the READ bound admits page 0 while the WRITE bound rejects it): `:: CFU: SYS_OPEN wrap/below/above ranges each -EFAULT, no side effect, in-window accepted, write-bound rejects the RO code page — witness OK ::`. Gates all green: `./arroyo check` both arches knob-on+off (zero new warnings, **zero aarch64 files touched** — the aarch64 boundary has its own discipline); knob-ON `UNAOS_IRQSTORAGE=1 UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test 200` 25 PASS 0 FAIL, S8-write intact + the CFU witness; knob-ON `UNAOS_SMOLNET=1 test 90` MISSION + SOCK-1..7 + zeolite + the SOCK-2/3/4 round-trips PASS (the converted socket data paths) + the CFU witness; knob-OFF `test 25` (18 PASS) / `UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test 200` (22 PASS) byte-identical apart from the witness; `test-arm 22` MISSION; NOSTORAGE clean (witness absent, no block device). **NOTE-1 rider (folded 2026-07-16):** the four `sys_write_file`/`sys_write_grow` `wstage_write_at` call sites that still passed a RING-3 `buf` VA now route through a `copy_from_user`-backed `wstage_write_from_user` (each already validated/copied that range, so the seam re-check cannot fail for a well-formed caller — byte-behavior unchanged, `-EFAULT` fail-closed for a malformed one); the plain `wstage_write_at` stays only for its one KERNEL-pointer caller (the `u11m2` in-kernel pattern seed), so no raw user-pointer read survives outside the CFU-1 seam. **The x86 SMAP follow-on is NAMED, NOT this arc** — the copy still goes through the identity-mapped window exactly as before; a SMAP-capable port must wrap the seam's `copy_nonoverlapping` in `stac`/`clac` (the 2012 rMBP is pre-Broadwell — no SMAP — so this is codeable-but-untestable on the target and deferred). **✅ METAL-CONFIRMED (Boots Y and Z, 2026-08-06, capture `rmbp-gr16-s73`).** The M3 negative witness prints `:: CFU: SYS_OPEN wrap/below/above ranges each -EFAULT … witness OK ::` on both metal boots — the three out-of-window arms and the two positive controls behave on silicon exactly as they do under TCG. The named SMAP follow-on is untouched and still untestable on this pre-Broadwell part.
- [x] **Live-leaf validation of kernel→user writes — CFU-1's one-page write bound was a reachable W^X bypass (CFU-2, 2026-08-06, QEMU-verified + falsification-proven; ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED on the real 2012 rMBP — Boot Z, 2026-08-06, media built at `3aa2b7a4`).** CFU-1 (above) admitted any write destination at or past `USER_BASE + PAGE_SIZE`, a bound inherited from the FLAT/U2 layout in which page 0 was the only code page. **Under the U3 ELF loader that is false, and the counterexamples ship on the boot media:** `protect_user_slot_range` clears `PTE_WRITABLE` across every page a non-`PF_W` segment covers, so `VUG-X86.ELF` (`R E`, memsz `0x1fe5`) and `PULSE-X86.ELF` (`R E`, memsz `0x13ec`) each leave **page 1 read-only *and* executable** — precisely the first address the validator admitted. A ring-3 `sys_read`/`sys_recvfrom` with `buf = USER_BASE + 0x1000` therefore made the kernel's own `copy_to_user` write into an executable ring-3 page: **a code-injection primitive with the kernel as the writer**, on unprivileged programs already present, with SMAP deliberately off on this silicon. It was silent only because `CR0.WP` is clear — and the WXN M3a milestone that arms WP would have converted it into a **fatal CPL-0 `#PF`, i.e. a ring-3-triggerable machine halt**, which is why this landed before M3a rather than after. **The fix consults the tables instead of guessing:** the cheap bounds test stays as a pre-filter, but the decision comes from `user_range_leaf_ok` — a per-4 KiB-page walk of the *live* leaves across the **whole** range, built by threading the existing `translate` walk through `wx_fold`/`WX_TOP`, the same pure functions WXAUDIT and `wx_check_slot` are built on, so there is one walker and one permission model rather than a second hand-rolled one. Writes require the folded `W`; both directions require present + folded `U`; `-EFAULT` is unchanged, so well-formed callers are byte-identical. Because the gate lives **inside `user_range_ok`** it covers every caller, not just `copy_to_user` — `sys_futex`'s `uaddr`, `sys_thread_spawn`'s stack word, and the up-front full-range validations in `sys_recvfrom`/`sys_sock_recv`/`sys_read_file`. **Evidence — and it is proven able to fail, which is the point:** the witness reshapes a scratch slot through the *real* loader primitive (not a hand-written PTE) into the U3 shape, then drives five arms through the *real* validator under that slot's own CR3: `:: CFU2-WGATE: kernel→user write validated against LIVE leaf W — RO+X page (U3 ELF shape, the VUG/PULSE bypass) REFUSED -EFAULT, RW page ACCEPTED, RW→RO straddle REFUSED while its in-page head is accepted, RO page still readable -> PASS ::`. The straddle pair is the one a first-page-only check cannot satisfy: same pointer, same direction, verdict decided by a page the pointer is not in. **Control run with the single gate line neutered prints `-> FAIL … ro_refused=false` and `arroyo test` exits RC=1** — the defect reproduced on demand, and the instrument shown non-vacuous. `./arroyo check` RC=0 both arches with a byte-identical warning multiset; `test 60` RC=0, 27 → 28 PASS, one line added and nothing else substantive. **✅ METAL-CONFIRMED (Boot Z, 2026-08-06, capture `rmbp-gr16-s73`, media built at trunk `3aa2b7a4`).** All five arms came back correct on real silicon on the first flight, arm for arm identical to the QEMU reading:

    ```
    :: CFU2-WGATE: kernel->user write validated against LIVE leaf W — RO+X page (U3 ELF shape, the VUG/PULSE bypass) REFUSED -EFAULT, RW page ACCEPTED, RW->RO straddle REFUSED while its in-page head is accepted, RO page still readable -> PASS ::
    :: CFU: SYS_OPEN wrap/below/above ranges each -EFAULT, no side effect, in-window accepted, write-bound rejects the RO code page — witness OK ::
    ```

    Both witnesses print on the same boot, so the CFU-1 pre-filter and the CFU-2 live-leaf walk are each demonstrably live on metal rather than one standing in for the other. **The cost arm is the one that had to be read on silicon and not assumed:** the gate adds a per-4 KiB-page walk of the live tables to every validated range, on hardware where a page walk is real memory traffic rather than TCG bookkeeping — and `BPACE: total gui=2217ms` is unchanged from Boot Y to the millisecond, with `ehci-hid-done d=1285` and `sched d=67` likewise unmoved. No measurable boot-time cost. Boot-pace detail: `docs/dev/OS/01_BOOT_HAL/bootpace.md` §10j.
- [x] **Kernel-half W^X, milestone 3a — `CR0.WP` armed on every core (WXN-x86 M3a, 2026-08-07, ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED on the real 2012 rMBP — Boot AA, 2026-08-07, media built at `37fc0f3c`).** All predictions held arm-for-arm on the first flight: `WXAUDIT-NXE: cores=8 nxe=8 nxe_mask=0xFF wp=8 wp_mask=0xFF -> PASS` at 171 ms; `cr0=0x0000000080010013` — Boot Z's value plus bit 16 and nothing else; no new serial line; zero pace cost (`gui=2217` for the third consecutive boot); `CFU2-WGATE` five arms unchanged; mbench 28/28, analyzer `--wxn` exit 0. Boot-pace detail: `docs/dev/OS/01_BOOT_HAL/bootpace.md` §10k. One RMW in `syscall::init()` — the per-core control-register seat that already arms `EFER.SCE|NXE` and `CR4.SMEP`, run exactly once per core on both bring-up paths — sets `CR0.WP`, and `smp.rs`'s `WXAUDIT-NXE` verdict widens to require `wp == cores` alongside `nxe == cores` (token stays the bare `PASS`; the analyzer matches it exactly, and the analyzer itself decides the M3a era from the capture, so a short post-M3a mask is a `WXN-WP-SHORT` finding, not an excused note). The two hunks are one commit by necessity: either alone reds every boot. **The draft's justifying comment was rewritten before commit rather than installed:** it claimed the ring-3 code page is "excluded from every `copy_to_user` by construction (`lo = USER_BASE + PAGE_SIZE`)" — the exact false model that hid CFU-2 above; the committed text names the truth (the bound is a fast reject; the exclusion is CFU-2's per-page live-leaf walk, witnessed `CFU2-WGATE … -> PASS` on Boot Z). Inert until M3b creates the first W=0 kernel leaf: every `PTE_WRITABLE`-omitting path in `memory.rs` is ring-3-facing, and all five `with_page_tables_writable` sites read live CR0 and toggle WP conditionally. Prediction on the wire (proven by nothing yet): metal `wp=8 wp_mask=0xFF -> PASS`, `cr0=0x0000000080010013` — Boot Z's value plus bit 16 and nothing else, no new serial line; QEMU 6-core `wp=6 wp_mask=0x3F`. Gate: `./arroyo check` RC=0, all 11 covering-array legs.
- [x] **Kernel-half W^X, milestone 3b — `W` cleared from the kernel's executable pages, behind a build arm (WXN-x86 M3b, 2026-08-07, QEMU-verified both arms; ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED on the real 2012 rMBP — Boot AI, 2026-08-07, media built at trunk `566d6c04`, `kernel.elf` sha256 `0a69cde6…`).** M3a armed `CR0.WP` so a leaf's `W` bit binds a CPL-0 store; M3b is the leaf edit that gives it something to bind. `memory::wxn_ro_stage` runs as a third stage of `wxn_pdpt_sweep`, after M2's splitter (it needs the 4 KiB granularity M2 creates) and before `smp::start_aps` (so no AP can have cached a writable translation — no cross-core shootdown needed). It walks the executable extent M2 already derives, **minus the page-rounded-OUT union of the image's writable `PT_LOAD`s**, and clears `PTE_WRITABLE` on each 4 KiB leaf. The extent is rounded **IN** for `W` where M2 rounds **OUT** for `X`: for execution the fail-safe direction is "keep it executable", for writes it is the opposite — a page any `PF_W` segment touches, even by one byte, must keep `W` or a legitimate store to `.data.rel.ro`/`.got` becomes a `#PF`. That subtraction is done at runtime and counted as `wskip=`; a non-zero `wskip` is a **W^X hole** (a page in the exec extent keeping both `W` and `X`) and a finding to chase, not a benign count. **`kern_WX` floors at 1, not 0, and the residue is the AP trampoline page at `0x8000`** — `start_aps` writes it wholesale once and then once per AP into a shared handoff slot, all strictly after `arch::init`, so an RX `0x8000` under WP=1 is a fatal CPL-0 `#PF` before the first AP starts. Clearing it needs a lifecycle after the last AP reports online and is named **M3c**, not attempted here. **The arm.** The WRITE is behind `feature = "wxnro"` (`UNAOS_WXNRO=1`, wired in **both** `arroyo` and `builder/src/main.rs` — the x86 ESP carries the builder's kernel, so a knob wired in only one place produces media that honestly reads `armed=0` on a run the operator armed); the WALK is not. Disarmed, the stage takes the identical census, writes nothing, and does not even open a `CR0.WP` window or pay a second CR3 reload. `cfg!(feature = ...)`, not `#[cfg]`, so the store type-checks in every leg; the `x86-all` matrix leg carries `wxnro` so the **armed** polarity is type-checked too. **Census first, then write** — the read-only pre-pass prints `:: WXN-M3B-PRE: … -> CENSUS ::` *before* the write window opens, so one armed boot is self-predicting and a death anywhere in the window still carries its census. This is deliberate in place of a separate dry-run flight: **no disarmed boot can predict the brick mode**, because the only condition that bricks — an unknown runtime writer inside the extent — writes nothing during a dry run and is invisible to it by construction. A dry run reduces *census* risk, not *brick* risk. **It can say no.** The pre-pass is what makes refusal possible while the map is still untouched: M3b writes only if the extent is exactly what it models (every page a present 4 KiB leaf, every non-`wskip` page writable). A huge leaf, an absent page or an `already_ro` page ⇒ `-> REFUSED`, census on the wire, **map unmodified**. And because that guarantees `already_ro == 0`, the cleared population is exactly the `would` population — which is what makes the **rollback** exact: if the post-write verify (`verify_w != expect_w`, a second independent count taken through the *fold* via `live_leaf`, not the leaf bit) or `cleared != would` does not close, M3b restores `W` on exactly the pages it cleared and refuses. Never half-applied. The GR15 belt is an assert **and** a wire field (`fb=`/`fb_delta=`/`fb_chk=`): without `fb_chk` a silently-skipped interlock — no panel, or `WRITER` contended — is indistinguishable from a pass, the instrument-that-cannot-fire class this track has paid for repeatedly. Note `with_page_tables_writable` now has **six** call sites, not the five M3a's entry names: `memory.rs`'s M1, M2, M3b, `set_framebuffer_wc` and `map_mmio_window`, plus `syscall.rs`'s user-page path. The two M3a's count omitted are exactly the GR15 framebuffer/MMIO retype paths; all six read live `CR0` and toggle `WP` conditionally, and neither GR15 path sets `PTE_WRITABLE` on an existing leaf. **Falsifiers are intra-boot, never cross-boot** — `cfg!` folds to a constant, so the two arms are different binaries: this commit's own QEMU pair links to `xpages=285` disarmed and `286` armed, a one-page difference on a healthy pair that would have failed a cross-boot check. The sound closures: `would + already_ro + wskip + absent + huge_pages == xpages`; `cleared == would` (armed); `verify_w == expect_w`; and `kern_WX == keep_x − already_ro − cleared`, which **replaces `kern_WX == keep_x` from M3b onward** (disarmed it degenerates to the old identity, so a disarmed boot is a true no-op on the analyzer as well as on the map). **QEMU, both arms, RC=0, 48 PASS each, zero faults:** armed `cleared=286 would=286 wskip=0 verify_w=0 expect_w=0 fb_delta=0x0 fb_chk=1 tramp_w=1 -> RO`, `kern_WX 287 → 1 (0 MiB)`, `WXPROBE map: at=ktext … w=0 fw=0 fx=1` and the same for `at=kimg`, `at=ap8000` unchanged at `w=1 fx=1`, `at=fb`/`bss`/`lapic` unchanged; disarmed `cleared=0 would=285 verify_w=285 expect_w=285 -> DRYRUN`, `kern_WX=286`, `at=ktext w=1 fw=1`. **What QEMU does not cover, stated plainly:** a different link (286 pages vs metal's 316) with Kepler/iGPU/SMC/EHCI-HID/gmux inert under TCG — so its clean boot proves nothing about *writers* living in those drivers, which is the entire residual risk; 6 cores vs 8 (`wp_mask=0x3F` vs `0xFF`); and a QEMU panel rather than the WC-typed metal fb reached through the Kepler takeover. **What is not proven by any of this:** `verify_w=0` and `w=0` are page-table READBACKS — they prove the bits, not the hardware honouring them. The only true falsifier is a CPL-0 store into the extent expected to fault and recovered from, a ring-0 twin of `u1b-code-write`, for which this kernel has no `#PF`-resume path; named **M3d**, not claimed. Metal prediction, derived from the armed ESP link at this commit with the bench feature set (`readelf -lW`: LOAD 02 `R E` `0x36120`+`0x1058ef` → end `0x13BA0F` → `x_hi = 0x13C000`): `xpages=316`, `keep_x=317`, `cleared=316`, `would=316`, `wskip=0`, `verify_w=0`, `expect_w=0`, `tramp_w=1`, `kern_WX 317 → 1 (0 MiB)`, identity `317 − 0 − 316 = 1`. Gate: `./arroyo check` RC=0, all 11 covering-array legs. **Analyzer owed (not touched here, deliberately):** `tools/serial-analyzer.py`'s rise-WARN discriminator is built on `keep_x == xpages + 1 == kern_WX`; post-M3b `kern_WX` stops tracking the image size and becomes the residue count, so on an armed capture the analyzer prints a factually wrong explanation ("an executable leaf the firmware left READ-ONLY") and — because `<` is its "explicable" direction — a **half-cleared extent would produce the same note and no warning**. The era split, plus a WARN rule for `wskip > 0`, must land before an armed metal flight is readable by the bench's own instrument. **✅ METAL-CONFIRMED (Boot AI, 2026-08-07, capture `rmbp-gr16-s73`, slice `~/unaos-bench/scratch/gr21/bootAI-slice-clean.log`, trunk `566d6c04`, `gui=2538`, `hz=2693860340`; boot-pace detail: `docs/dev/OS/01_BOOT_HAL/bootpace.md` §10s).** The flight was kept **dedicated** — Boot AH's knob set plus `UNAOS_WXNRO=1`, no `gmux_igd`, no `bcmarecon`/`bcmaS1` — so nothing the map edit broke could be blamed on a second passenger. **The metal prediction above is stale and was recomputed rather than copied, which is the methodological point:** its `xpages=316 / keep_x=317` were derived from the armed link at `ee0c5628` with a different feature set; `scratch/gr21/m3b-prediction.md`, written before the flight, re-derived **N = 337** from the armed `esp-x86` link at this trunk by `readelf -lW` over the four `PT_LOAD`s, and verified that the mid-measurement trunk move `0962f271 → 76df0c82` left all four `p_vaddr`/`p_memsz` values byte-identical (the igpu changes sit behind `gmux_igd`, off in this set). A prediction carried forward would have missed by 21 pages with no way to distinguish that miss from a coverage failure. **Every field of the recomputed prediction landed exactly, on the first flight:** `WXN-M2 … xpages=337 keep_x=338 nx_4k=1198 -> SPLIT`; `WXN-M3B-PRE … armed=1 would=337 already_ro=0 wskip=0 absent=0 huge_leaves=0 huge_pages=0 pre_w=337 would_w=337 -> CENSUS`; `WXN-M3B … armed=1 cleared=337 would=337 verify_w=0 expect_w=0 fb_delta=0x0 fb_chk=1 tramp_w=1 -> RO`; `WXAUDIT x86: … kern_WX=1 (0 MiB) … l3=1536`. **The identity closes: `kern_WX = keep_x − already_ro − cleared = 338 − 0 − 337 = 1`**, and Boot AH's `kern_WX=337` — every page of the kernel's own text, writable by construction — becomes **one**, the AP trampoline at `0x8000` whose lifecycle is M3c. Readback on the wire: `WXPROBE map: at=ktext … w=0 fw=0 fx=1` and the same at `at=kimg`; `at=ap8000` **unchanged** at `w=1`; `at=fb` still WC (`lvl=2M pat=1 w=1`), the GR15 retype intact across the write window and corroborated by the belt that *ran* (`fb_chk=1`). **The instrument was proven able to convict before the boot, which is what makes the green meaningful:** the analyzer debt named immediately above was paid by `02df0530`, and its four fixtures re-run against the trunk analyzer ahead of the flight behave — `synth-m3b-healthy` **0**, `synth-m3b-disarmed` **0**, `synth-m3b-short` (`cleared=315 would=316`) **5**, `synth-m3b-wskip` **5**. Gates re-run by the recording seat against this slice: mbench **33/33 required, 0 forbidden**, 1791 lines; `serial-analyzer --wxn` **exit 0**; `--wxprobe` **exit 0**. **Zero measured boot cost:** `gui` 2532 → 2538, inside the 2532–2542 band Boots AG/AH already occupy without a W-clear aboard, and the +6 is entirely `sdhc` 320 → 326 on 399 ACMD41 polls against AH's 389 — the same card-power-up variance that moved AG → AH by −5 the other way. `wxn_ro_stage` runs before `smp::start_aps` and before the TSC has a timebase (its lines carry the `[      ?ms]` prefix), so its true price is a 337-leaf walk and one CR3 reload inside a window the ledger does not stamp. **Unaffected, and this is the load-bearing negative:** `WINX-1/2/3/7/8` and `PULSE-W` all PASS, and `CFU2-WGATE` attests all five arms a third consecutive boot — every one of those runs through `with_page_tables_writable`, whose six call sites opened a `CR0.WP` window against genuinely `W=0` leaves for the first time. **Still open and not claimed:** `verify_w=0` and `w=0` are page-table *readbacks* — they prove the bits, not that the silicon honours them; the only true falsifier is a faulting-and-recovered CPL-0 store into the extent (**M3d**), for which this kernel has no `#PF`-resume path. **What this closes:** with M3a (Boot AA) the kernel half now enforces NX *and* RO on real hardware — the arc GR19 reopened when an adversarial review of M3 found the live ring-3 code-injection bypass recorded as CFU-2 above.

- [ ] **aarch64 twin of CFU-2 — the same bypass, reachable today, and it needs no WP milestone to arm.** Reported by the CFU-2 work, deliberately **not touched** (STOP tripwire: out of the x86 lane). `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs:6484`'s `user_range_ok` computes `lo = base + USER_CODE_SIZE` for writable ranges, and `USER_CODE_SIZE` is `0x1000` (`arch/aarch64/boot.rs:55`) — the identical one-page assumption. But `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs:6975` applies permissions **per segment** (`protect_user_slot_code_range(slot, dst_off, s.memsz)`), so any aarch64 ELF whose `R+X` LOAD exceeds one page gets the same RO+X page 1, and `copy_to_user` (`:6514`) will aim an EL1 store at an `AP=0b11` page — which that function's own doc comment says permission-faults into the kernel-fault path that **halts the core**. So on aarch64 the denial-of-service arm is live *now*, with no CR0.WP analogue required. **The fix has a ready-made analogue there:** `arch/aarch64/boot.rs:809`'s `slot_page_is_data(s, va)` already reads the live leaf's `AP`/`UXN` bits and is exactly the predicate `user_range_ok` should consult, the same way x86 now consults `user_range_leaf_ok`. Belongs to the pi/jetson briefs.
- [ ] SMAP-toggled user-pointer access (`stac`/`clac` around the CFU-1 seam) — the named CFU-1 follow-on; pre-Broadwell rMBP has no SMAP, so this needs a SMAP-capable x86 target to test
- [ ] CR4.UMIP — **newly ledgered (WXAUDIT audit pass, 2026-08-06); previously absent from this document entirely.** `syscall::init` sets SMEP and deliberately not SMAP, and has never considered UMIP: ring 3 can therefore execute `SGDT`/`SIDT`/`SLDT`/`SMSW`/`STR` and read back the GDT/IDT/TSS descriptor-table bases. Ranked LOW and left open on purpose, not overlooked: the leak is an information leak of kernel addresses, and UnaOS has no KASLR for it to defeat (the identity map puts the kernel at a fixed, known VA), so it discloses nothing an attacker could not already compute. UMIP is also Cannon Lake+, so the Ivy Bridge bench target cannot test it — this is a `CPUID.7:ECX.UMIP`-gated one-liner alongside the existing SMEP gate whenever a capable x86 target exists, and it belongs to the same arc as the SMAP item above.
- [x] **Per-process address spaces (U3, 2026-07-04, QEMU-verified; metal-pending) — the x86 mirror of aarch64 M6d.** Each user process now runs in its OWN top-level page table (its own CR3): a static 8-slot pool (`memory.rs` `SLOT_PML4/PDPT/PD/PT` + backing + `SLOT_USED`), each slot SHARING the kernel half — `build_slot` copies every live kernel PML4 entry EXCEPT PML4[2], so the kernel mappings (identity map incl. high MMIO like the xHCI BAR at 768 GiB, heap, kernel code/stacks, IST/percpu/GDT) are shared by pointing at the SAME kernel next-level tables — while owning a PRIVATE PML4[2] user window at `USER_BASE`. `user_task_trampoline` installs the task's CR3 (`Task.user_cr3`) before the `iretq`; `exit` restores the kernel CR3 then frees the slot. Two processes can map the same VA to different frames. **Evidence:** a deterministic kernel isolation probe (two slots, same VA, distinct sentinels, swap CR3 and read — `:: U3: per-process CR3 isolation … distinct -> PASS ::`) plus two ring-3 tasks each in their OWN CR3 each reading their slot-private sentinel (`:: U3: 2 ring-3 tasks each in a private CR3 read their own sentinel … -> PASS ::`); U1a/U1b/U2/U2.5 byte-identical (the `user_cr3 == 0` shared-window path is untouched). **Scope is honest:** plain `mov cr3` (full non-global-TLB flush) — **no PCID** (the x86 analogue of M6d's ASID) yet; a **static 8-slot pool** (hard cap = STOP tripwire) — no dynamic user-memory allocator yet; **per-slot table freeze** (a kernel PML4-*entry* added post-boot would need mirroring into live slots — the kernel never adds one; edits BELOW the PML4 propagate via the shared tables); ring 3 still **cooperative** (the CR3 switch lives in the trampoline/`exit`, not a preemptive dispatch path — that moves when preemptible ring 3 lands). The probe's supervisor read of a user page relies on **no SMAP** (correct on the Ivy Bridge target; a SMAP-capable port must use an AC-toggle or validated copy). **Metal-pending** (rides the next reflash / FTDI cable day).
- [x] **Preemptible ring 3 (U3.5, x86 twin of aarch64 M6e; 2026-07-04, QEMU-verified + ★ METAL-CONFIRMED real 2012 rMBP).** A ring-3 task can now be dropped **preemptible** (`Task.preemptible` → `user_task_trampoline` sets `RFLAGS.IF` in the `iretq` frame), so the local-APIC timer evicts it and other work shares its core — closing the one-core DoS a never-syscalling program (`jmp $`) was. The timer **and reschedule-IPI** ISRs — the two maskable handlers that touch per-CPU GS — conditionally `swapgs` on a CPL-3 entry (like the U1b fault handler) so `this_cpu()`/the scheduler resolve the kernel per-CPU block, and swap back before `iretq` (the xHCI/NIC MSI handlers and `eoi()` touch no GS, so they need no swap); the per-process **CR3 install moved from the trampoline into the scheduler DISPATCH path** (`run()`), so it now covers a resumed-after-preemption task (which never re-enters the trampoline) as well as first entry. The full user register file is preserved across preempt/resume by the existing `x86-interrupt` save + `switch_context` machinery (the same that already preempts kernel tasks). **Evidence:** `:: U3.5: ring-3 preemption — IRQs-at-ring3=<n>, co-task ran, spinner resumed -> PASS ::` — a preemptible spinner (increments a private-CR3 counter, never syscalls) is preempted (`IRQs-at-ring3 > 0` — cooperative ring 3 can never show this), a kernel co-task on the SAME core runs to completion (the DoS fix), the spinner's counter keeps climbing across preemptions (correct resume through the CR3-at-dispatch path), and a watchdog reaps it via a scheduler `KillSwitch`; U1a/U1b/U2/U2.5/U3 byte-identical (they stay `preemptible=false` — IF clear, cooperative FIFO, never preempted). **Scope is honest:** a **per-task opt-in** (only the U3.5 spinner is preemptible); **FP/SIMD is NOT saved across preemption** (no FXSAVE/FXRSTOR context switch — see the FP item below); only **one user task per core** at a time (TSS.RSP0/`syscall_kernel_rsp` are set at first entry only — a second concurrent ring-3 task per core would need RSP0-at-dispatch, an M7-twin arc); plain `mov cr3` (no PCID). **★ METAL-CONFIRMED on the real 2012 rMBP (2026-07-04, bootlog photo):** `:: SMEP on ::` (real supervisor-execute protection ACTIVE while the preemptible spinner ran — the metal-only win TCG can't show) then `:: U3.5: ring-3 preemption — IRQs-at-ring3=156, co-task ran, spinner resumed -> PASS ::`; the real timer preempted the ring-3 spinner 156× and the swapgs-on-ring3-timer + CR3-at-dispatch + reap all ran correctly on Ivy Bridge, with every prior fixture PASS (U1a/U1b/U2-0a/U3, byte-consistent) and 0 unexpected faults. The same boot also **metal-confirmed the U2.5 APIC ms-clock fix** (`initcnt=6236 [1 kHz calibrated]`, `ms-clock 999 Hz` — the old ~119 Hz bug gone). (Item surfaced by the U2 integrator review, 2026-07-02; closed by U3.5.)
- [x] **Process model — `sys_spawn`/`sys_wait` + per-process handle table (U4x, x86 twin of aarch64 M7/U4; 2026-07-05, QEMU-verified; metal-pending).** The x86 process abstraction U3/U3.5 began now closes: a parent loads a child program into its OWN address space, runs it ring-3, and reaps it by an owner-scoped HANDLE. **Part 0 (the enabler):** the `TSS.RSP0` + per-CPU `syscall_kernel_rsp` install MOVED from `user_task_trampoline` into the scheduler DISPATCH path (`run()`), beside U3.5's CR3-at-dispatch — so a task RESUMED after a block/preempt (which never re-enters the trampoline) gets ITS OWN kernel stack installed. This closes a use-after-free a second concurrent user task per core would otherwise hit: task B's syscall/fault would land on task A's kernel stack (installed at A's first entry and never updated on B's resume) — and if A had exited and its `Box` kernel stack been freed, on a freed/reused stack. Concretely: parent blocks in `sys_wait`, child A (first entry) sets `syscall_kernel_rsp = A_kstack`, A exits and frees it, child B runs+exits, the parent RESUMES (no trampoline) and its next SYSCALL does `mov rsp, gs:[syscall_kernel_rsp]` — which is `parent_kstack` only because dispatch re-installs it. This CLOSES U3.5's "one user task per core" scope note. **Part A:** `SYS_SPAWN=8` returns a small HANDLE index into the caller's per-process handle table (`HANDLES`, keyed by the caller's address-space SLOT — the x86 stand-in for aarch64's ASID, read from the live CR3 via `current_slot`); `SYS_WAIT=9` blocks on the child's done-semaphore, returns its exit status, and CONSUMES the handle (a second wait ⇒ `-ECHILD`). `PROCS` (pid-keyed control blocks: `status`/`state`/`done`) and `HANDLES` (slot-keyed ownership namespace) are separate, static, const-init — no heap — mirroring the reviewed aarch64 U4. Reaping is structurally owner-scoped: a task can only wait a handle in its OWN slot's row. **Part B:** a parent spawns TWO children and reaps both by distinct handles; a separate orphan's `sys_wait(0)` returns `-ECHILD` (same index 0, different slot row — proving the tables are per-process). **Evidence:** `:: U4x: x86 process model — parent reaped 2 children by handle, non-child sys_wait -ECHILD -> PASS ::` under `UNAOS_FATIMG=1` / `test-fat part` / `test-fat sf`; U1a/U1b/U2/U2.5/U3/U3.5 **byte-identical** (proven by a pre-change baseline diff — the RSP0-at-dispatch move does not disturb the cooperative single-user-task path); `./arroyo check` both arches, **zero aarch64 files touched**. **Load path — an honest x86 divergence from the aarch64 twin:** aarch64's `sys_spawn` reads `HELLO.BIN` off the SD card INSIDE the SVC handler because its EMMC2 driver is PIO (no interrupts). x86 storage is USB-mass-storage over xHCI, whose BOT read pump `hlt()`s to await the async completion — and `hlt` with IF=0 hangs forever, while the SYSCALL handler runs IF-masked (SFMASK clears IF). So the FAT read is hoisted OUT of the syscall: `HELLO.BIN` is pre-staged off FAT ONCE by `stage_hello` on the BSP main loop (IF=1 — the proven U2 read path), and `sys_spawn` instantiates each child by a pure memcpy of the staged bytes into a fresh slot (IF=0-safe). The child still runs storage-loaded bytes gated on storage being present — the observable behavior is the aarch64 twin's. The untrusted-loader posture is unchanged (size-bounded to one code page; contained by ring-3 + NX + W^X + SMEP + the U1b fault-kill net; no signature gate — the shared code-signing item). **Scope is honest:** the fixtures are **register-only** and **cooperative** (IF clear — never preempted in ring 3); a `MAX_PROCS = 4` cap and the static 8-slot pool (STOP tripwires); no PCID; no `copy_from_user` (program-by-name `sys_spawn` with a validated user name is a later arc — a STOP tripwire here); handle rows are NOT cleared on slot teardown (harmless today — reapers consume their handles and the demo holds distinct slots — but a lifecycle concern once processes churn slots: that + the capability CHECK + grant/attenuate/revoke is U5, exactly the aarch64 U4→U5 boundary). Two independent adversarial reviews (an 8-lens sweep + a 3-lens deep pass on the RSP0-at-dispatch use-after-free, the spawn/wait concurrency+leak+hang surface, and the blob's cross-syscall register ABI) each returned **0 findings**. **Metal-pending** (rides the next reflash / FTDI cable day).
- [x] **Handles as capabilities — the enforcement CHECK + grant/attenuate/revoke + routed `sys_write` + teardown-clear (U5x, x86 twin of aarch64 U5; 2026-07-06, QEMU-verified; no metal this arc — pure syscall logic, and the child loads ride U2/U4x's already-metal-confirmed FAT path).** Turns U4x's handle STRUCTURE into a real capability, mirroring the reviewed aarch64 U5 with one substitution: aarch64 keys the tables by ASID; x86 keys by the address-space **SLOT** index (`memory::current_slot()`, read from the live CR3). A handle now carries **rights** (a `CAP_READ|CAP_WRITE|CAP_EXEC|CAP_GRANT|CAP_REVOKE` bitmask, held in a **sidecar** `HANDLE_RIGHTS[[AtomicU32; 8]; USER_SLOTS+1]` keyed identically to `HANDLES`, so U4x's `0`/`RESERVING` value-word sentinel logic is byte-unperturbed) and names a **target** beyond "child pid" (a well-known `HANDLE_CONSOLE = u64::MAX-1` token — kept to two kinds, `Child(pid)` and `Console`, NOT a general object table, which is U6). **The CHECK** is a single `handle_resolve(row, idx, req_rights)` at the one lookup point every handle-consuming path goes through: out-of-range/Empty/`RESERVING` ⇒ the caller's own errno (`sys_wait` → `-ECHILD`, preserving U4x structural ownership; `sys_write`/`SYS_CAP` → `-EACCES`), present-but-missing-a-right ⇒ `-EACCES`. **grant/attenuate/revoke** are first-class via `SYS_CAP = 10` (sub-op selector): GRANT mints a new handle to the same target carrying a rights mask that must be a **subset** of the granter's rights on the source (the **monotonic-decrease / attenuation invariant** — `req & !src_rights != 0` ⇒ `-EACCES`; a grant can never amplify), requiring `CAP_GRANT` on the source; REVOKE drops a handle the caller owns (`-EACCES`/`-ECHILD` on subsequent use). **`sys_write` now routes through the table** — `fd` is a handle index that must resolve to a `Console` handle with `CAP_WRITE` (subsuming U1a's `fd==1 → -EBADF`); there is no ambient stdout. Every printing process is **endowed** a `Console`+`CAP_WRITE` cap at the conventional index `CONSOLE_FD = 1`: the **shared kernel window** (U1a/U1b/U2, which run with `user_cr3 == 0` so `current_slot()` is `None`) is given a dedicated `SHARED_ROW` (index `USER_SLOTS` — the x86 twin of aarch64 ASID 0, since x86 has no ASID-0 row) endowed in `setup()`, and each spawned child in `sys_spawn`; the existing `USER_BASE` range-check + all-or-nothing `-EFAULT` semantics are byte-identical, so a hostile pointer still yields `-EFAULT`, not `-EACCES`. **Teardown-clear** (folds U4x's one deferred lifecycle note): `memory::free_user_space_by_cr3` now wipes the whole `HANDLES[slot]` row + its rights **before** releasing the slot's used-flag (not after — a post-release clear could race a concurrent `alloc_user_space` on another core that reclaims the same slot), and because BOTH teardown paths (normal `exit` and the KillSwitch reap of a never-exiting preemptible task) funnel through that one function, the clear rides both. Evidence — the inline `u5x-cap` fixture proves the four ring-3-observable behaviours against its own slot's table (conveying a 4-bit witness bitmask as its `sys_exit` status, routed by task **name** — x86 needs no `SYS_REPORT`) and the launcher proves the fifth kernel-side: `:: U5x: x86 capabilities — write-cap OK, no-cap -EACCES, attenuated grant bounded, revoke enforced, teardown-clear clean -> PASS ::` under `UNAOS_FATIMG=1` / `test-fat part` / `test-fat sf`, with every U1a/U1b/U2/U2.5/U3/U3.5/U4x verdict byte-identical (all prior `hello from ring 3`/`hello from disk` prints land — their processes hold the endowed console cap); `./arroyo check` both arches, **zero aarch64 files touched**. Still deferred to **U6** (the x86 twin of pi4 U6): bandy handle-transfer between principals, a general object table (routing fs/net syscalls through handles, first-free `Console`), and cross-process revocation trees (`CAP_REVOKE` defined but reserved); PCID and `copy_from_user`/`copy_to_user` (the M6f twin) remain separately deferred. **Metal-pending** (pure syscall logic; the child loads ride U2's metal-confirmed FAT path).
- [x] **General object table — `(kind, target, rights)` descriptors, first-free for ALL kinds, the `CONSOLE_FD` collision closed (U6x, x86 twin of aarch64 U6a; 2026-07-06, QEMU-verified; no metal this arc — pure syscall logic, and the demo's children ride U2/U4x's already-metal-confirmed FAT load path).** Generalizes U5x's fixed-shape handle into a general object descriptor, mirroring the reviewed aarch64 U6a with the one x86 substitution (the tables are keyed by the address-space **SLOT**/`row`, not the ASID). **The kind rides in a parallel sidecar** `HANDLE_KIND[[AtomicU8; 8]; USER_SLOTS+1]` (keyed identically to `HANDLES`/`HANDLE_RIGHTS`), so the value word keeps U4x/U5x's sentinels **byte-identical** — `0` = Empty (the lock-free allocator's free marker), `u64::MAX` = `RESERVING` (an in-flight claim) — and nothing else is reserved. A handle now names one of four kinds — `Child(pid)` (U4x), `Console` (U5x), and the **scaffolds** `File(id)` / `Socket(id)` (defined + resolvable via `handle_resolve`, though no fs/net syscall routes through them yet — they prove the table is genuinely general). The sidecar choice is deliberate: it makes the STOP-tripwire sentinel-collision reasoning *structurally impossible* (a `File`/`Socket` id may be any non-`0`/non-`u64::MAX` word with no high-bit masking), and mirrors the rights sidecar 1:1 (same publish-kind-and-rights-before-the-live-value / observe-after discipline; single-writer-per-slot is the backstop). **The `CONSOLE_FD` collision is closed** — the raison d'être of the arc. U5x pinned the console cap at a fixed index `CONSOLE_FD = 1` via an unconditional store while `handle_install`'s first-free scan allocated children from index 0, so a process that both PRINTED and SPAWNED 2+ children could have a child auto-allocated onto index 1 and then clobbered by the console install (harmless only because no such process existed on x86). U6x makes `CONSOLE_FD` a **reserved index the first-free allocator SKIPS**: the console lives there by the `fd=1`/stdout convention (keeping every prior blob's `sys_write(fd=1)` byte-identical), and auto-allocated child/object handles fill `{0, 2, 3, ..}` — so a console cap and N child/object caps coexist with **zero index collision, for any interleaving of installs**. Every existing consumer is behaviour-preserved: `handle_resolve` dispatches on the kind sidecar (`sys_wait` requires `Child`, `sys_write` requires `Console`+`CAP_WRITE`, `sys_cap` grant/revoke on any kind); the **attenuation invariant is unchanged** and the mint now copies the source handle's kind (a grant attenuates rights, never re-kinds the object) and publishes the value LAST; `handle_clear`/`clear_handle_row`/`handle_row_is_clear` (the U5x teardown-clear) also clear/verify the kind.
