So the write STAYS staged in the handler (a pure memcpy into `wstage`) and is FLUSHED to disk OUT of the handler, at IF=1, over the shell-proven `fat::write_at`→`block::write_block` path — which on x86 is USB-over-xHCI, and (the load-bearing new fact) the BOT pump is **self-servicing and CPU-agnostic**: it drains its own DMA'd event ring and acks the interrupter via RAW MMIO (IMAN/ERDP/EINT), and its `hlt` is woken by the per-CPU timer tick, so it drives from ANY core, not only the BSP that owns the xHCI MSI. **THE crux this arc solves (why M1 stopped):** the dirty `wstage` buffer is FREED at the fixture's teardown (`clear_files_row`, IF=0), but the flush needs IF=1 disk I/O — a naive "flush at teardown" is impossible. **The chosen protocol — a flush queue that survives teardown, drained at IF=1.** Teardown COPIES each dirty descriptor's bytes + its `(cluster, size, dirty [lo,hi))` into a small static flush queue (`flush_enqueue`) BEFORE freeing the `wstage` slot; the copy makes each entry SELF-CONTAINED, so a fixture that faults mid-flow can never strand a queue entry pointing at a freed buffer (the M2 robustness requirement). The demo launcher (running on the demo AP at IF=1) then DRAINS the queue (`flush_drain_one`→`fat::write_at`) and raw-re-reads the sector. Keeping the copy-then-free order means `wstage_all_free()` is still TRUE at teardown, so M1's reviewed teardown-clear proof is undisturbed; the launcher gates its drain on observing that teardown (`cleared`), whose `wstage_all_free()` Acquire pairs with `wstage_free`'s Release to make the enqueue's stores visible (no drain-before-populate race, no stranded late enqueue). **Revoke ordering (a revoked dirty cap must NOT persist stale bytes):** a whole-task TEARDOWN (`clear_files_row`) ENQUEUES dirty; a per-handle REVOKE / open-unwind (`files_free`) DISCARDS dirty (frees the buffer without enqueuing) — so a `CAP_REVOKE` on a dirty File cap repudiates the staged write and it never reaches disk. **The flush is IN-PLACE ONLY — the same discipline as pi4 U9's `write_at`:** clamped to `size`, it never grows/allocates/writes-a-FAT-entry/touches-a-directory, and it RMWs exactly the sectors the dirty `[lo,hi)` span overlaps (for the demo's single contiguous write, exactly one sector — the partial-sector case, other bytes preserved). The dirty range is SET fresh on the first write (not min/max'd against a 0 init), so the flushed span can never start at offset 0 and clobber an un-dirtied sector. **The flush cannot hang:** the BOT pump is BOUNDED (a 2000-iter timeout → `BlockError::Io`), so if the concurrent AP-side drive ever failed it would be a LOUD verdict FAIL (`flushed=false`), never a wedged core — `test-fat sf` is the first empirical proof of concurrent-AP xHCI BOT I/O in the tree. **The M1 capability surface is otherwise byte-identical** (the `u9x-write` fixture blob is UNCHANGED — M2 is entirely kernel-side capture/dirty/enqueue/flush); `SCRATCH.BIN`'s on-disk chain head is captured at the launcher pre-flight (IF=1, gated on `HELLO_STAGED`) and published to `SCRATCH_CLUSTER` before the fixture opens (the x86 stand-in for pi4 capturing `FILE_CLUSTER` in-handler). **Two M1 review notes folded:** `sys_write_file`'s offset advance is now a tx-exact `compare_exchange` claim (CAS-symmetric with `sys_read`, closing the load/store asymmetry before any shared writable descriptor exists), and a writable (`CAP_WRITE`) `SYS_OPEN` on `SHARED_ROW` is refused (a shared multi-tenant writable descriptor could race the unsynchronized staging memcpy). **DUAL-MODE evidence** — disk-backed (`UNAOS_FATIMG=sf ./arroyo test-fat sf 300`): the launcher raw-re-reads the flushed sector from a fresh mount and confirms it CHANGED (to the 16-byte pattern at offset 520, differing from the `0xEE` pre-image) while the directory SIZE did NOT (in place, never grew) — `:: U9x: real File writes — open-RW+seek+write+readback OK, RO-write/wrong-kind/revoked-cap all -EACCES, staged write FLUSHED to FAT (on-disk sector changed + size unchanged) -> PASS ::`, with all 16 prior U1a→U8x verdicts BYTE-IDENTICAL (sorted scratch-worktree baseline diff vs `ddefc40` — the only verdict delta is the U9x line evolving M1→M2; two non-verdict setup banners show best-effort-console-drop jitter, present in the baseline run too — the same artifact the U8x entry documents). The revoke-discard proof is NON-VACUOUS — `u9x_check_revoked_write` first shows (positive control) that a dirty descriptor run through `clear_files_row` DOES enqueue a flush, then that the revoke path leaves the queue empty; only the contrast proves discard. Default no-FAT `./arroyo test 25` stays MISSION SUCCESS AND U9x PASSes its IN-MEMORY core (`in-memory core; no FAT volume, flush is a no-op`) with ZERO AP disk I/O (the pre-flight is `HELLO_STAGED`-gated). `./arroyo check` both arches, **zero aarch64 files touched**. Adversarial 4-lens code review (atomics/ordering + cross-CPU visibility, lifecycle/leaks/double-free, flush correctness + FAT safety, revoke-discard proof + dual-mode verdict), each finding skeptic-verified: 0 confirmed. **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` + `make-fat-img.sh` (the `SCRATCH.BIN` plant) — the write stack (`fat.rs`/`block.rs`/`drivers/xhci`) is REUSED unchanged via its existing API. **Out of scope, tracked:** generation-tagged file-ids (a bare descriptor index lets a revoke+reopen alias a reused slot to a new file's write — real but not ring-3-reachable this arc; the x86 twin of pi4 U11, lands as **U11x**, kept arch-symmetric). **Metal:** CANNOT be metal-confirmed — the xHCI enumeration blocker (`task_47291f90`) leaves `block::info()` None on the real rMBP, so the whole storage-gated demo SKIPS there exactly as M1/U8x do; QEMU-green is the ceiling, the gate is NOT relaxed. - [x] **Open-file lifecycle — `SYS_CLOSE` + generation-tagged file-ids close the revoke+reopen descriptor-aliasing gap (U11x, x86 twin of aarch64 U11 M1 `714daad`; 2026-07-08, QEMU-verified; no metal expected — storage-gated like the rest of the chain).** Closes the hazard U9x M2 ledgered: a per-task FILES descriptor was named to ring 3 by a bare `+1`-biased slot index, so when a File `sys_cap_revoke` freed a slot and a later `sys_open` first-fit-reused it, a lingering sibling handle carrying the old index would silently **re-bind** to the different file now living in that slot (the `FILE_USED` guard lapsed the instant the slot went live again — a use-after-free / descriptor-aliasing disclosure). **Fix = the standard slotmap/generation-index:** a per-slot `FILE_GEN[[AtomicU32; NFILE]; USER_SLOTS+1]` counter bumped **LAST** on EVERY free (`files_free` — the ONE primitive `SYS_CLOSE`, the File-revoke drop, and `sys_open`'s reserve/unwind all route through — and `clear_files_row` at teardown, per slot); File handle value words now pack `(gen << 32) | (idx + 1)` (the low `+1` half keeps the word clear of the `0`=Empty / `u64::MAX`=RESERVING sentinels for any index and gen; **gen 0 packs to exactly `idx+1`, byte-identical to the pre-U11x file-id**, so every prior scaffold/kernel check that reads the value word is unperturbed); and **`file_desc_validate` is the SINGLE seam** — range + `FILE_USED` + gen-match — that EVERY File consumer funnels through: `sys_read`, `sys_write_file`, `sys_seek`, the new `sys_close`, AND `sys_cap_revoke`'s File-descriptor drop (which previously decoded the value word with a bare `checked_sub(1)` — now REQUIRED to route through the seam, since a gen-tagged word's high bits would otherwise land in the index). A stale handle to a reused slot is `-EACCES` (a gen mismatch), never a rebind. **`SYS_CLOSE = 17`** frees the caller's descriptor (bumping its gen) + clears the handle word; it requires NO right (`handle_resolve(row, handle, 0)` — a process may always close a handle it holds; kind + descriptor identity still enforced); a non-File kind is `-EINVAL` (object table UNTOUCHED — never corrupt it by freeing a File slot it does not own), and an unresolvable / already-closed / stale-slot handle is `-EBADF` (new errno `-9`) so a double-close returns cleanly and a use-after-close is denied. **x86 divergence from pi4:** `files_free` DISCARDS un-flushed dirty bytes (the staged write-back is a whole-task-teardown event, `clear_files_row`), so an explicit close of a dirty RW descriptor drops the write exactly as a revoke does — the demo closes RO handles; a future arc can make close enqueue the flush. **The gap is revoke+reopen, not unlink** (x86 has no U10 create/unlink) and it is **NOT ring-3-reachable at U9x** (a revoke clears the only handle, and `sys_open` endows no `CAP_GRANT` to duplicate one — no way for a ring-3 program to hold a stale file-id across a free), so the airtight proof is kernel-side: `u11x_check_gen_rebind` (scratch row 6) claims a descriptor slot + mints its `(gen, idx)`, frees it (gen bumps), first-fit-RE-claims the SAME slot at the bumped gen (a "different file"), and proves the OLD file-id is rejected (`file_desc_validate` → `None`, a gen mismatch) EVEN THOUGH the slot is live again (`FILE_USED` true), while a FRESH file-id resolves — "no silent rebind on slot reuse", isolated from disk/EL0 timing, the row left clean. The ring-3 `u11x-close` fixture proves the SYS_CLOSE contract end-to-end over the immutable staged `SCRATCH.BIN` (witness `0x1F`): open RO + read the 16-byte seed; `SYS_CLOSE` → 0; double-close → `-EBADF`; a read through the closed handle → `-EACCES` (use-after-close denied); reopen (a fresh handle first-fit-reusing the freed slot) + read the seed again. **Evidence:** `:: U11x: open-file lifecycle — open+read/close/double-close(-EBADF)/use-after-close(-EACCES)/reopen+read OK, gen-tagged file-id rejects a stale sibling to a reused slot -> PASS ::` under `UNAOS_FATIMG=sf ./arroyo test-fat sf 300`, with all 17 prior U1a→U9x verdicts **byte-identical**; chained off `u9x_launcher`, storage-gated (chain-inherited) but needing no disk itself, so default no-FAT `./arroyo test 30` stays MISSION SUCCESS AND U11x PASSes there too; `./arroyo check` both arches green, **zero aarch64 files touched** (`arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` only). Every File value word backing a real descriptor is now gen-tagged (`sys_open`, the U6bx no-cap plant, the U9x revoke-check plant); the two opaque scaffold ids (`0x100`/`0x200`) are untouched and resolve identically (idx ≥ `NFILE` → rejected as before). **Still deferred:** cross-process open refcounts + `unlink`-defers-free (pi4 U11 M2); file growth/create/delete (U10 twin); IF-safe interrupt-driven x86 storage. **Metal:** storage-gated like the rest of the capability chain (the xHCI enumeration blocker `task_47291f90` keeps `block::info()` None on the real rMBP), so the demo SKIPS on metal — the gen-tag mechanism itself is always-on in the syscall path, only the demo is gated. **Metal-pending.**

- [x] **UnaFS owner/grants — the by-NAME namespace ACL at `SYS_OPEN` (owned-by-default) + `SYS_FGRANT` delegation + F1 owner-only unlink (U6gx, x86 twin of the aarch64 U6 `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs`; 2026-07-10, QEMU-verified; metal storage-gated).** Closes the U11x M2 ledger-anchor residual #6: on x86 the by-name namespace was GRANT-FREE — any process could open/create/unlink any other process's created file by name. This makes it secure-by-DEFAULT and brings the arches to owner/grants parity (naming: pi4's owner/grants is "U6"; x86 already spent "U6x" on the earlier general-object-table arc, so this twin is **U6gx**). **Owned-by-default:** an `O_CREAT` of a NEW name records the creating principal as the file's OWNER (PRIVATE); the new `O_PUBLIC` mode bit (bit2) opts a create OUT into world-access (the U11x cross-process `DEFER.BIN` fixture tags its shared file `O_PUBLIC`, one bit — U11x stays byte-identical). An open of an EXISTING owned file is admitted only for the owner or a granted principal, else `-EACCES`; a file with NO owner row (a STAGED file — HELLO.BIN/SCRATCH.BIN/GROW.BIN carry no created-name identity — or an `O_PUBLIC` create, or one whose owner has torn down) is PUBLIC, byte-identical to pre-U6gx. **KEY vs PRINCIPAL — the x86 substitutions:** the ACL is keyed by the created file's `U10_NAMES` **name-id** (a direct index — x86's created-file identity space IS the static name table, the same key `DYN_DELETED_G`/`OPENF_*` use), NOT pi4's on-disk `(dir_lba,dir_off)` — so there is no recycled-key aliasing class by construction (U11x M2's reasoning) and no bounded-owner-table "full" case, hence `owned_set_owner` is infallible and a private create has **no fail-closed `-ENOSPC` path** (a clean divergence from pi4). The PRINCIPAL is the address-space **SLOT** fenced by its `SLOT_GEN` incarnation (the x86 `(ASID, ASID_GEN)` analogue, bumped at the top of `clear_handle_row`): a stale owner/grant whose gen no longer matches never authorizes a recycled slot's next tenant. **The store:** an in-kernel `OWNED_FILES` — a `SpinMutex<[OwnedFile; N_U10_NAMES]>` (owner slot+gen, up to 4 grants), taken IRQ-masked via `without_interrupts` on every access (the SYSCALL handler already masks IF; the teardown path does not — the mask makes them symmetric, the pi4 `IrqGuard` discipline). Cleared at `sys_unlink` (the name is gone) and at OWNER teardown (`owned_clear_owner_slot` in `clear_handle_row` — the file reverts to PUBLIC + the table self-cleans). **`SYS_FGRANT = 18`:** the owner grants (a `CAP_READ|CAP_WRITE` subset) or revokes (`rights == 0`) access to a principal named OWNER-SCOPED by a `Child` handle it holds (the `SYS_XFER` idiom — ring 3 never supplies a raw pid/slot); ownership is checked BEFORE the grantee handle is resolved (a non-owner is `-EACCES` whatever it passes, and never learns whether that handle was valid), a nonzero request naming only unsupported bits is `-EINVAL`, a full grant list `-ENOSPC`. The grant is an ACL edge on the FILE — nothing is delivered to the grantee's table; it opens the name and the ACL admits it, and a handle it ALREADY holds survives a revoke (the ACL gates ACQUISITION, not held caps). **F1 — folded from the START** (pi4 learned it post-hoc): `SYS_UNLINK` is OWNER-only for an owned file — the `CAP_WRITE` CHECK admits both the owner AND a WRITE-grantee, but a content grantee must NOT be able to `unlink` + `O_CREAT` to STEAL ownership; a PUBLIC file keeps the prior `CAP_WRITE`-gated unlink. **Evidence** — a TWO-process `u6gx_launcher` choreographs `u6gx-owner` (A) + `u6gx-grantee` (B, its own address space, a launcher-planted Proc entry so A's `Child` handle names it) over per-slot GO/SIG words; A owns `OWNED.BIN` (a 5th `U10_NAMES` member). A (`0x3F`): create PRIVATE + write; grant B R|W → 0; revoke → 0; owner re-open after revoke; **owner unlink while B still holds the file open → 0 (deferred — the U11x M2 combined path)**; O_CREAT re-create → `-EBUSY`. B (`0x1F`): pre-grant open → `-EACCES`; granted RW open + read-back matches A's bytes (content crossed processes); non-owner `SYS_FGRANT` → `-EACCES`; grantee `SYS_UNLINK` → `-EACCES` (F1); post-revoke open → `-EACCES` (its already-held handle survives). B closes at the last step, releasing A's deferred delete; the launcher then proves ownership DIED at the last close (refcount 0, not pending, name re-creatable, `OWNED_FILES` row PUBLIC) and drains the queued delete (disk mode). Verdict `:: U6gx: UnaFS owner/grants — non-owner open -EACCES, owner grant admits R|W (content crossed), non-owner grant -EACCES, grantee unlink -EACCES (delete owner-only), revoke re-denies, owner unlink defers while grantee holds + re-create -EBUSY, ownership dies at last close -> PASS ::`. Gates: `./arroyo test 40`/`90` (in-memory core) 17 PASS + U6gx; `test-fat sf` **22 PASS 0 FAIL** (the 21 priors byte-identical + U6gx — note `grep 'PASS ::'` undercounts by one, U2-0a's verdict ends `PASS (...)`); `p16` 0 FAIL (the documented intermittent best-effort console one-line drop can show 21); `UNAOS_NOSTORAGE=1 test 90` clean skip; `check` both arches, **zero aarch64 files**. **Lane:** additive on `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` + docs; the pattern data lives in-blob. **Flagged, deferred (honest scope, mirroring pi4's):** the owner/grants store is in-kernel, VOLATILE, boot-scoped (no persistent principals yet — the model's own gap), so ownership is meaningful only within a boot and only while the owner lives; a persistent form + retroactive owners for host files awaits on-disk UnaFS attributes. The U11x M2 residuals it inherits (snapshot torn-copy / `SYS_CLOSE` discards dirty / open-vs-unlink TOCTOU) are unchanged — the product fix is the same IF-safe interrupt-driven-storage arc. A `SYS_FGRANT` that endows the grantee with a directly-usable handle (vs. an open-by-name) remains future. **Metal:** pure syscall logic, storage-gated (metal-pending like U8x/U11x; flash-and-watch at the next attended bench). **Metal-pending.**
- [x] **Interrupt-driven x86 storage — the storage service task + a submit/block/complete syscall, live in-place reads + write-through behind the `irqstorage` knob (STOR-1 S1–S3, 2026-07-10, QEMU-verified + ✅ core mechanism METAL-CONFIRMED on the real 2012 rMBP; off by default).** Retires the staged-buffer divergence for the READ source and the in-place WRITE, giving x86 the aarch64 *semantics* (a syscall that blocks on a real transfer completion and reads/writes the live volume in place) via a different *mechanism* (interrupt completion + a sleeping syscall, since x86 storage is async DMA not synchronous PIO). **The mechanism (S1).** A scheduled kernel task — the STORAGE SERVICE TASK — owns the xHCI BOT pump; it runs at IF=1 (like the U9x/U10x launcher drains already do), so the pump's `hlt` wakes on the storage MSI-X. A storage syscall builds a `BlockRequest` on its OWN kernel stack, pushes it on `REQ_QUEUE`, wakes the service task, and blocks on the request's own `Semaphore`; `Semaphore::wait` restores the caller's IF snapshot across the switch, so the handler SLEEPS WITH IF=1 SEMANTICS though it entered IF-masked (SFMASK) — the crux that makes it IF-safe. The task is off every run queue while blocked (`STATE_BLOCKED`), so nothing runs the half-finished syscall on another core, and the frame (hence the request + its semaphore) stays pinned and alive for the whole transfer (the `Condvar::wait`/`done_sem` lifetime discipline). The service task runs the transaction and `post`s the submitter — ALL wakeups at IF=1, never in interrupt context, so the wake-only MSI-X handler stays lock-free (the `sched.rs:28` invariant — handlers never `make_ready` — is preserved by construction), and there is exactly one BOT submitter (no self-deadlock). A bounded wall-clock pump timeout surfaces a wedged transfer as `BlockError::Io` to the sleeping submitter — never a hang; endpoint recovery beyond that is explicitly out of scope. Witness: `bx-blockreq`, a kernel self-test that reads LBA 0 both polled and through a `BlockRequest` and compares → PASS. **Live reads (S2).** A staged descriptor's `SYS_READ` resolves the file BY NAME on the live volume (`find_located` + `read_at`), retiring the in-memory staged source as the READ source (the U6bx "staged-set constraint retires when an IF-safe storage path lands" — closed-when-on). The read BOUNCES through a kernel-stack buffer: the ring-3 destination lives in the submitter's private CR3 (PML4[2]), unreachable by the service task under the kernel CR3, but the submitter's kernel stack is in the shared kernel half, which the service task can reach — the handler copies the fetched bytes out to the (already-validated) user destination on resume, under its own re-installed CR3. **Live write-through (S3).** A non-growable staged descriptor's `SYS_WRITE` (SCRATCH.BIN, `FILE_OPNAME == 0`) writes THROUGH in place via `write_at` — synchronously, out of the IF-masked handler, with nothing staged or marked dirty. This retires the U9x wstage-as-backing + FLUSH queue for the write and CLOSES the close-discards-dirty residual (a `SYS_CLOSE`/teardown of the written descriptor discards nothing — the write is already on the volume). The matching read broadens to `FILE_OPNAME == 0` (RO opens AND non-growable RW opens), so SCRATCH.BIN's write-then-read-back reads the just-written disk bytes. **The gate is per-descriptor and coherent:** the live path fires only for staged descriptors that are in-place-only (`FILE_OPNAME == 0`), with a FAT volume mounted (`HELLO_STAGED`) and the service task up (`service_ready()`); GROWABLE / created descriptors (`FILE_OPNAME` set) and the no-FAT in-memory core fall through to the staged path unchanged. **Everything is behind the `irqstorage` cargo feature (`UNAOS_IRQSTORAGE=1`; mapped in `builder` + `arroyo`), x86_64 only** — the default build never links `drivers/xhci/irqstorage.rs`, so the whole staged path is BYTE-IDENTICAL. Evidence — knob-ON `test 40` (non-FAT) = 18 PASS + MISSION + `:: bx-blockreq: PASS ::` (the live path falls through where there is no volume); knob-ON `UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test` = 23 PASS with `u6bx-file`'s HELLO.BIN read served LIVE, `u9x-write`'s write+read-back served LIVE, and a new witness `:: S3: synchronous write-through — SYS_WRITE persisted to FAT in-syscall (disk held the write pre-drain: true, no deferred flush: true), close-discards-dirty residual retired -> PASS ::`; knob-OFF `test 40` = 18 PASS + MISSION and `UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test` = 22 PASS, BYTE-IDENTICAL (no STOR-1 lines); `UNAOS_NOSTORAGE` clean both knob states; `./arroyo check` both arches (knob on + off), **zero aarch64 files touched**. **Lane:** a new `drivers/xhci/irqstorage.rs` (the service task + `BlockRequest` + `submit`/`submit_read_file`/`submit_write_file`) + `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` (the routing) + `builder`/`arroyo` (the knob) + `main.rs` (spawn + self-test) — the `fat.rs`/`block.rs` writers are REUSED unchanged via their existing API. **Metal:** the 2012 rMBP Panther Point xHCI transfer-IRQ behaviour is UNPROVEN for I/O (enumeration is metal-confirmed; transfer-event-driven I/O has never been exercised on that controller) — so this stays OFF BY DEFAULT until an attended bench confirms; QEMU-green is the ceiling, and the knob is the metal caveat, not a relaxed gate. **STOR-1 S4 — synchronous grow/create/delete in-syscall (LANDED 2026-07-10 knob-on; QEMU-green, metal-pending):** grow/create/delete now run SYNCHRONOUSLY in the syscall via the storage service task (out of the IF-masked handler), retiring the U10x deferred op-queue + its launcher-replay causal-fidelity gap WHEN THE KNOB IS ON. CONSERVATIVE-HYBRID — only the DISK OPS became synchronous (`BlockOp::{Create,Grow,Delete}` + `submit_create`/`submit_grow`/`submit_delete`); the created-file wstage-for-reads, the snapshot-sibling model, and the U11x M2 defer LOGIC (`DYN_DELETED_G`/`OPENF_*`) are UNCHANGED (shared-backing cross-process READS are S5). **S4a** `open_create_new` submits `Create` (idempotent `find_located` → `create_in_root`) FIRST — the file is on disk in-syscall. **S4b** `sys_write_grow` submits `Grow` disk-FIRST, then mirrors the extend into wstage + `FILE_SIZE` (reads still serve wstage this arc); no `mark_dirty` (already durable, so the teardown flush + `clear_files_row` enqueue are skipped knob-on). **S4c** `sys_unlink` enqueues NO held op — the deferred DELETE runs synchronously at the LAST close (`openf_release`). **The teardown-safety crux:** the last-close release runs from a syscall handler (`files_free`) or the launcher's non-self teardown (both BLOCKING-SAFE), OR an `exit`/reap SELF-teardown (IF=0 mid-death, or the reaper with no current task — MUST NOT block: blocking would resume a task whose CR3 is being freed, or `submit` off a scheduler). So `openf_decref` stays pure atomics and SIGNALS the caller; `clear_files_row` detects blocking-safety at runtime via `current_user_cr3() != slot_cr3(slot)` (a launcher is a kernel task, `user_cr3 == 0`; `exit` of the slot has `user_cr3 ==` the slot's CR3; the reaper has no current → both `false`). Every DEMO delete-trigger is blocking-safe (the unlinking fixtures sweep their own descriptors at unlink, and the cross-process holders — u6gx's B, u11m2's launcher — close/tear-down from a blocking-safe context). **The unsafe-teardown last-close IS reachable** (adversarial-review catch): a process that opened a created file CROSS-PROCESS (a U6 grantee / an `O_PUBLIC` sharer) and became its LAST holder, then `exit`s/faults WITHOUT an explicit close, reaches `clear_files_row` with `can_block == false` — where blocking would corrupt (its CR3 is being freed / no current task). That branch therefore DEFERS the delete the IF=0-safe way (the knob-off mechanism): it enqueues a `U10OP_DELETE` op (the file is on disk; no bytes) that a launcher's IF=1 drain completes, leaving `DYN_DELETED_G` SET meanwhile so no re-create aliases the undeleted file — best-effort completion, fail-safe if it strands (name blocked + a chkdsk-reclaimable orphan, no corruption). The clean fix (shared on-disk backing so reads/deletes need no per-holder blocking) is S5. **Created-file in-place writes also write through** (review catch): an in-place SYS_WRITE (within EOF) to a created/growable descriptor persists synchronously via `write_at` + mirrors wstage — else knob-on it would land only in wstage and the created-file teardown flush (disabled knob-on) would drop the acknowledged write on remount. **S4d** the launcher verdicts require the U10 op-queue drained NOTHING knob-on (`count == 0`, `u10_drain_verdict`), reading the state the synchronous ops already wrote (`u11m2` also grows DEFER.BIN on disk so its freed-cluster proof stays non-vacuous); a new `:: S4: grow/create/delete SYNCHRONOUS … op-queue drained NOTHING … ::` witness. (Ledgered residual: a knob-on `open_create_new` whose in-memory claim fails AFTER the synchronous `create_in_root` leaves a harmless 0-length on-disk orphan — the idempotent re-submit + the launcher pre-flight self-heal reconcile it.) Evidence — `./arroyo check` both arches (on+off); knob-ON `test 40` = 18 PASS + MISSION (S4 inert without a FAT volume); knob-ON `UNAOS_IRQSTORAGE=1 UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test 150` = **24 PASS** (22 fixtures + the S3 + S4 witnesses) with u10x-grow / u10cx-create / u10dx-delete / u11m2-unlink / u6gx SYNCHRONOUS and zero `U10_OVERFLOW`; knob-OFF `test 40` = 18 PASS + MISSION and `UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test` = 22 PASS **byte-identical** (no S4/synchronous lines); `UNAOS_NOSTORAGE` clean both knob states; **zero aarch64 files** (`fat.rs`/`block.rs` reused unchanged; new `arch/x86_64/sched.rs::current_user_cr3`). The N1 offset-over-advance note (ledgered at S3) is addressed for the grow: S4b advances `FILE_OFFSET` by the count actually persisted. **Metal (S4):** the cross-process delete-at-last-close races (u11m2/u6gx) are metal-only-validatable (QEMU-TCG will not interleave; design risk 3) — rides the next attended rMBP bench. Also future: real shared backing for cross-process opens (S5, retires the U11x M2 torn-copy/TOCTOU residuals); activating `FAT_MUTATION` on x86 (S6); retiring the staged OPEN (S7). **Security-arc review (2 must-fix + 3 notes, adversarial refuter panel, all folded before merge):** **MF1** — every `REQ_QUEUE` acquisition is now IRQ-masked (`without_interrupts`, scoped to the O(1) push/pop, mask ENDS before the pump's `hlt`) at all three sites (submit push, service pop, start reserve): the service task pops at IF=1 and is timer-preemptible, and run queues are per-CPU with no steal, so a plain spinlock hold preempted mid-flight would deadlock a same-core IF=0 submitter — masking BOTH sides closes it (masking only the service task swaps the trap onto IF=1 kernel-task submitters). **MF2** — `sys_open` refuses a WRITABLE open of HELLO.BIN (staged index 0, immutable EL0 code): `rw && sidx == 0 -> -EACCES`, the ROOT-CAUSE close for the S3 live write-through (which resolves the target BY NAME and would otherwise overwrite the on-disk executable in place, surviving reboot, defeating the default staged path's `staged_cluster(0) == 0` never-flush) AND the pre-S3 in-memory-dirty leg, in one place; SCRATCH.BIN/GROW.BIN keep their write-through. **Severity, honest:** a feature-gated regression that turned a read-only code object into a survives-reboot RW overwrite of the boot-critical image — NOT a live ring-3 escalation (x86 has no arbitrary-blob loader; `sys_spawn` only re-instantiates the pre-staged HELLO.BIN; fixtures are kernel-authored), and no fixture opens HELLO.BIN RW so the fix regresses no verdict. This fix applies in BOTH knob states (immutable code is read-only regardless of the knob). **N1** (ledgered, deferred to S4): a live short-read/EIO leaves the CAS-advanced offset ahead of the bytes delivered → a sequential stream would skip the tail; BENIGN today (live size == staged size, so the delivered count always equals the claim), the advance-by-actual clamp rides S4 where a live grow diverges the sizes. **N2**: `submit()` now panics on an off-scheduler `wait() == false` rather than fall through (no dead-frame DMA; unreachable today). **✅ Core mechanism METAL-CONFIRMED (2026-07-10 attended bench, real 2012 rMBP):** a clean full-chain usbdebug boot captured over FTDI serial — `:: bx-blockreq: PASS ::` (a raw LBA-0 read driven through the SERVICE TASK matched the polled read → transfer-IRQ I/O + the submit/block/complete handshake WORK on the real Panther Point xHCI, RESOLVING design risk 1, the one genuinely unproven thing), `:: U6bx … PASS ::` (HELLO.BIN read served LIVE, S2), `:: S3: synchronous write-through … PASS ::` + `:: U9x … on-disk sector changed … PASS ::` (SCRATCH.BIN written+read-back LIVE + synchronous on the real FAT16 card, S3), and the ENTIRE prior chain U1a→U6gx re-confirmed knob-ON incl. the deferred U10 on-disk grow/create/delete proofs; zero faults / timeouts / deadlock, HELLO.BIN intact. The bench ran the PRE-fix code, but the two review fixes are behaviorally transparent to every witnessed path (they touch REQ_QUEUE lock timing and HELLO.BIN RW-open, which the witnesses do not exercise), so the metal-confirm STANDS; the fixed code re-benches at the S4 attended bench. **STOR-1 S5 — real shared backing for cross-process created-file reads (LANDED 2026-07-11 knob-on; QEMU-green, metal-pending):** a CREATED-file descriptor's `SYS_READ` now reads the LIVE shared on-disk volume BY NAME (`created_read_live` → `submit_read_file`), retiring the private wstage snapshot as the read SOURCE — so a cross-process (or same-process sibling) opener READS a peer's writes (shared backing) instead of a stale open-time copy. Three coupled changes, all knob-on (`s4_sync_storage()` = knob + FAT + service): **(C2)** `sys_read`'s created branch (gated `FILE_CREATED && FILE_OPNAME != 0 && HELLO_STAGED && service_ready`) resolves `U10_NAMES[nameid]` and bounces the live read through a kernel-stack buffer (GROW.BIN — a growable STAGED file, `FILE_CREATED == false` — keeps its wstage serve, byte-identical); **(C3)** `open_created_sibling` seeds the sibling wstage EMPTY (no snapshot COPY — the ledgered torn-copy is closed BY CONSTRUCTION, there is no copy to tear) and stamps the sibling's identity from the CALLER-verified `nameid` (never a re-read of the source slot) after RE-VALIDATING the source still names it (`FILE_USED && FILE_CREATED && FILE_OPNAME == nameid+1`, else `-ENOENT`); **(C4)** `sys_open_dynamic` re-checks `DYN_DELETED_G[nameid]` AFTER `created_desc_any_row` resolves (fail-closed `-EBUSY`/`-ENOENT`). **Residuals 3 (torn-copy/disclosure) + 4 (open-vs-unlink TOCTOU) are CLOSED-WHEN-ON,** with one honest residual: the source read + the C3 re-check are not atomic, so a metal-only recycle-to-a-DIFFERENT-name window is NARROWED (the sibling can only ever name the caller-verified nameid, so a recycle to another name is caught fail-closed) but not eliminated — the airtight fix is the S6 namespace lock (`with_fat_lock`, out-of-lane `fat.rs`). No corruption / freed-chain / wrong-file LIVE read is possible: the single service task serializes read-vs-delete, and the sibling names the caller-verified nameid. (**Seat-review precision, 2026-07-11:** that claim covers the different-name leg; the same-NAMEID reincarnation leg — an unlink+synchronous-delete+recreate of the SAME name landing entirely inside one IF-masked open window, rebinding a stale ACL vet to a fresh incarnation — is physically near-unrealizable but not excluded by this argument; S6's namespace lock closes it. Also carried, NOT fixed by S5: the pre-existing -EIO-synchronous-delete-clears-`DYN_DELETED_G` wart — a knob-on re-create after a storage error idempotently adopts the stale on-disk entry; on the S6 baton.) **Witness:** `s5_shared_backing_witness` (kernel-side, cross-row, reuses DEFER.BIN, runs first in the u11m2 launcher, SILENT skip off knob-on-FAT, unconditional cleanup) proves NON-VACUOUSLY that the read source is shared/live: a cross-row sibling holds an EMPTY private wstage (`WSTAGE_LEN == 0` — the discriminator; the old snapshot model would hold 16 bytes) YET `created_read_live` returns a peer's POST-OPEN overwrite (P2) — the private buffer never saw it, only the live read did. The u11m2/u6gx EL0 fixtures supply the production-faithful DISPATCH proof (a real `SYS_READ` of a cross-process sibling returns the correct 16-byte pattern from an EMPTY-wstage sibling → the read can only be live). **NO concurrent stress + no metal-race framing:** read/write file ops serialize through the single service task, so tearing is impossible by construction (architectural, not a QEMU-TCG artifact). **⚠ SCHEDULING DEADLOCK found + fixed (QEMU-reproducible, NOT metal-only):** routing created reads through the single service task makes a read BLOCK on it; a NON-preemptible ring-3 fixture busy-spinning (IF=0, `spawn_user_in_space`) on the service task's core (u6gx's owner A parked on its GO word; A and the service task both take `online.first()` = cpu 1) makes the core unschedulable, so the service task never runs and u6gx's grantee B's cross-core created read blocks forever (deadlock: launcher waits B → B waits the service task → the service task waits behind A's IF=0 spin → A waits the launcher). No priority preempts an IF=0 task. Fixed by (a) spawning the service task `PRIO_HIGH` (a system service other tasks block on must out-rank a spinning user task — a same-priority cross-core wake only "waits its turn", `sched::poke_for` preempts only `prio > running`), and (b) making u6gx's cooperative-spin fixtures PREEMPTIBLE knob-on (`spawn_user_preemptible`, gated on `s4_sync_storage()` so knob-off keeps the byte-identical non-preemptible spawn; u6gx is register-only so the ledgered unsaved-FP-across-preemptible-switch gap is not reachable) — the timer then evicts A and the `PRIO_HIGH` service task runs. **Metal relevance (honest):** a well-behaved program blocks/yields; a MALICIOUS busy-spinner on the service core could still DoS created reads on this single-service-task design — a broader scheduler-fairness concern (a yield syscall / a dedicated service core / per-core service tasks) noted as future work, out of S5's scope. **Evidence** — `./arroyo check` both arches (knob on+off); knob-ON `UNAOS_IRQSTORAGE=1 UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test` = full chain 0 FAIL with `:: S3:`/`:: S4:`/`:: S5:` witnesses + u10cx/u11m2/u6gx created reads served LIVE + u6gx PASS (owner/grantee choreography completes — the deadlock fix); knob-ON `test 40` = MISSION + `bx-blockreq` with NO `:: S5:` line (silent skip, no FAT); knob-OFF `test 40` = MISSION and `UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test` = BYTE-IDENTICAL (no `:: S3:`/`:: S4:`/`:: S5:`/`PRIO_HIGH` lines; u6gx passes non-preemptibly); `UNAOS_NOSTORAGE` clean both knob states; the documented best-effort console drops one verdict line under multi-AP contention (0 FAIL every run). **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` (the read routing + `open_created_sibling` + `sys_open_dynamic` + the witness + the u6gx preemptible spawn) + `drivers/xhci/irqstorage.rs` (the service task `PRIO_HIGH`); `fat.rs`/`block.rs` reused unchanged; **zero aarch64.** **Metal (S5):** the shared-backing correctness is deterministic (proven), but S5 rides the same attended rMBP bench as S4 (transfer-IRQ I/O + create/grow/delete + the cross-process read/write + fsck) — ✅ **METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-11 (round-6 attended rMBP bench, Boot 1 on a pristine card + the pristine re-confirm boot 3′):** `./arroyo mbench` PASS 29/29 required witnesses + 0 forbidden + 0 FAIL/EXCEPTION/panic on the real 2012 rMBP over FTDI serial (knob-ON usbdebug videobench media, kernel.elf sha 872f3e4a; live UNAOSRW FAT16 card). `:: S5: … cross-process read serves LIVE shared backing … -> PASS ::` fired FIRST-EVER on metal; **`:: U6gx: … -> PASS ::` is the DEADLOCK-CLOSURE witness under real SMP** — u6gx's grantee completes its LIVE cross-core read, which is possible ONLY via the PRIO_HIGH wake + the per-core timer evicting the preemptible spinner, so the scheduler deadlock fix WORKS on silicon (the metal proof the security-tier review deferred; re-confirmed a 3rd time on the pristine boot 3′). `:: STOR-1: storage service task live on cpu 1 (irqstorage, PRIO_HIGH) ::` + S4-first-metal (`:: S4: … SYNCHRONOUS … -> PASS ::`) + `:: bx-blockreq: PASS ::` (transfer-IRQ I/O re-confirm on the MF1/MF2-fixed code) + the full U-chain all PASS; HELLO.BIN intact. **Honest artifact (ledgered, NOT a regression):** a later reboot on a NON-re-prepped card showed a `witness=0x0` false-FAIL across the WHOLE created-file family (U11x/U11m2/U6gx, `done=2` = fixtures EXITED, not the deadlock's `done=0` hang) — the documented stateful-fixture staleness; forensics on the stale card (archived) showed created-family leftovers (a 0-byte create-then-cut `DELME.BIN` + `FRESH.BIN` + a grown `GROW.BIN`) that a pristine card never carries, and a pristine re-prep re-confirmed all-PASS on the SAME kernel (identical storage code), proving it a card-state artifact orthogonal to the mechanism. **STOR-1 S6 — the syscall-layer NAMESPACE lock (LANDED 2026-07-11 knob-on; QEMU-green + ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-12 rMBP): CLOSES S5 residual 1 AIRTIGHT.** S5 narrowed the created-file recycle / open-vs-unlink windows to fail-SAFE (a sibling can only ever name the caller-verified name-id) but left the source-resolve (`created_desc_any_row`) + the re-validate (`== nameid+1`) NON-atomic — a metal-only recycle-to-a-different-name / same-name-reincarnation window. S6 adds an IRQ-masked `SpinMutex` (`NAMESPACE`/`ns_lock`, `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs`, the pi4 F3 `NAMESPACE` twin) that makes the THREE created-file name sequences MUTUALLY ATOMIC: the sibling-open decision (`sys_open_dynamic`: deleted-check → `created_desc_any_row` → ACL → `open_created_sibling`), the fresh create (`open_create_new`, with a new ACL-checked idempotent-sibling fallback closing the create-races-create ownership-theft window), and the `sys_unlink` claim + descriptor sweep. No unlink/create can interleave inside another sequence's resolve→claim, so the recycle/reincarnation legs are closed by exclusion. **⚠ The lock is held for the O(1) IN-MEMORY namespace decision ONLY, NEVER across a `submit`/BOT pump — the S5 deadlock lesson binds:** the two blocking disk ops are lifted OUT of the locked region — the idempotent `submit_create` runs BEFORE the lock (`open_create_new`), and the last-close `submit_delete` runs AFTER it (`sys_unlink` performs it post-`drop(ns)`; `files_free` split into `files_free_clear` [atomic clears, lock-safe] + `openf_release` [blocking, lock-free]). The disk create/delete are idempotent AND serialized by the single-service-task BOT writer, so lifting them out re-introduces no disk-level race. `sys_close`/`files_free` need NOT take the lock: completing a recycle requires a `files_alloc` REUSE, which happens only inside a lock-held open sequence; a bare close mid-resolve makes the re-validate fail closed (`-ENOENT`, fail-safe). **§6 decision 2 RESOLVED:** `FAT_MUTATION` is NOT activated on x86 — VACUOUS under the single-service-task-writer invariant (the pi4 FAT lost-update RMW race is unreachable with exactly one BOT writer); the real gap was syscall-layer namespace atomicity, not the FAT RMW. `fat.rs` (shared kernel-core) stays untouched. **Carry-overs folded (seat fold-ins):** the `-EIO` synchronous-delete now gates its `DYN_DELETED_G` clear on delete SUCCESS (`openf_perform_delete`) — a wedged delete leaves the name blocked (fail-SAFE) rather than a knob-on re-create adopting a stale on-disk entry; + three uncounted mbench witnesses (MF2 immutable-code RW refusal `S4-mf2`; the last-close synchronous-delete outcome `S4-race`; the S6 lock-holds `S6-witness`) + a u6gx drain-verdict tighten (`count==0` knob-on). **Witness `s6_witness_launcher` (`:: S6-witness: … witness OK ::`, uncounted, knob-on FAT):** a cross-core in-RAM RMW on the SAME `ns_lock` — LOCKED reaches `2*N` intact; the UNLOCKED control loses under contention (QEMU actually interleaved: locked 240000/240000, unlocked lost ~119800/240000, proving the lock closes a REAL race — the positive proof; true-parallelism is otherwise metal-latent per risk 3). **Evidence:** `./arroyo check` both arches (knob on+off, zero aarch64); knob-ON `UNAOS_IRQSTORAGE=1 UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test` = full chain 0 FAIL with `:: S3:`/`:: S4:`/`:: S5:` + `:: S4-mf2:`/`:: S4-race:`/`:: S6-witness:` witnesses + u10c/u11m2/u6gx created reads served LIVE + u6gx PASS; knob-ON `test 40` = MISSION + `bx-blockreq` (no FAT witnesses, silent skip); knob-OFF `test 40` = MISSION and `UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test` = BYTE-IDENTICAL (no `:: S3/S4/S5/S6:`/witness lines; the NAMESPACE lock is uncontended-transparent knob-off); `UNAOS_NOSTORAGE` clean both. **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` only (`fat.rs`/`block.rs` reused unchanged); zero aarch64. **Metal (S6): ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-12 (round-9 attended rMBP bench, Boot 1, TWO clean boots).** The true-parallelism proof landed on silicon: `:: S6-witness: NAMESPACE cross-core RMW (worker on cpu 1) — locked 240000/240000 intact (0 lost); unlocked lost 119996/240000 -> the created-file open/create/unlink sequence lock serializes under real contention -> witness OK ::` — the UNLOCKED control lost ~50% of its increments to REAL cross-core parallelism while the LOCKED path stayed 0-lost (the metal proof risk 3 deferred; QEMU also interleaved here, metal confirms it on true SMP). The two seat-fold-in witnesses fired clean too: `:: S4-mf2: RW open of staged code (HELLO.BIN) refused -EACCES … witness OK ::` and both `:: S4-race(close-release): … ::` / `:: S4-race(teardown-release): last close took the SYNCHRONOUS in-syscall delete path (no held op) … witness OK ::` (the S4 cross-process delete-at-last-close races — QEMU-TCG-uninterleavable, design risk 3 — took the synchronous path on real SMP). Knob-ON usbdebug+videobench media (kernel.elf sha `3083b467`); `./arroyo mbench` PASS 37/37 required + 0 forbidden + 6/6 pending matched, 0 fault; log `~/unaos-bench/rmbp-serial-2026-07-12-185626.log`. **STOR-1 S7 — retire the U6bx staged-open constraint (LANDED 2026-07-12 knob-on; QEMU-green + ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-12 rMBP): `sys_open` resolves an open of ANY on-disk file.** The read machinery already resolved arbitrary names live (`service_read_file` → `find_located`); only the OPEN layer was gated to the staged/`U10_NAMES` name tables, so a pre-existing file that was neither staged nor a U10 name was `-ENOENT` at open. S7 adds a DYNAMIC fallthrough: knob-on, when a name is neither staged nor a U10 name, `sys_open_dynamic` calls `open_dynamic_ondisk`, which sizes the file via a new `BlockOp::Stat` (`submit_stat` → `find_located`, the one fact the IF-masked handler cannot resolve itself) and opens a READ-ONLY descriptor whose reads route live BY the stored NAME through `submit_read_file` (`sys_read`'s new `FILE_DYNLEN != 0` branch, filled a page at a time so an arbitrary file may exceed the one-page staged bound). **Three security properties hold by construction:** (1) **READ-ONLY — MF2 generalized:** a writable dynamic open is refused `-EACCES`, so S7 opens NO write path to arbitrary files (the S3 in-place write-through resolves by name and could otherwise overwrite any resolvable file — the exact hazard MF2 closed for HELLO.BIN, here generalized to every non-staged file). (2) **ACL surface unchanged:** a dynamic on-disk file has no `U10_NAMES` id, so it can never enter `OWNED_FILES` and is inherently PUBLIC — no new policy (invariant: mirror created files' ACL). (3) **Staged/U10 names EXCLUDED — CASE-INSENSITIVELY** (the security-critical step, security-review CONFIRMED-and-FIXED before merge): the dynamic fallthrough first CANONICALIZES the ring-3 name to 8.3 UPPERCASE (`make_ascii_uppercase`), then excludes it if the canonical form is a staged name (`staged_lookup`) OR a U10 name (`u10_name_id`). This is load-bearing because the on-disk resolver the dynamic path submits to (`find_located` → `DirEntry::eq_name`) matches CASE-INSENSITIVELY (equal length + `eq_ignore_ascii_case`), whereas `staged_lookup`/`u10_name_id` compare byte-EXACT against uppercase constant tables — so WITHOUT canonicalization a case-variant of an owned/created U10 name (e.g. `owned.bin`) would miss both exact-match exclusions yet resolve on disk to the OWNED file, bypassing the U6gx owner ACL and reading a private file (a straight confidentiality break). Because `eq_name` requires EQUAL LENGTH, the only queries that resolve to a U10/staged name are exactly its case-variants, every one of which uppercases back to the exact table entry — so canonicalization is a PROVABLY COMPLETE defense (no trailing-space/padding gap). A closed created file thus stays `-ENOENT` in any casing, never re-resolved from disk as PUBLIC; created-file + owner-ACL semantics preserved exactly. The `s7_openany_witness` includes a NEGATIVE regression-lock leg: `sys_open_dynamic("owned.bin")` MUST be refused (a name-table decision, so deterministic regardless of on-disk state). **No `ns_lock`:** a dynamic file is outside the U10 mutation namespace (it can't be created/grown/unlinked via the syscall API), so its resolve races no create/unlink and the `Stat` block never runs under the namespace lock (the S5 deadlock class stays closed). **The pre-stage buffer is RETAINED** — knob-off it still backs every staged read, and `HELLO_BYTES` still serves `sys_spawn`'s program-image copy in BOTH knob states (no live alternative off the IF-masked path) — but knob-on it is no longer the file-open boundary: all reads (staged S2/S3, created S5, arbitrary on-disk S7) serve the live volume. **Seat fold (S6 fold-in 1):** `install_file_handle`'s handle-table-full unwind now documents the non-local invariant (pending ⟹ deleted ⟹ the open was already `-EBUSY`) that makes its `openf_release` safe against a concurrent last-close; a dynamic descriptor has no name-id so its unwind skips `openf_release` (vacuous). **Witness `s7_openany_witness` (`:: S7-openany: … resolved dynamically + read its live content off the pre-stage set … witness OK ::`, uncounted, knob-on FAT):** drives the REAL dispatcher (`sys_open_dynamic`) on a scratch row for README.TXT (a non-staged/non-U10 file every FAT image carries; pre-S7 this exact open was `-ENOENT`), proving a conjunction — the open minted a DYNAMIC descriptor (`FILE_DYNLEN != 0`) stamped with the resolved name, sized from the LIVE volume (`FILE_SIZE == 57` on sf), and a read BY THE STORED NAME returned the file's known content prefix. **Evidence:** `./arroyo check` both arches (knob on+off, zero aarch64, zero new warnings); knob-ON `UNAOS_IRQSTORAGE=1 UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test 200` = full chain 0 FAIL with `:: S3/S4/S5:` + `:: S4-mf2/S4-race/S6-witness:` witnesses intact + the new `:: S7-openany: … witness OK ::` + u6gx/u11m2 PASS; knob-OFF `test 25` = MISSION and `UNAOS_FATIMG=sf test 200` = BYTE-IDENTICAL (all S7 code `irqstorage`-gated or comment-only — no S7/dynamic lines); `UNAOS_NOSTORAGE` clean both. **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` + `drivers/xhci/irqstorage.rs` (both x86/`irqstorage`-gated); `fat.rs`/`block.rs` reused unchanged; **zero aarch64.** **Residuals (ledgered, out of scope):** arbitrary-file open is READ-ONLY (a writable arbitrary-file path is future work); files > 2 GiB are not openable (the `i32` stat channel); GROW.BIN reads still serve wstage knob-on. **Metal (S7): ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-12 (round-9 attended rMBP bench, Boot 1, TWO clean boots).** `:: S7-openany: a non-staged on-disk file (README.TXT, 57 bytes) resolved dynamically + read its live content off the pre-stage set, and a case-variant of an owned name ("owned.bin") was refused (owner ACL not bypassed) == expected — witness OK ::` fired on the real 2012 rMBP over FTDI serial (pristine UNAOSRW FAT16 card carrying README.TXT + HELLO/SCRATCH/GROW, created names absent). The arbitrary-file open resolves + reads the live volume BY NAME on silicon, AND the security-critical case-insensitive owner-ACL exclusion HOLDS on metal (`owned.bin` lowercase refused — the U6gx private OWNED.BIN not disclosed): the confidentiality regression-lock the security review confirmed-and-fixed is now metal-proven. Rode the knob-ON usbdebug+videobench media (kernel.elf sha `3083b467`); `./arroyo mbench` PASS 37/37 + 0 forbidden + 6/6 pending matched, 0 fault. **STOR-1 S8 — writable dynamic on-disk files, OVERWRITE-ONLY (LANDED 2026-07-15 knob-on; ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED same day on the real 2012 rMBP): a RW open of ANY arbitrary on-disk file overwrites it live.** S7 opened arbitrary files read-only; S8 honors `mode` bit0 in `open_dynamic_ondisk`, so a RW open of a non-staged/non-U10 file gets `CAP_READ｜CAP_WRITE` (still NO wstage — a dynamic descriptor never stages). `sys_write_file` gains a dynamic branch keyed on `FILE_DYNLEN != 0`, inserted BEFORE the `FILE_WSTAGE == 0 → EIO` guard: it CAS-claims `[offset, offset+want)` with `want = min(len, FILE_SIZE − offset)`, validates the whole user source, then bounces page-at-a-time through a kernel `[u8; PAGE_SIZE]` to `submit_write_file` BY the stored NAME (`dyn_write_live`, the `created_read_live` write twin). **This IS a deliberate widening — ledgered, not hidden:** any ring-3 task with `sys_open` can now overwrite any genuinely-arbitrary on-disk file, PUBLIC-by-default for writes exactly as S7 made it PUBLIC for reads (a dynamic file has no U10 name-id, so it never enters `OWNED_FILES` — invariant 5: mirror created-file policy, don't invent new policy). **What did NOT widen:** (1) **MF2 stays closed by EXCLUSION** — `sys_open_dynamic` canonicalizes to 8.3 UPPERCASE and drops every staged name (HELLO.BIN EL0 code) and every U10 owned/created name in ANY casing BEFORE the dynamic path, so a writable dynamic descriptor can only name a file that is neither code nor ownable (the same case-insensitive exclusion S7 introduced — `owned.bin` lowercase still refused, owner ACL intact); (2) **overwrite-only** — a write AT/PAST EOF returns 0 (never grows: `write_at` allocates no clusters and touches no directory by contract), so the on-disk SIZE is immutable for the boot even as CONTENT changes; (3) **fail-closed** — ANY submit error or short chunk fails the WHOLE write `-EIO` (never a masked partial; the CAS-advanced offset makes a partial-return silently skip bytes AND hide the error — the ledgered N1 pattern); (4) **no `ns_lock`** anywhere in the new path (a dynamic name is outside the U10 mutation namespace — the S5 deadlock class stays closed). The ACL surface, the owner-only unlink, and the staged/U10 exclusion are all unchanged. **Witness `s8_write_witness` (`:: S8-write: … overwrote a live 16-byte pattern … RESTORED the seed … overwrite never grows … MF2 intact … witness OK ::`, uncounted, knob-on FAT):** RW-opens S8W.BIN (64 B of 0xA5, non-staged/non-U10) via the REAL `sys_open_dynamic`, asserts `CAP_WRITE` + `FILE_DYNLEN != 0` + size 64, overwrites a distinct 16-byte pattern live at offset 8 (`dyn_write_live`), reads it back == pattern, then RESTORES the 0xA5 seed and re-verifies (idempotent across boots/power-cuts, image left pristine); NEGATIVE legs — a write claim at offset == size returned 0 while S8's overwrite-only constraint stood (that witness leg was retired when STOR-1 S9 made past-EOF growth the defined, bounded behavior — see the S9 entry), and a case-variant of staged code (`hello.bin`) RW is refused `< 0` (the MF2-under-S8 regression lock). **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` (`open_dynamic_ondisk` rights + the `sys_write_file` dynamic branch + `dyn_write_live` + the witness) + `scripts/make-fat-img.sh` (the S8W.BIN plant) + specs; `fat.rs`/`irqstorage.rs`/`block.rs` reused unchanged; **zero aarch64.** **Metal: ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-15** (attended rMBP bench): `S8-write` fired on 3 boots incl. a genuine power-cut pair, both full-spec boots 43/43 required / 0 forbidden; `round6-rmbp.spec` `S8-write` PROMOTED to REQUIRE (details: docs/MILESTONES.md, serial log `rmbp-serial-2026-07-15-101517.log`). **STOR-1 S9 — dynamic on-disk file GROWTH (LANDED 2026-07-16 knob-on; QEMU-verified, metal PENDING): a past-EOF write on a dynamic RW descriptor now EXTENDS the file live instead of returning 0.** S8 was strictly overwrite-only (a write at/past EOF returned 0, the on-disk SIZE immutable for the boot); S9 retires that constraint the S-chain way. A past-EOF write on a dynamic RW descriptor (`open_dynamic_ondisk`, `FILE_DYNLEN != 0`) routes through a new `dyn_write_grow` → `dyn_grow_live` → the storage service task's existing `Grow` op (`submit_grow` → `service_grow_file` → `fat::write_grow`: alloc + zero-fill + chain new clusters, RMW the data, bump the directory size LAST), so the extend lands SYNCHRONOUSLY on the live volume; on success `FILE_SIZE` then `FILE_OFFSET` are published (size-before-offset). An IN-EOF write keeps S8's in-place overwrite UNCHANGED. **GROWTH BOUNDS (the DoS ledger — unbounded growth off a syscall is a resource-exhaustion surface, so both caps are explicit):** PER-WRITE `DYN_GROW_MAX` = one page (4096 B) — a single grow syscall persists at most one page through ONE kernel bounce buffer and ONE atomic `submit_grow` (no chunk loop), a longer write returns the page-clamped count; PER-FILE `DYN_FILE_MAX` = 64 KiB — the dynamic path may never grow a file's EOF past this absolute ceiling (a target offset at/past it is refused `-ENOSPC`, a crossing write clamps to `offset + want <= DYN_FILE_MAX`). The per-file cap is ABSOLUTE (not opened-size relative), so a close+reopen cannot walk a file past the ceiling. **-EIO honesty (whole-op-or-error, mirroring S2/S3/S8):** disk is written FIRST; a failed grow returns `-EIO` with the descriptor UNTOUCHED (offset/size never CAS-advanced) and the on-disk size unchanged — `write_grow` bumps the visible directory size LAST, so a mid-op failure leaves no half-acknowledged extend. **Defenses unchanged:** the staged/U10 exclusion set + the uppercase-canon MF2 refusal + the public-by-default ACL surface are EXACTLY as S8 built them — S9 widens WRITE semantics for already-S8-writable files only, never WHICH files are writable (HELLO.BIN / staged-code RW stays `-EACCES`). No `ns_lock` (the dynamic path stays outside the U10 namespace — the S5 deadlock class stays closed). NEW hardening: `open_dynamic_ondisk` now refuses a RW open on `SHARED_ROW` (mirroring `sys_open_staged`), guaranteeing a dynamic RW descriptor is always a PRIVATE single-writer slot — the grow path's un-CAS'd offset advance then races nothing (this also tightens the S8 overwrite path, whose CAS claim was previously the only guard). **Witness:** `s9_grow_witness` (uncounted knob-on FAT) owns a throwaway S9G.BIN lifecycle (self-heal delete → create → grow 0→48→96 via the live seam → `submit_stat` confirms the on-disk size GREW → `submit_read_file` matches the appended bytes → REAL `sys_write_file` at the 64 KiB ceiling refused `-ENOSPC` → `hello.bin` RW still refused → delete), idempotent by construction. RESIDUAL (ledgered, mirrors S8's accepted user-pointer-clamp NOTE): the PER-WRITE page clamp is a short-count path verified by review, not by an automated witness — no user memory is mappable from the launcher context to drive a > 1-page grow through the full `sys_write_file` bounce; the PER-FILE `-ENOSPC` ceiling IS witnessed (the cap fires before any user deref). **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` (the `dyn_write_grow`/`dyn_grow_live` grow path + the `DYN_GROW_MAX`/`DYN_FILE_MAX` caps + the `SHARED_ROW` guard + the S9 witness; the S8 witness's obsolete past-EOF-returns-0 leg retired) + specs; `fat.rs`/`irqstorage.rs`/`block.rs` reused unchanged (the `Grow` op + `write_grow` machinery already exist); **zero aarch64 diff**. Gates: `./arroyo check` both arches 0 new warnings; knob-on `S9-grow … witness OK` (mbench 22/22, 0 forbidden); knob-off byte-identical (0 S9 lines); NOSTORAGE clean; `test-arm` MISSION SUCCESS. `round6-rmbp.spec` `PENDING S9-grow` promotes to REQUIRE after the attended rMBP bench.
- [x] **Ring-3 FP/SIMD hygiene (x86) — first-entry scrub (U2.5 Part 0-i, 2026-07-03, QEMU-verified; metal-pending FTDI cable day).** `user_task_trampoline` now runs `FNINIT` then eight `FLDZ` + eight `FSTP ST(0)` before the GPR scrub, so all eight *physical* x87/MMX registers hold 0.0 at first ring-3 entry — `FNINIT` alone only empties the tags, and an MMX move reads the aliased mantissa bits regardless of tag, hence the `FLDZ`/`FSTP` pass. The x86 twin of the aarch64 M6f FP/SIMD scrub. **Scope is honest — first entry only:** there is still NO FXSAVE/FXRSTOR context switching, so cross-*task* FP residue once multiple programs share an AP is NOT yet covered (rides the x86 context-switch work), and there is no negative residue-read fixture — this is exercised transitively (U1a/U1b/U2 still enter ring 3 and pass). **U3.5 (preemptible ring 3) makes this a live gap, not just a future one:** a preempted ring-3 task's FP/SIMD state is NOT saved/restored across the switch (only its GPRs are), so a preemptible program that used FP would see its FP file clobbered by whatever ran in between. The U3.5 spinner fixture is **register-only** (it touches no FP/SIMD), so it is safe today; a preemptible program that uses FP must wait for the FXSAVE/FXRSTOR context-switch work. **U4x (the process model) widens this further:** Part 0 (RSP0-at-dispatch) enables >1 user task per core, so FP state is now unsaved across ANY ring-3 context switch between two user tasks (e.g. a parent that blocks in `sys_wait` while a child runs), not only across a timer preemption. The U4x parent/orphan/child fixtures are ALL register-only (GPR only), so the gap is reachable but not triggered — it is deliberately ledgered here rather than closed this arc. The `[ ]` FP context-switch item stays open (now with two consumers: U3.5 preemption and U4x multi-task-per-core). The x87 mnemonics assembled cleanly under the `+soft-float` kernel target, so no `FNINIT`-only fallback (and no MMX-alias residue caveat) was needed. SSE stays contained separately by `OSFXSR=0` (#UD → kill), so only x87/MMX is at risk. (First step surfaced by the U2 integrator review, 2026-07-02.)
- [ ] Self-vs-non-self loader check for U2-loaded programs — **not yet.** The U2 FAT loader treats `HELLO.BIN` as UNTRUSTED INPUT: it is bounded only by size (must fit one 4 KiB ring-3 code page; oversize/missing → clean skip, no panic) and is contained solely by ring-3 + NX + W^X + SMEP + the U1b fault-kill net (a malformed program cannot corrupt kernel state or escalate privilege — though it *can* still spin forever and wedge its AP; see the preemptible-ring-3 ledger item below). The loader now **zeros the ENTIRE ring-3 window** (all four pages — code + data + two stack) through each page's identity alias before copying the program in (U2.5 Part 0-iii, 2026-07-03), not just the code page, so a second loaded program cannot read the previous program's / the U1a/U1b fixtures' residue from the data or stack pages; the writes go through the identity aliases, never `USER_BASE`, so the code mapping stays read-only (W^X across cores holds). No signature or allowlist gate exists yet — that is the U-chain's code-signing item (design: `dev/OS/04_SECURITY_IMMUNITY/intrusion_detection.md`), the first consumer of which is exactly this loader. **The aarch64 M6g loader (2026-07-03) is the exact twin of this posture** (size-bounded only, contained by EL0 + per-page perms + the M6b fault-kill net — see the aarch64 ledger below); the code-signing gate, when it lands, must cover BOTH loaders

- [x] **Sockets as capabilities — the UDP socket syscall family, ring 3's first network reach (SOCK-2, 2026-07-12, QEMU-verified; metal-pending — x86-only, behind `UNAOS_SMOLNET`).** Four new syscalls give ring 3 datagram send/recv over the persistent smoltcp `SocketSet`: `sys_socket = 40` (mints a socket), `sys_bind = 41`, `sys_sendto = 42`, `sys_recvfrom = 43`. **SOCKNUM (WINX-1, 2026-07-29): the family moved from 19–27 to 40–48** (relative order preserved; the numbers in this ledger are the CURRENT ones — SOCK-2 landed at 19–22, SOCK-3 at 23–25, SOCK-6 at 26–27). The original range violated the **cross-arch shared-number law** — a syscall number names the same verb on every arch — because aarch64 had already spent 19–27 on `MSEND`/`MRECV`/`THREAD_SPAWN`/`THREAD_EXIT`/`THREAD_JOIN`/`FB_MAP`/`FB_PRESENT`/`FUTEX`/`INPUT_POLL`. Nothing caught it while the families could not coexist (x86 compiled no window/thread verbs, aarch64 compiles no socket verbs); bringing the x86 WINDOW verbs up made it load-bearing (`SYS_INPUT_POLL` and `SYS_ACCEPT` would both have to be 27 in one dispatch), so the x86-only family moved. **No security delta** — the rights model, the `handle_resolve` CHECK, the gen fence and every refusal are byte-for-byte unchanged; only the dispatch immediates moved, and every caller was in-tree. **A socket is a capability, enforced at the SAME single `handle_resolve` CHECK the File syscalls use:** it is a new object-table kind `KIND_SOCKET` (already scaffolded as the U6bx/U9x kind-negative) whose value word is the persistent-set id (+1-biased off the 0/`u64::MAX` sentinels); `sys_socket` mints `CAP_READ|CAP_WRITE`, **send requires `CAP_WRITE`, recv requires `CAP_READ`** — so `SYS_CAP` GRANT attenuates a socket cap to send-only / recv-only and `SYS_XFER` hands one to another process, for free, with no new enforcement code. A wrong-kind / missing-right / no-handle all fail closed `-EACCES`. **What ring 3 gets, and ONLY that:** datagram send/recv on a UDP socket it created and bound — and nothing else. NO raw-frame access (the `raw_rx`/`raw_tx` Device accessors are kernel-only, `cfg`-gated), NO interface configuration (IP/routes/neighbor cache are kernel-set at init), NO promiscuous receive (a socket receives only what smoltcp demuxes to its bound port). The peer address is DATA the kernel copies through the ring-3-window bound check (the `sys_write`/`sys_open` pointer discipline — the whole `msg = [ip[4]][port u16 LE][pad] + payload` is proven in-window before any deref; full `copy_from_user` is still deferred). **`recvfrom` cannot block the IF-masked handler:** it drives a BOUNDED poll pump and returns `-EAGAIN` when empty — a ring-3 caller can never wedge the core waiting on a datagram (the U3.5 preemptible-ring-3 DoS class stays closed). **A dying task's sockets are freed at teardown** (`clear_handle_row` → `smolnet::free_row_sockets` removes the smoltcp socket + reclaims its static buffers), so a reused address-space slot inherits no live socket. **Fully static** (no heap: the socket set, per-socket packet buffers, and device scratch are all BSS `&'static mut`), and the ~5-7 KiB smolnet frames stay off AP scheduler stacks (device scratch in BSS ⇒ only smoltcp's ~2 KiB poll frames touch the BSP-witness / IF-masked-syscall stacks). **Evidence** — a ring-3 fixture (`sock2-udp`) makes all four syscalls and completes an end-to-end datagram round-trip to slirp's DNS server (`10.0.2.3:53`), `:: SOCK-2: ring-3 udp round-trip — socket/bind/sendto OK, recvfrom returned a datagram FROM 10.0.2.3:53, socket teardown clean -> PASS ::`, plus a kernel-side witness `:: SOCK-2: smoltcp udp dns query 10.0.2.3:53 -> 64 bytes back — witness OK ::`; `UNAOS_SMOLNET=1 ./arroyo test 60` MISSION SUCCESS + both lines; knob-off `./arroyo test`/`test-arm` MISSION with NO SOCK-2 lines (all code `#[cfg(all(feature = "smolnet", target_arch = "x86_64"))]` — byte-identical both arches); `check` both arches on+off, zero new warnings. **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` + `smolnet.rs` + `drivers/e1000.rs` (additive) + `Cargo.toml` (`socket-udp`); `crates/net`/`fat.rs`/`sched.rs` untouched; **zero aarch64.** **Residuals (ledgered):** UDP only (TCP is SOCK-3); full `copy_from_user` deferred (the cheap window check holds today); the SOCK-1 frame-stealing residual persists cross-CPU (the ring-3 fixture runs on an AP while the BSP's hand-rolled `service_net` also drains the NIC), handled by the fixture's sendto+recvfrom retry loop — a bounded liveness cost, never a safety issue. **The socket value word is only `+1`-biased, with NO generation tag** (unlike the U11x file-id's packed `(gen << 32) | (idx+1)`): safe TODAY because sockets are never transferable (they mint `CAP_READ|CAP_WRITE`, no `CAP_GRANT`, so `SYS_CAP`/`SYS_XFER` cannot move one across rows), but **the moment a future arc makes a socket transferable this is a recycled-slot UAF** — a stale cross-row socket handle would rebind to whatever socket next takes the freed registry slot. SOCK-3 (or the transferable-socket arc) MUST pack a slot-generation into the value word first (the U11x fd discipline). **Metal-pending** (pure syscall logic + slirp witness; joins the accrued x86 attended-bench batch).
- [x] **TCP client sockets — ring 3 gets a byte stream (SOCK-3, 2026-07-12, QEMU-verified; metal-pending — x86-only, behind `UNAOS_SMOLNET`).** Three more syscalls give ring 3 a connected TCP byte stream on the same persistent smoltcp stack: `sys_connect = 44`, `sys_send = 45`, `sys_sock_recv = 46` (named so — `SYS_RECV = 14` is the capability-transfer inbox recv). A TCP socket is minted by `sys_socket` with `type = SOCK_STREAM(1)`; it is the **same `KIND_SOCKET` capability** enforced at the same `handle_resolve` CHECK — **connect + send require `CAP_WRITE`, recv requires `CAP_READ`**, so `SYS_CAP` GRANT still attenuates. **What stream reach adds over UDP, and ONLY that:** a ring-3 program can now active-open a TCP connection to a peer address it names and exchange a byte stream on it — and nothing else new. Still **NO raw-frame access, NO listen/accept (client only this arc), NO interface configuration, NO promiscuous receive**; the peer address is the same 8-byte `[ip[4]][port u16 LE][pad]` header the kernel copies through the ring-3-window bound check (connect: read-source `>= USER_BASE`; send: read-source; recv: write-dest `>= USER_BASE + PAGE_SIZE`, past the RO code page — the `sys_sendto`/`sys_recvfrom` disciplines). **A UDP handle routed to a stream syscall (or a TCP handle to a datagram syscall) fails closed `-EACCES`** on the `SockKind` registry tag, **before** smoltcp's typed accessor can panic — a kernel-integrity guard on a ring-3-chosen syscall/handle pairing. **Nothing blocks the IF-masked handler:** `connect` is non-blocking with a ring-3 poll model (`0` established / `-EINPROGRESS` still handshaking — ring 3 re-drives / `-ECONNREFUSED` reset), issuing the SYN only from state Closed and pumping a BOUNDED loop; `send`/`recv` drive bounded pumps and return `-EAGAIN` / `0`-EOF rather than waiting (the U3.5 preemptible-ring-3 DoS class stays closed). **The recycled-slot UAF the SOCK-2 review flagged is now CLOSED for all socket kinds:** the socket handle value word is the **gen-fenced** `(gen << 32) | (sid + 1)` (`sock_id_pack`), and `socket_id_of` validates the packed generation against the live registry (`smolnet::sock_valid`: present, owner-matched, generation-matched — the U11x `file_desc_validate` discipline). `SOCK_GEN[sid]` bumps on every free (`stack_close` / `free_row_sockets`), so a stale handle to a freed-and-reused slot resolves `-EACCES`, no rebind — making a future transferable-socket arc safe by construction. **No global-lock busy-wait:** every TCP pump (`tcp_pump_chunked`) releases the global `STACK` lock between `TCP_CHUNK`-sized chunks and re-validates the reg slot on re-acquire, so a cross-CPU socket syscall never spins on `STACK.lock()` for a full ~400 k-iteration pump, and a concurrent teardown is seen as the connection vanishing, never a UAF. **A dying task's TCP sockets are freed at teardown** exactly like UDP (`free_row_sockets` removes the smoltcp socket + reclaims its 2 KiB×2 static stream rings + bumps the gen). **Fully static** (the TCP stream ring buffers are BSS `&'static mut`, borrowed once when a slot is built). **Evidence** — a ring-3 fixture (`sock3-tcp`) makes all three stream syscalls and completes an end-to-end byte-stream round-trip to slirp's DNS resolver over **DNS-over-TCP** (a hermetic 3-way handshake + stream reply under the default slirp backend — no injector, no netdev change), `:: SOCK-3: ring-3 tcp round-trip — socket/connect/send OK, recv returned a byte stream FROM 10.0.2.3:53, socket teardown clean -> PASS ::`, plus a kernel-side witness `:: SOCK-3: smoltcp tcp connect 10.0.2.3:53 established, 64 bytes back — witness OK ::`; `UNAOS_SMOLNET=1 ./arroyo test 90` MISSION SUCCESS + both lines; knob-off `./arroyo test`/`test-arm` MISSION with NO SOCK-3 lines (all code `#[cfg(all(feature = "smolnet", target_arch = "x86_64"))]` — byte-identical both arches); `check` both arches on+off. **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` + `smolnet.rs` + `drivers/e1000.rs` (additive) + `Cargo.toml` (`socket-tcp`); `crates/net`/`fat.rs`/`sched.rs` untouched; **zero aarch64.** **Residuals (ledgered):** TCP **client** only (no listen/accept yet — SOCK-4); full `copy_from_user` still deferred (the cheap window check holds); the SOCK-1 cross-CPU frame-stealing residual persists (the AP fixture retries via the non-blocking poll model); sockets remain non-transferable (they mint `CAP_READ|CAP_WRITE`, no `CAP_GRANT`) but the gen fence is now IN PLACE so a transferable-socket arc no longer needs to add it. **Metal-pending** (pure syscall logic + slirp witness; SOCK has no metal leg — no wired NIC on any current board — so QEMU slirp is the honest gate).
- [x] **Transferable sockets — a socket capability moves across processes (SOCK-4 scope B, 2026-07-14, QEMU-verified; metal-pending — x86-only, behind `UNAOS_SMOLNET`).** A socket cap is now movable to another principal, the socket analogue of the U7x/U8x console-cap transfer — with **no new syscall** (a socket rides the existing `SYS_XFER`(13)/`SYS_RECV`(14) inbox machinery, which already special-cased `KIND_SOCKET`). Two changes make it real, and safe: **(1) `sys_socket` now mints `CAP_READ|CAP_WRITE|CAP_GRANT`.** SOCK-2/3 minted no `CAP_GRANT`, so `SYS_XFER` (which demands `CAP_GRANT` on the source) could never move a socket; the mint endows it because a socket is the owning process's own resource and `CAP_GRANT` cannot be self-added later (rights only attenuate). Send still needs `CAP_WRITE`, recv `CAP_READ`, so a transfer/grant still **attenuates** to send-only/recv-only, and a transfer that drops `CAP_GRANT` (single-level) keeps the grantee from re-delegating. **(2) `sys_recv` MIGRATES socket ownership.** The persistent registry slot records an owner row, and `sock_valid` requires `owner == caller_row`; a transferred handle carries the same gen-fenced value word but its registry owner is still the grantor's row, so without migration it would fail `sock_valid` at the grantee (`-EACCES`). When `sys_recv` installs a received `KIND_SOCKET` cap it calls `smolnet::reassign_owner(sid, gen, new_row)` (`xfer_socket_migrate`): under the `STACK` lock, iff slot `sid` is still present at the SAME generation, ownership moves to the receiving row — only the owner field changes, the smoltcp socket + its buffers are untouched (a MOVE, not a re-open; a bound port / in-flight connection survives). **Single-owner, gen-fenced, safe by construction:** a socket has exactly ONE owner at any instant — after the move the grantor's original handle is owner-mismatched (`sys_send`/`recv` through it is `-EACCES`, the **cross-row stale-handle rejection**), `free_row_sockets` frees a socket only for its current owner (the grantee's exit reclaims it; the grantor's exit reclaims nothing), and once the owner frees the slot the SOCK-3 **generation fence** (`SOCK_GEN[sid]` bump) makes a stale old-gen handle `-EACCES` against BOTH the freed slot and whatever socket first-fit-reuses it — **no rebind** (the U11x `file_desc_validate` discipline, socket edition). A stale deposit (socket freed+reused between XFER and RECV) fails `reassign_owner`'s gen check, so the received handle is dead-on-arrival rather than stealing a different tenant's socket. **The SOCK-2 review's explicit warning — "the moment a future arc makes a socket transferable this is a recycled-slot UAF" — is thereby DISCHARGED:** the gen fence SOCK-3 pre-installed + this arc's owner migration close it by construction. **Evidence** — a two-fixture ring-3 demo (`sock4-grantor`/`sock4-grantee`, the U7x idiom on dedicated APs): the grantor mints a UDP socket, proves cross-process attenuation (an over-rights `SYS_XFER` is `-EACCES`) and transfers it (dropping `CAP_GRANT`); the grantee `SYS_RECV`s it and completes a datagram round-trip to slirp's resolver **on the moved socket** (`bind`/`sendto`/`recvfrom` FROM `10.0.2.3:53`); then the grantor's post-transfer `SYS_SENDTO` through its migrated-away handle is `-EACCES`; single-writer snapshot + teardown-clear hold. Plus a kernel-side check (`sock4_kernel_check`) that folds the **U11x gen-rebind proof** (grantee frees, a fresh socket reuses the slot at the new gen, the old-gen handle stays `-EACCES`). `:: SOCK-4: transferable sockets — grantee received + round-tripped the moved socket, grantor's migrated-away handle -EACCES, gen-rebind rejected, teardown clean -> PASS ::`; `UNAOS_SMOLNET=1 ./arroyo test 90` MISSION SUCCESS + the line (3/3 deterministic); knob-off `./arroyo test`/`test-arm` MISSION with NO SOCK-4 lines (all code `#[cfg(all(feature = "smolnet", target_arch = "x86_64"))]` — byte-identical both arches); `check` both arches on+off. **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` + `smolnet.rs`; `crates/net`/`fat.rs`/`sched.rs`/`Cargo.toml` untouched (reuses the existing socket features); **zero aarch64.** **Residuals (ledgered):** it is a MOVE not a copy (the safe choice for a stateful owner-scoped resource); a double transfer is last-recv-wins (no UAF/leak — single owner throughout); single-level (a transferred socket drops `CAP_GRANT`, no re-delegation — socket revocation trees deferred); the demo round-trips UDP but a TCP socket transfers identically (the kind-agnostic kernel check covers it). **Metal-pending** (pure syscall logic + slirp witness; SOCK has no metal leg — QEMU slirp is the honest gate).
- [x] **DHCP via smoltcp — the persistent stack leases its own address (SOCK-5 scope B, 2026-07-14, QEMU-verified; metal-pending — x86-only, behind `UNAOS_SMOLNET`).** Knob-on, the persistent smoltcp interface now obtains its IPv4 address with its **own** `dhcpv4::Socket` instead of copying the static lease the hand-rolled `crates/net` DHCP set (which `hw_addr` returns). **This adds NO new syscall (next free stays 26) and NO new ring-3 surface** — DHCP is kernel-internal interface configuration, so the SOCK-2 invariant "ring 3 gets NO interface configuration (IP/routes/neighbor cache are kernel-set at init)" is **preserved, not weakened**: ring 3 still cannot set an address, add a route, or drive the DHCP socket; it is a kernel socket in a reserved storage slot (`NSOCK+1`) that **never appears in `reg`**, so no `sys_socket`/`handle_resolve` path can ever resolve to it, and no `stack_open*`/`free_row_sockets` touches it. The acquisition (`configure_via_dhcp`) is a **bounded, clock-free poll pump** run once at first stack build from a large-stack context (never a ring-3 syscall stack), and it **falls back to the static `hw_addr` lease** on a silent server so the interface is never left unconfigured (SOCK-1/2/3/4 never regress). Fully static/BSS (the DHCP socket's storage is smoltcp-internal fixed; no heap). **Evidence** — a kernel witness `:: SOCK-5: smoltcp dhcpv4 lease 10.0.2.20/24 gw 10.0.2.2 — witness OK ::` (the leased `.20` ≠ the `.15` static default the interface used to hard-code, so the address demonstrably came from smoltcp's DHCP), and the SOCK-2/3/4 round-trips all still PASS on that address; `UNAOS_SMOLNET=1 ./arroyo test 90` MISSION SUCCESS + the SOCK-5 line, SOCK-1/2/3/4 intact; knob-off `./arroyo test`/`test-arm` MISSION with NO SOCK-5 line (all code `#[cfg(all(feature = "smolnet", target_arch = "x86_64"))]` — byte-identical both arches); `check` both arches on+off. **Lane:** `smolnet.rs` + `Cargo.toml` (`socket-dhcpv4`) + docs; `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs`/`drivers/e1000.rs`/`crates/net`/`fat.rs`/`sched.rs` untouched; **zero aarch64.** **Residuals (ledgered):** one-shot acquisition, no lease renewal (adequate under slirp's effectively-infinite lease; renewal is a future `service_net`-pump arc); the hand-rolled DHCP still runs in the driver (live stack knob-off, and knob-on still leases the driver's own `hw_addr`). **Metal-pending** (no wired NIC on any current board — QEMU slirp is the honest gate).
- [x] **TCP server / listen sockets — ring 3 accepts inbound TCP (SOCK-6 scope A, 2026-07-14, QEMU-verified; metal-pending — x86-only, behind `UNAOS_SMOLNET`).** Two syscalls give ring 3 the **server** side of TCP over the same persistent smoltcp stack: `sys_listen = 47` (arm a passive listener) and `sys_accept = 48` (poll for an inbound connection). **This is the first ring-3 surface that ACCEPTS inbound TCP** — the threat delta over SOCK-3's client-only reach is exactly "a ring-3 program can now listen on a port it names and accept a connection a remote peer opens to it, and nothing else new." Still **NO raw-frame access, NO interface configuration, NO promiscuous receive**; a listener is one `KIND_SOCKET` capability enforced at the same `handle_resolve` CHECK — **`listen` requires `CAP_WRITE`** (arming a listener is a configuring authority, like `bind`/`connect`), **`accept` requires `CAP_READ`** (accepting an inbound connection is a receiving authority), so `SYS_CAP` GRANT still attenuates. **Nothing blocks the IF-masked handler:** `accept` is non-blocking with the same ring-3 poll model as `connect` (`-EAGAIN` = no connection yet, ring 3 re-drives; `-EINVAL` = not armed for listen), pumping a BOUNDED loop (`ACCEPT_PUMP`) that releases the global `STACK` lock between chunks (`tcp_pump_chunked`) and re-validates the reg slot — no cross-CPU busy-wait, a concurrent teardown seen as the connection vanishing, never a UAF. **The accepted connection is gen-fenced and single-owner like every socket:** smoltcp's listener *becomes* the connection in place, so `accept` mints a **fresh `KIND_SOCKET` handle aliasing the same gen-fenced socket-id** (`sock_id_pack`; the SOCK-4 multi-handle-to-one-socket pattern) whose rights are **DERIVED, not minted: `CAP_READ|CAP_WRITE|CAP_GRANT` INTERSECTED with the listener handle's current rights** (security-lens fix at landing: an ATTENUATED listener — e.g. a `CAP_READ`-only SOCK-4 grantee — must not amplify itself a full-rights connection; a full-rights owner still gets the POSIX-like full handle) — so the accepted connection is itself transferable (accept→`SYS_XFER`→handler, inetd-style), and a later `sys_close` on it bumps `SOCK_GEN[sid]`, making any stale alias `-EACCES` (no rebind). **A UDP handle routed to `listen`/`accept` fails closed `-EINVAL`/`-EACCES`** on the `SockKind` tag before smoltcp's typed accessor can panic. **Fully static** (reuses the SOCK-3 TCP stream rings;
