no new BSS, no heap). **Evidence** — because slirp's NAT will not open a connection INTO the guest, the server witness uses the `UNAOS_NET=socket` builder mode SOCK-1 already wired (**no builder change**): `scripts/net-inject.py sock6` active-opens to the guest's listener, and the stateful kernel witness `smolnet::witness_tick6()` accepts + echoes the probe — `:: SOCK-6: smoltcp tcp accept :8080 — received 11 bytes, echoed 11 back — witness OK ::` (confirmed on two runs). Hermetic `UNAOS_SMOLNET=1 ./arroyo test 90` prints the honest `witness PENDING` note (no inbound peer under default slirp), MISSION SUCCESS, SOCK-1/2/3/5 intact; knob-off `./arroyo test`/`test-arm` MISSION with NO SOCK-6 line (all code `#[cfg(all(feature = "smolnet", target_arch = "x86_64"))]` — byte-identical both arches); `check` both arches on+off. **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` + `smolnet.rs` + `drivers/e1000.rs` (one witness call) + `scripts/net-inject.py` + docs; `crates/net`/`fat.rs`/`sched.rs`/`builder`/`Cargo.toml` untouched; **zero aarch64.** **Residuals (ledgered):** single-accept-per-listen (smoltcp's listener becomes the connection; a persistent-listener acceptor pool needs decoupling the per-slot stream buffers from the registry slot — deferred); the two syscalls are the delivered ring-3 surface but the witness is a stateful BSP-loop poll wrapping the same seam, not a dedicated ring-3 accept fixture (deferred); full `copy_from_user` still deferred. **Metal-pending** (no wired NIC on any current board — QEMU slirp / the socket-netdev injector is the honest gate).
- [x] **Persistent-listener acceptor pool — the listener survives accepts (SOCK-7, 2026-07-15, QEMU-verified; metal-pending — x86-only, behind `UNAOS_SMOLNET`).** SOCK-6's listener was **consumed** by its accept (smoltcp's listener *becomes* the connection in place); SOCK-7 makes `sys_listen` arm a **persistent** listener so each `sys_accept` hands back a fresh connection and the port **keeps listening**. **NO new syscall (26/27 unchanged, next free stays 28) and NO new ring-3 surface** — the change is entirely behind the existing `sys_accept`, so **the threat model is UNCHANGED from SOCK-6**: still one `CAP_WRITE`-gated `listen` + one `CAP_READ`-gated `accept`, no raw frames, no interface configuration, no promiscuous receive. **The new standing surface is exactly "a listening port now stays open across connections instead of needing a fresh `listen` per accept"** — a convenience over SOCK-6's single-shot listener, not a new authority; a ring-3 program could already re-`listen` to accept repeatedly. **Each accepted connection stays gen-fenced and single-owner**, and the rights derivation is unchanged (the SOCK-4 intersection: minted rights = `CAP_READ|CAP_WRITE|CAP_GRANT` ∩ the listener handle's current rights, so an attenuated listener cannot amplify). The mechanism (design shape (i)) decouples the per-slot TCP stream buffers from the reg-slot index via a **free-list** (`SmolStack.tcp_buf_used`, `SockKind::Tcp(buf)`): on accept, `peel_and_rearm` (one `STACK`-lock hold, no pump) **peels** the established socket — keeping the buffer set it owns — into a **fresh reg slot** (the caller's connection, its own gen-fenced socket-id), and **re-arms** the original listener slot in place with a new socket on a fresh buffer set, **keeping the listener slot's socket-id AND generation** so the caller's listener handle stays valid across unbounded accepts. **Bounded by `NSOCK`, fails safe:** the invariant *used buffer sets = live TCP sockets ≤ used reg slots* guarantees a free buffer whenever a slot is free (peel never starves); when **neither** is free the completed handshake stays buffered in the listener and is peeled by a later accept (`-EAGAIN`) — never lost, listener never consumed. **No new BSS, no heap** (reuses the `NSOCK` SOCK-3 stream-buffer sets). **Evidence** — `scripts/net-inject.py sock7` drives **two sequential** inbound connections to `:8080`; the extended `smolnet::witness_tick6()` accepts+echoes the first (`:: SOCK-6: … accept :8080 — received 11 bytes, echoed 11 back — witness OK ::`) and, the listener having survived, accepts+echoes the second (`:: SOCK-7: … accept :8080 #2 — received 12 bytes, echoed 12 back on a PERSISTENT listener … — witness OK ::`); the injector prints `GUEST SOCK-6 ACCEPT OK` then `GUEST SOCK-7 PERSISTENT LISTENER OK`. Hermetic `UNAOS_SMOLNET=1 ./arroyo test 90` prints both honest PENDING lines, MISSION SUCCESS, SOCK-1/2/3/5 intact; knob-off `./arroyo test 25` / `test-arm 22` MISSION with NO SOCK-7 line (all code `#[cfg(all(feature = "smolnet", target_arch = "x86_64"))]` — byte-identical both arches); `check` both arches on+off. **Lane:** `smolnet.rs` + `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` (the `sys_accept` seam) + `scripts/net-inject.py` + docs; `drivers/e1000.rs`/`crates/net`/`fat.rs`/`sched.rs`/`builder`/`Cargo.toml` untouched; **zero aarch64.** **Residuals (ledgered):** the persistent listener holds one reg slot + buffer set for its lifetime (`NSOCK − 1` left for connections — a sizing change, not a design one); back-pressure buffers-in-listener rather than a separate accept backlog queue; the witness is a stateful BSP-loop poll, not a dedicated ring-3 persistent-accept fixture (deferred); full `copy_from_user` still deferred. **Metal-pending** (no wired NIC on any current board — QEMU slirp / the socket-netdev injector is the honest gate).
- [x] **A ring-3 service now ANSWERS network queries — the DNS resolver / sinkhole (SINKHOLE-1 "zeolite", 2026-07-15, QEMU-verified; metal-pending — x86-only, behind `UNAOS_SMOLNET`).** The first ring-3 program that BINDS a well-known port and answers unsolicited inbound traffic: a DNS resolver (`zeolite-resolver`, the sock2 inline-blob idiom) that binds UDP `:53`, answers BLOCKED names with `0.0.0.0`, and forwards the rest to the upstream resolver. **NO new syscall (next free stays 28) and NO new ring-3 surface** — the resolver is composed *entirely* from already-landed, already-audited capabilities, and the point of the arc is that the composition is **capability-scoped by construction**: it holds ONLY its **two UDP sockets** (a serve socket bound `:53`, a transient upstream socket, each a gen-fenced single-owner `KIND_SOCKET` cap) and **one read-only blocklist descriptor** (a `File` handle carrying `CAP_READ`, opened via the STOR-1 S7 dynamic path and `sys_close`d after the read). It has **no raw-frame access, no interface configuration, no promiscuous receive, no write capability to anything** — a compromised resolver can send/recv datagrams on its two sockets and re-read its blocklist, and nothing else; a dying resolver's sockets + descriptor are reclaimed at teardown like any other. **The blocklist is DATA on the FAT volume** (`BLOCK.TXT`, read RO via `sys_open`→`open_dynamic_ondisk`→`sys_read` — STOR feeds NET), with a builtin fallback (+ honest marker) when no FAT is mounted. **The security-critical surface is the HOSTILE-payload parser** (`zdns_parse_and_match`, ring 3 — the first UnaOS code to parse an attacker-controlled wire payload): every field access is bounds-checked against the packet end *before* the read; any label-length byte with EITHER high bit set is REFUSED, which rejects DNS **compression pointers** (`0b11…`) and the reserved `0b01`/`0b10` forms alike — **so no pointer is ever followed and no compression loop can exist**; labels are capped (≤ 63) and the assembled name capped (≤ 250, well under its 256-byte buffer); no attacker-controlled length feeds any arithmetic; and any malformed packet is rejected without a crash (the resolver declines to sinkhole/forward it — fail-`malformed`, the conservative choice). The `0.0.0.0` response builder writes a fixed 16-byte answer, no length from the wire. **`recvfrom` on `:53` cannot block the IF-masked handler** (bounded pump, `-EAGAIN`) — the serve loop is a bounded window, so a ring-3 resolver can never wedge the core. **Evidence** — hermetic `UNAOS_SMOLNET=1 ./arroyo test 90` runs the parser on two self-test queries and proves the sinkhole decision + `0.0.0.0` answer build + the forward round-trip: `:: zeolite: blocklist from BLOCK.TXT via S7 dynamic-open, blocked ADS.EXAMPLE -> 0.0.0.0 (answer built), forwarded una.os -> 10.0.2.3:53 real answer relayed — witness OK ::` (fully under `+IRQSTORAGE +FATIMG`; `builtin list (no FAT)` hermetically), plus the STOR chain 0 FAIL + S8-write intact on the composition run; the over-the-wire sinkhole is driven by `scripts/net-inject.py dns` under `UNAOS_NET=socket` (guest-side proven: `:: zeolite: served an inbound query on :53 — blocked name sinkholed to 0.0.0.0 over the wire — witness OK ::`; the committed hermetic gate carries the honest PENDING line, as SOCK-6/7). Knob-off `./arroyo test 25` / `test-arm 22` MISSION with NO zeolite line (all code `#[cfg(all(feature = "smolnet", target_arch = "x86_64"))]` — byte-identical both arches); `check` both arches on+off. **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` + `smolnet.rs` (a one-line SOCK-7 `peel_and_rearm` occupancy `debug_assert`) + `scripts/net-inject.py` (`dns` verb) + `scripts/make-fat-img.sh` (`BLOCK.TXT`) + docs; `crates/net`/`fat.rs`/`sched.rs`/`builder`/`Cargo.toml` untouched; **zero aarch64.** **Residuals (ledgered):** single in-flight forward, no cache, no query log, single-question/A-record only; medium (a) same-stack loopback ruled out (smoltcp has no loopback), so the serve leg's committed proof is the injector medium; `copy_from_user` still deferred. **Metal-pending** (no wired NIC on any current board — QEMU slirp / the socket-netdev injector is the honest gate).
- [x] **The resolver now parses a HOSTILE FILE, not just a hostile packet — real blocklist ingest + subdomain matching + metrics (ZEOLITE-2 "zeolite", 2026-07-17, QEMU-verified; metal-pending — x86-only, behind `UNAOS_SMOLNET`).** ZEOLITE-2 makes the SINKHOLE-1 resolver's list handling real without a new syscall, a new ring-3 surface, or any kernel-net change (all still `#[cfg(all(feature = "smolnet", target_arch = "x86_64"))]`; aarch64 + knob-off byte-identical). **The security-relevant addition is a SECOND hostile-input surface: the blocklist FILE itself.** SINKHOLE-1 parsed only the wire packet; ZEOLITE-2's `BLOCK.TXT` is now **real hosts-file format** (`0.0.0.0 domain` / `127.0.0.1 domain`, `#`/`;` comments, blank lines), and `BLOCK.TXT` is untrusted input exactly like a packet (it comes off the FAT volume). The hosts-format line parser inside `zdns_parse_and_match` is therefore held to the **same bounds discipline as the packet parser**: every byte access is checked against `file_len` *before* the read, across the leading-whitespace skip, the field-1/field-2 scans, and the domain compare — so a truncated IP field, a line with no domain, a giant (2048-byte) line, an all-comment or CRLF-only file, or embedded control/NUL bytes can never read past the buffer or crash; a malformed line matches nothing and is skipped (fail-safe: a list the resolver cannot parse under-blocks, it does not crash or over-block). **Subdomain (label-boundary suffix) matching** blocks `www.ads.example` from a listed `ads.example` but NOT `notads.example` — the tail compare requires the byte before the match to be `.`, and the tail offset is `namelen − domainlen` with `domainlen ≤ namelen` enforced first, so the suffix logic reads no memory the exact-match logic did not (no new OOB surface). **Metrics** (queries seen / blocked / forwarded) are carried out with **no new syscall or data channel**: three counts saturate at 63 and pack into the spare high bits (`[10:28]`) of the single witness/exit word, clear of the decision flags — no attacker-controlled value is packed (the counts are the resolver's own tallies). **Evidence** — hermetic `UNAOS_SMOLNET=1 ./arroyo test 90` (builtin hosts-format list) and full composition `UNAOS_SMOLNET=1 UNAOS_IRQSTORAGE=1 UNAOS_FATIMG=sf ./arroyo test 200` (hosts-format `BLOCK.TXT` from FAT via S7) both prove ingest + suffix + metrics: `:: zeolite: hosts-format blocklist from BLOCK.TXT via S7 dynamic-open, blocked ADS.EXAMPLE -> 0.0.0.0 (answer built), subdomain WWW.ADS.EXAMPLE sinkholed + NOTADS.EXAMPLE not over-blocked, forwarded una.os -> 10.0.2.3:53 real answer relayed — witness OK ::` and `:: zeolite: metrics — 4 queries seen, 2 blocked (sinkholed), 1 forwarded upstream ::`; STOR chain 0 FAIL on the composition run. Knob-off `./arroyo test 40` / `test-arm 22` MISSION with NO zeolite line; `check` both arches on+off. **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` (the `zeolite-resolver` blob + launcher) + `scripts/make-fat-img.sh` (`BLOCK.TXT`) + docs; the packet-parser bounds, the compression-pointer refusal, and the `0.0.0.0` response builder are unchanged from SINKHOLE-1; `smolnet.rs`/`crates/net`/`fat.rs`/`sched.rs`/`builder`/`Cargo.toml` untouched; **zero aarch64.** **Residuals (ledgered):** cache, query log, and multi-in-flight forwarding remain future work (the appliance arcs); `copy_from_user` still deferred. **Metal-pending** (QEMU slirp is the honest gate).
- [x] **The saved ring-3 stack pointer is per-TASK, not per-CPU — cross-task user-stack aliasing closed (U4y, 2026-08-03, metal-observed defect; fix compiled + disassembly-verified, metal-pending).** The SYSCALL stub parked the entering task's user `rsp` in `PerCpuData::syscall_user_rsp` and reloaded it at `sysretq`. That slot is **per-CPU**, has **no setter anywhere in the tree** (`USER_RSP_OFFSET` had exactly one reference site — the stub itself), and the U4x dispatch-site install that re-arms CR3 / `TSS.RSP0` / `syscall_kernel_rsp` per task **did not re-arm the user twin**. A per-CPU slot was holding per-task state across a boundary a task can block on: `SYS_YIELD` context-switches from *inside* the syscall, and so does a futex wait. Consequence: **any task that blocked inside a syscall left its user `rsp` in the shared slot, and the next task to `sysret` on that core resumed on the blocked task's user stack** — two ring-3 tasks executing with aliased frames, their locals at identical offsets overwriting each other. This is a confidentiality *and* integrity defect between ring-3 tasks that share a core, independent of address space: it is not caught by W^X, by the NX map, or by the U1b fault-kill, because nothing about it is architecturally invalid — the CPU is doing exactly what it was told. **Observed on metal** (s68, rMBP): `VUG.ELF`'s worker A and its parent aliased at `0x10(%rsp)`; the parent's 32-bit `movl $0x0, 0x10(%rsp)` zeroed the low half of the worker's saved surface pointer, turning `USER_BASE+0x5000` into `USER_BASE` exactly, and the worker then wrote to its own read-only code page — `:: RING-3 FAULT: task 'user-thread' KILLED — vec=14 err=0x7 rip=0x1000000127d cr2=0x10000000000 ::`. **Note the detection accident:** the aliasing store happened to be 32-bit, which is the only reason the corrupted value landed on a recognisable constant instead of junk; the same bug with a 64-bit store presents as unattributable corruption. **Fix:** the user `rsp` is pushed onto the task's own kernel stack immediately after the stack switch and popped at the tail, so it lives on memory the scheduler preserves per task (`Box<[u8]>` owned by the `Task`, freed only at reap) instead of in shared per-CPU scratch. Two copies are pushed, not one — the entry `rsp` is 16-aligned and the pre-existing two pushes are what put `call {dispatch}` at `rsp ≡ 8 (mod 16)`; four pushes hold the SysV requirement, three would invert it silently until the first SSE spill. **Every existing protection verified intact in the emitted code**: the U1b B2 canonical-`rcx` guard still runs *before* any user-controlled `rsp` is loaded (`cmp rdx, rcx` / `jne 2f` precede `pop rsp`), so a non-canonical `rcx` still cannot reach `sysretq`; the U1b B1 six-register scrub is unmoved; the `swapgs` pairing is unchanged and the new pushes sit strictly between them; the non-canonical kill path's `call` is now ABI-aligned as well; `unaos_syscall_entry_end` still bounds exactly the stub with no slack, so `rip_in_entry_stub` needed no edit. **Evidence** — `llvm-objdump` walk of the linked stub with `rsp` tracked at every step (entry → `call` → `sysretq`), the alignment arithmetic stated per push, and `pop %rsp` semantics **measured on host silicon** rather than taken from the SDM's ambiguous POP pseudocode (the memory value is loaded, not `old_rsp+8`). Both gates green on both arches. **Lane:** `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` (the stub) + `arch/x86_64/percpu.rs` and `arch/x86_64/sched.rs` (doc only — the slot's "scratch slot" comment was the wrong mental model and is arguably why this survived). **aarch64 does not share the defect** and was audited by the pi4 seat rather than assumed: `__vec_svc` banks ELR/SPSR/SP_EL0 onto the task's own kernel stack at entry and restores from that frame before `eret`, and its entry comment names this exact hazard as the reason ("per-core, and a blocking syscall would clobber them"); the IRQ path names the failure verbatim ("resume at EL0 with another task's user stack pointer in SP_EL0") and was fixed structurally at M6e. The two arches now hold the same invariant for the same stated reason. **✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-08-03** (rMBP, boot s69, trunk `2bdae79b`): zero `RING-3 FAULT` lines beyond the three intentional `u1b-*` fixtures — the `user-thread` kill at `err=0x7 cr2=0x10000000000` that boot s68 produced reproducibly is gone — and `WINX-8` went from `presents=1 … FAIL` to `:: WINX-8: VUG.ELF end-to-end — loaded + windowed + 3 presents with 2 ring-3 thread(s), killed + reaped, teardown clean -> PASS ::`. The two ring-3 threads now run a full present loop on their own stacks. **Verification note worth keeping:** the s68 and s69 boots share one capture file, so the s68 tail contains the *old* fault line; the confirmation is that no fault appears after the s69 boundary, not that the string is absent from the file. A whole-file grep would have read as a failure.

### aarch64
- [x] EL0/EL1 privilege split (M6a-0/M6a, 2026-07-01, metal-confirmed)
- [x] Per-page user permissions: user code EL0-RX, stacks RW-XN; UXN/PXN on user pages (M6b)
- [x] EL0 fault → task kill with syndrome-matched accounting (M6b)
- [x] Preemptible EL0 — SP_EL0 banked in the IRQ frame; `spawn_user` starts I-unmasked (M6e, 2026-07-02, QEMU-verified + **metal-confirmed** real Pi 4: `IRQs-taken-at-EL0=18`, spinner resumed correctly, no faults). W^X map **unchanged**: the shared user stack is retained (no EL0 program writes its stack), so no new user page and no permission change — the audit table below still holds.
- [x] Kernel W^X / WXN audit (SCTLR_EL1.WXN feasibility) — audit complete; WXN enable pending review (M6c/H1; table below)
- [ ] PAN (Privileged Access Never) where silicon supports it
- [x] Validated user-pointer access — `copy_from_user`/`copy_to_user` (M6f, 2026-07-02, QEMU-verified; ★ metal-confirmed real Pi 4 2026-07-04 on the M6g reflash) — every user pointer crossing the syscall boundary is range-checked before any deref: `copy_from_user` requires `[va, va+len)` fully inside the caller's EL0 window with a non-wrapping length (`checked_add`); `copy_to_user` additionally excludes the read-only code page from its writable range, so a write aimed there is refused with `-EFAULT` **before** the store (never the faulting EL1 write a code-page target would otherwise take). SYS_WRITE now streams through `copy_from_user` (validate-whole-range-then-chunk, so a bad pointer produces `-EFAULT` with no partial output). A bad pointer is an **error return, not a task-kill** (kills stay for hardware-raised faults). Evidence: the M6f hostile fixture issues four bad pointers — `sys_write`(kernel RAM VA), `sys_write`(unmapped window-adjacent VA), `sys_write`(wrapping length), `sys_getinfo`(RO code-page target) — and the kernel refused all four with `-EFAULT` and **0 kills, 0 kernel halts** (`:: M6f: 4 hostile pointers refused (EFAULT), 0 kills -> PASS ::`), while the well-behaved `sys_getinfo` round-trip (`copy_to_user` then EL0 read-back matching `SYS_GETPID`) passed. Scope is honest: A72 is Armv8.0 with **no FEAT_PAN**, so this is pure software validation — the direct EL1 access is sound only because a syscall executes with the caller's TTBR0/ASID live (M6d), so an in-window VA can reach only that task's frames; a PAN-capable port (Jetson A78) must switch to an unprivileged (LDTR) or validated-copy path. **Metal-confirmed (real Pi 4, 2026-07-04):** all four hostile pointers refused with 0 kills / 0 kernel halts and the getinfo `copy_to_user` round-trip passed on silicon (`:: M6f: 4 hostile pointers refused (EFAULT), 0 kills -> PASS ::`, `getinfo/copy_to_user round-trip -> PASS`).
- [x] Per-task address-space isolation via ASIDs (M6d, 2026-07-02, QEMU-verified + **★ metal-confirmed** real Pi 4) — each EL0 task runs in its own translation-table branch with its own 16 KiB backing mapped at the same VAs; user-window leaves are non-global (`nG=1`, ASID-tagged 1..8), kernel leaves stay Global; `TTBR0_EL1` is switched (root + ASID) on dispatch and the slot is broadcast-`TLBI ASIDE1IS`'d and repointed off at exit. A task can no longer see or corrupt another task's user memory at a shared VA. **Metal proof (2026-07-02, EL=1/54 MHz — the class of thing QEMU cannot test):** `same-VA isolation A=0xa5a5…1 B=0x5a5a…2 distinct -> PASS` on real A72 TLB/caches (backed by a deterministic kernel-side TTBR0-swap `nG` probe), EL0 stack write/readback + SP-sentinel `-> PASS`, and with EL0 preemption live in the same boot (aggregate `IRQs-taken-at-EL0=21`, QEMU=0 — a demo-wide counter with no per-task attribution) all four slot tasks reported correct sentinel/stack values across interleaved per-task `TTBR0`/ASID dispatches; M6b `exited=1 killed=3 -> PASS` unchanged, 0 unexpected faults.
- [x] EL0 first-entry GPR scrub (M6d, 2026-07-02) — `user_task_trampoline` zeroes x0–x30 before the first `eret` to EL0, so no live kernel value (the raw `Task` pointer, kernel x29/x30, ...) reaches EL0 **in the general-purpose file** at entry. The FP/SIMD file is NOT yet covered (next item). The aarch64 twin of the x86 SYSRET GPR scrub (merged with U1b).
- [x] Loadable program from storage — the untrusted-loader path (M6g, 2026-07-03, QEMU-verified; ★ metal-confirmed real Pi 4 2026-07-04) — the block layer dispatches over a registered backend so a read-only BCM2711 EMMC2/SDHCI microSD driver (PIO, single-block CMD17, polled, no writes; every wait CNTPCT-bounded) sits beside the untouched xHCI path (x86 read/write byte-identical — the seam is cfg-gated to `aarch64+baremetal`). The M6g loader reads `HELLO.BIN` off the card's FAT volume, **rejects it up-front from the on-disk directory size** if it does not fit one 4 KiB code page (the U2 truncation lesson: `read_file` caps at `min(size, cap)`, so a post-read check could never SEE an oversize file), copies it into a fresh M6d per-task slot, and protects the page EL0-RX/EL1-RO **before the task exists** (so the untrusted bytes are never writable-and-executable). **The loaded bytes are UNTRUSTED and bounded ONLY by size** — contained by EL0 + per-page perms + the M6b fault-kill net, exactly the x86 U2 posture; there is **no signature or allowlist gate yet** (see the shared code-signing item below). Same residual DoS as U2/x86: a program that never `svc`s can spin its EL0 core (M6e makes EL0 preemptible on metal, so the timer *can* evict it there; QEMU delivers no timer IRQ). QEMU proves the seam, the whole SDHCI ladder against the legacy base, FAT mount off SD, and the EL0 load + run + name-routed exit accounting; the EMMC2 base actually carrying the card is metal-only — and it **ran on silicon (2026-07-04):** no fallback line, `SD card @0xfe340000 identified — 31116288 blocks (15193 MiB, CSD v2)` (a real ~16 GB SDHC card, block-addressed — vs QEMU's 64 MiB/CSD v1 legacy fallback), then FAT mount + `HELLO.BIN loaded from SD (51 bytes) -> EL0` + `disk-loaded EL0 program exited ok -> PASS`, with 0 unexpected faults.
- [x] Minimal process model — `sys_spawn` + `sys_wait` (M7, 2026-07-04, QEMU-verified; ★ metal-confirmed real Pi 4 2026-07-04) — an EL0 program can spawn a child program (`SYS_SPAWN = 8` loads the fixed on-disk `HELLO.BIN` into a fresh per-task slot and runs it at EL0, returning its pid) and reap it (`SYS_WAIT = 9` blocks on a per-child counting `Semaphore` the child posts once — on its normal exit **or** on a fault-kill, so a killed child can never hang its parent). **The security scope is deliberately narrow and stated honestly: there is NO capability or permission check on spawn yet** — any EL0 program that reaches `SYS_SPAWN` gets a child. What contains the child is the SAME chain as every prior EL0 program, nothing weaker: it runs on its own fresh slot (own ASID, per-page EL0-RX/EL1-RO code protected *before the task exists*), **inherits nothing** from the parent (no shared memory, no shared handles — there is no handle table yet), and is bounded by EL0 + per-page perms + the M6b fault-kill net. The child bytes are the same size-bounded **untrusted** on-disk program as M6g (no signature/allowlist — the shared code-signing item below gates BOTH the child load and the M6g loader). Resource bounds are fixed, not grown under load: a 4-entry process table and the 8-slot pool both return `-EAGAIN`/`-errno` on exhaustion rather than expanding. Residual DoS is unchanged from M6g/U2 — a child that never `svc`s can spin its EL0 core (the timer evicts it on metal, M6e; QEMU delivers none). **`sys_spawn` takes no name/path argument** — the program is fixed to `HELLO.BIN`; arbitrary program-by-name is M8 and needs a validated `copy_from_user` name (a spawn argument is a new attack surface, held off deliberately). The capability model — *which* principals may spawn *what*, plus inheritance/grant semantics — is U5/U6. QEMU exercises the whole reap round-trip end to end (the waker is a scheduler post, not the timer): `:: M7: parent spawned child pid=<p>, waited, child exited status 0 -> PASS ::`, with every M6b/M6d/M6e/M6f/M6g verdict byte-identical (the child-exit accounting is routed by pid through the process table, never through the M6b/sentinel counters). **Metal-confirmed (real Pi 4, 2026-07-04):** `:: M7: parent spawned child pid=41, waited, child exited status 0 -> PASS ::` on silicon — the child loaded off the real card via the EMMC2-first path QEMU cannot exercise (`SD card @0xfe340000 — 15193 MiB, CSD v2`), and the parent's blocking `sys_wait` was woken by the child's scheduler post and reaped it under a live timer (EL0 preemption live, `IRQs-taken-at-EL0=23`), with 0 unexpected faults / 0 FAIL lines and the whole prior battery green (M6b `exited=1 killed=3 PASS`, M6d ×3, M6f ×3, M6g PASS, CAPSTONE 6/6, EL=1/54 MHz).
- [x] Per-process handle table + owner-scoped reaping (U4, 2026-07-04, QEMU-verified; no metal this arc — fully QEMU-verifiable scheduler/syscall logic) — evolves M7 into the ownership substrate. `SYS_SPAWN = 8` now installs the child into the *spawner's* per-process handle table and returns a small **handle index** (not the raw pid); `SYS_WAIT = 9` takes that **handle**. The table is a static, const-init `HANDLES[[AtomicU64; 8]; USER_SLOTS+1]` keyed by the caller's **ASID** (read from `TTBR0_EL1[63:48]` synchronously in the SVC handler — the caller's root is live there); `PROCS` stays keyed by pid (exit-accounting control blocks) while `HANDLES` is keyed by ASID (the spawner's private namespace of child capabilities). **Honest scope — this is STRUCTURE, not yet a CHECK:** the per-process handle table now holds child-process handles and **reaping is owner-scoped BY CONSTRUCTION** — a task can only reap children whose handles are in ITS table, so the M7 review note (any EL0 task could `sys_wait` any pid) is folded structurally — **but there is NO capability CHECK yet.** The capability model proper — handles-as-capabilities with grant/attenuate/revoke, routing resource syscalls (e.g. `sys_write`) through the table, and bandy handle-transfer — is **U5**, which adds its check at *exactly the handle lookup this arc builds*. `sys_write` is deliberately **left as the `fd==1` path** (routing it through the table now would force seeding a table for every existing EL0 writer for no capability benefit — a STOP tripwire). No new syscall number, no name/path argument to `sys_spawn` (that is still M8), no new scheduler primitive; the child bytes remain the same size-bounded **untrusted** on-disk program as M6g/M7 (no signature/allowlist — the shared code-signing item still gates the load). Resource bounds stay fixed (a 4-entry process table + the 8-slot pool return `-EAGAIN`; the 8-slot-per-process handle table likewise). Evidence — the demo proves the isolation the structure gives: a parent (`el0-u4parent`) `sys_spawn`s two children and reaps **both by handle**; an ownership negative (`el0-u4orphan`, its own slot/ASID) calls `sys_wait(0)` on an Empty handle in its OWN table and gets `-ECHILD` — same handle *index* 0, different tables, different outcomes: `:: U4: process model — parent reaped 2 children by handle, non-child sys_wait -ECHILD (per-process handle tables) -> PASS ::`, with every M6b/M6d/M6e/M6f/M6g verdict byte-identical (child-exit accounting stays pid-keyed through `PROCS`, never the handle or the sentinel counters).
- [x] **Handles as capabilities — the enforcement CHECK + grant/attenuate/revoke + routed `sys_write` + teardown-clear (U5, 2026-07-05, QEMU-verified; no metal this arc — pure syscall logic, and the child loads ride U4/M7's already-metal-confirmed EMMC2 path).** Turns U4's handle STRUCTURE into a real capability. A handle now carries **rights** (a `u8`/`u16`-scale bitmask `CAP_READ|CAP_WRITE|CAP_EXEC|CAP_GRANT|CAP_REVOKE`, held in a **sidecar** `HANDLE_RIGHTS[[AtomicU32; 8]; USER_SLOTS+1]` keyed identically to `HANDLES`, so U4's `0`/`RESERVING` value-word sentinel logic is byte-unperturbed) and names a **target** beyond "child pid" (a well-known `HANDLE_CONSOLE = u64::MAX-1` token — kept to two kinds, `CHILD(pid)` and `CONSOLE`, NOT a general object table, which is U6). **The CHECK** is a single `handle_resolve(asid, idx, req_rights)` at the one lookup point every handle-consuming path goes through: out-of-range/Empty ⇒ the caller's own errno (`sys_wait` → `-ECHILD`, preserving U4 structural ownership; `sys_write`/`SYS_CAP` → `-EACCES`), present-but-missing-a-right ⇒ `-EACCES`. **grant/attenuate/revoke** are first-class via `SYS_CAP = 10` (sub-op selector): GRANT mints a new handle to the same target carrying a rights mask that must be a **subset** of the granter's rights on the source (the **monotonic-decrease / attenuation invariant** — `req & !src_rights != 0` ⇒ `-EACCES`; a grant can never amplify), requiring `CAP_GRANT` on the source; REVOKE drops a handle the caller owns (`-EACCES`/`-ECHILD` on subsequent use). **`sys_write` now routes through the table** — `fd` is a handle index that must resolve to a `CONSOLE` handle with `CAP_WRITE`; there is no ambient stdout. Every EL0 process that prints is **endowed** a `CONSOLE`+`CAP_WRITE` cap at the conventional index `CONSOLE_FD = 1` at spawn/launch (the shared window ASID 0 for `el0-hello`; each M6f/M6g/U4-child slot); the `copy_from_user` validation and all-or-nothing `-EFAULT` semantics are byte-identical, so the M6f hostile fixture (which holds the cap) still gets `-EFAULT`, not `-EACCES`. **Teardown-clear** (folds U4's one deferred lifecycle note): `boot::teardown_user_slot` now wipes the whole `HANDLES[asid]` row + its rights **before** releasing the slot's used-flag (not after — a post-release clear could race a concurrent `alloc_user_slot` on another core that reclaims the same ASID), so no stale capability outlives its owning ASID. Evidence — the `el0-u5cap` fixture proves the four EL0-observable behaviours against its own table and the launcher proves the fifth kernel-side: `:: U5: capabilities — write-cap OK, no-cap -EACCES, attenuated grant bounded, revoke enforced, teardown-clear clean -> PASS ::`, with every M6b/M6d/M6e/M6f/M6g/U4 verdict byte-identical (all four prior `hello from EL0` prints land — their processes hold the endowed console cap — and CAPSTONE 6/6). Still deferred to **U6**: bandy handle-transfer between principals, a general object table (routing fs/net syscalls through handles), and cross-process revocation trees (`CAP_REVOKE` as a right is defined but reserved).
- [x] **General object table — `(kind, target, rights)` descriptors, first-free for ALL kinds, the `CONSOLE_FD` collision closed (U6a, 2026-07-06, QEMU-verified; no metal this arc — pure syscall logic, and the demo's children ride U4/M7's already-metal-confirmed EMMC2 load path).** Generalizes U5's fixed-shape handle into a general object descriptor. **The kind rides in a parallel sidecar** `HANDLE_KIND[[AtomicU8; 8]; USER_SLOTS+1]` (keyed identically to `HANDLES`/`HANDLE_RIGHTS`), so the value word keeps U4/U5's sentinels **byte-identical** — `0` = Empty (the lock-free allocator's free marker), `u64::MAX` = `RESERVING` (an in-flight claim) — and nothing else is reserved. A handle now names one of four kinds — `Child(pid)` (U4), `Console` (U5), and the **scaffolds** `File(id)` / `Socket(id)` (defined + resolvable via `handle_resolve`, though no fs/net syscall routes through them yet — they prove the table is genuinely general). The sidecar choice is deliberate: it makes the STOP-tripwire sentinel-collision reasoning *structurally impossible* (a `File`/`Socket` id may be any non-`0`/non-`u64::MAX` word with no high-bit masking), and mirrors the rights sidecar 1:1 (same publish-kind-and-rights-before-the-live-value / observe-after discipline; single-writer-per-ASID is the backstop). **The `CONSOLE_FD` collision is closed** — the raison d'être of the arc. U5 pinned the console cap at a fixed index `CONSOLE_FD = 1` via an unconditional store while `handle_install`'s first-free scan allocated children from index 0, so a process that both PRINTED and SPAWNED 2+ children could have a child auto-allocated onto index 1 and then clobbered by the console install (harmless only because no such process existed). U6 makes `CONSOLE_FD` a **reserved index the first-free allocator SKIPS**: the console lives there by the `fd=1`/stdout convention (keeping every prior blob's `sys_write(fd=1)` byte-identical), and auto-allocated child/object handles fill `{0, 2, 3, ..}` — so a console cap and N child/object caps coexist with **zero index collision, for any interleaving of installs** (rather than allocating the console through the shared allocator, which would break the stable stdout index). Every existing consumer is behaviour-preserved: `handle_resolve` dispatches on the kind sidecar (`sys_wait` requires `Child`, `sys_write` requires `Console`+`CAP_WRITE`, `sys_cap` grant/revoke on any kind); the **attenuation invariant is unchanged** and the mint now copies the source handle's kind (a grant attenuates rights, never re-kinds the object); `handle_clear`/`clear_handle_row`/`handle_row_is_clear` (the U5 teardown-clear) also clear/verify the kind. Evidence — the `el0-u6spawn` fixture is the printing spawner U5 could not serve: it prints, spawns 2 children (distinct auto-allocated handles, both off the reserved index), prints AGAIN (proving the console cap survived the spawns), and reaps both by handle; the launcher additionally proves kernel-side that the `File`/`Socket` kinds resolve to their kind with the required right (and `-EACCES`-equivalent without) and that the exact U5-breaking console-vs-two-children interleaving no longer collides: `:: U6: general object table — printing spawner + 2 children, no index collision, File/Socket kinds resolve -> PASS ::`, with every M6b/M6d/M6e/M6f/M6g/U4/U5 verdict **byte-identical** and CAPSTONE 6/6. **Honest scope — this is (a) the OBJECT TABLE only.** Deferred to **U7**: cross-process handle-transfer (`SYS_XFER` — a cross-ASID write discipline that breaks the single-writer invariant) + revocation trees (`CAP_REVOKE`, still reserved) + the bandy Ring-3 delegation wrapper; and to a later arc: real `File`/`Socket` fs/net syscalls routing through these kinds, plus UnaFS `owner`/`grants:*` enforcement on `SYS_OPEN` (rides the kernel UnaFS mount, K2/K3). Six-lens adversarial self-review (no-collision under every interleaving, kind sentinel-safety + ordering, attenuation, consumer preservation, demo correctness, holistic).
- [x] **Real `File` handles — `SYS_OPEN`/`SYS_READ` routed through the object table via `File` + `CAP_READ` (U6b, 2026-07-06, QEMU-verified + ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED on the real Pi 4 — the fixture opened `HELLO.BIN` and read it through a `File` capability off the real EMMC2/SD card, both `-EACCES` denials holding, full battery green).** Makes U6a's `File` scaffold real: the first resource syscall routed through a **non-Console** object, and the direct precursor to UnaFS grants. **`SYS_OPEN = 11`**`(name_ptr, name_len)` `copy_from_user`s the bounded 8.3 name, mounts the single **read-only** FAT volume, finds the top-level entry (a directory ⇒ `-EISDIR`), records a per-task **open-file descriptor**, and installs a `File` handle carrying `CAP_READ` (first-free, skipping the reserved `CONSOLE_FD`). **`SYS_READ = 12`**`(handle, buf, len)` is the enforcement point — `handle_resolve(asid, handle, CAP_READ)` must yield a `File`; a missing right (`Denied`), a non-File kind (`Console`/`Child`/`Socket`), or no handle (Empty/oob) **all return `-EACCES`**, the exact twin of `sys_write`'s Console+`CAP_WRITE`. It then clamps the request to `min(len, size − offset)`, validates the **whole** destination up front (`user_range_ok(buf, want, writable=true)` — the RO code page excluded — so a bad buffer is `-EFAULT` with no disk read and **no offset advance**), reads `want` bytes from the current offset via a new read-only **offset-aware** FAT reader (`fat::read_at`, walking the chain and skipping to `start`; `read_file` left byte-identical for its M6g/U4 caller), `copy_to_user`s, and advances the offset by exactly the count delivered (`0` = EOF; **sequential, no seek**). The descriptor lives in a small **per-task FILES table** — parallel atomic arrays `FILE_USED`/`FILE_CLUSTER`/`FILE_SIZE`/`FILE_OFFSET` keyed `[asid][idx]` (`NFILE = 4`), the same lock-free sidecar shape and Release-publish/Acquire-resolve discipline as `HANDLE_RIGHTS`/`HANDLE_KIND`; the `File` handle's value word carries the **file-id = descriptor index + 1** (the `+1` bias keeps it clear of the value word's `0`/`u64::MAX` sentinels, **structurally**, so a `File` id never aliases Empty/RESERVING). `sys_open` claims resources **last** and mirrors `sys_spawn`'s reserve/unwind: a full handle table after a descriptor was claimed frees the descriptor then returns `-EAGAIN`, so no failure path leaks a FILES slot or a handle. **Teardown-clear** extends U5's lifecycle rule to files — `clear_handle_row` (called from `boot::teardown_user_slot` before the slot's used-flag is released) now also clears the FILES row, so a reused ASID starts with no stale file, no leaked offset, no aliasable descriptor. Evidence — the `el0-u6bfile` fixture opens `HELLO.BIN`, reads its first bytes through the `File` capability and verifies they equal the kernel-planted on-disk prefix, then proves the CHECK denies both a **present File lacking `CAP_READ`** (the rights arm) and a **`Socket` carrying `CAP_READ`** (the kind arm) with `-EACCES` — witness `0x1F`; the launcher proves the file-row teardown-clear kernel-side: `:: U6b: real File handles — open+read via a File capability OK, no-CAP_READ -EACCES, wrong-kind -EACCES -> PASS ::`, with every M6b/M6d/M6e/M6f/M6g/U4/U5/U6 verdict **byte-identical** (the shared FAT mount does not regress M6g/U4) and CAPSTONE 6/6. **Honest scope:** read-only, flat root, one FAT volume — no write/create/delete, no seek, no directory ops, no second mount. Deferred: file writes/dirs/seek; real `Socket` fs/net syscalls (the fs twin); **UnaFS `owner`/`grants:*` checked on `SYS_OPEN`** (this arc's `SYS_OPEN` is the hook), riding the kernel UnaFS mount (K2/K3). Five-lens adversarial self-review (reserve/unwind + leaks, the SYS_READ CHECK for bypasses, offset/EOF + `read_at` correctness, teardown-clear + atomic ordering, regression + fixture ABI).
- [x] **Cross-process capability transfer — inbox-mediated `SYS_XFER`/`SYS_RECV` + sender-owned single-level revoke, the single-writer invariant preserved (U7, 2026-07-07, QEMU-verified; no metal expected — pure syscall logic; Campaign 1, Fable-executed).** The first CROSS-process operation on the object table — the desktop's delegation primitive (a shell handing an editor a console/file cap). **The invariant the design exists to preserve:** every `HANDLES[asid]` row keeps exactly ONE writer (U4's lock-free foundation). A sender therefore NEVER writes the recipient's row: **`SYS_XFER = 13`**`(dest, src, req_rights)` deposits an **attenuated** `(kind, target, rights)` descriptor into the recipient's per-ASID transfer **INBOX** (`XFER_SLOT_*`, `NXFER = 4` — the one deliberately cross-ASID surface, where every claim/consume/retract/teardown is a **tx-exact CAS** over a `0`/`RESERVING`/unique-transfer-id state word, with the publish-Release/observe-Acquire discipline of the handle sidecars), and **`SYS_RECV = 14`** pulls it into the CALLER's OWN row — the recipient writes only itself. Delegation is **owner-scoped**: `dest` must be a `Child` handle in the SENDER's own table (pid→ASID through a new `Proc.asid` field — no global process namespace); the source must carry **`CAP_GRANT`** (the same delegation right `sys_cap` GRANT demands); and **attenuation crosses processes intact** — `req & !src_rights != 0` ⇒ `-EACCES`, a transfer can never amplify. **Revocation is one-way too:** `SYS_CAP` **XREVOKE**`(transfer id)` flips a bit in the **sender-owned transfer RECORD** (`XFER_REC_*`, `MAX_XFERS = 8`, sender-only — `-EACCES` for anyone else; transfer ids are globally unique, so a stale id can never alias a new transfer); the recipient's next `handle_resolve` of the received cap reads that bit through a recipient-written sidecar (`HANDLE_XFER_REC`) and fails `Denied` — **stale at use** — while a still-PENDING revoked transfer is discarded at RECV, never delivered. Record lifetime = transfer lifetime: freed by exactly one of received-handle drop / pending-discard / sender retract / recipient teardown. A **post-check + retract** closes the deposit-vs-recipient-exit race from the sender's side (re-read the recipient's Proc entry after the deposit goes live; a tx-exact CAS undoes only our own deposit); the one residual TOCTOU (recipient exit + ASID recycle + new-tenant consume, all between the live-store and the post-check) is documented and accepted this arc — generation-tagged inboxes ride the revocation-tree arc. **Teardown** extends `clear_handle_row`: received-cap records are freed with their handles and the inbox swept (claim-to-clear per slot), so a recycled ASID inherits no stale pending cap and no dangling record. Payload kinds are **`Console`/`Socket` only** — `File` is refused (`-EACCES`: a file-id indexes the SENDER's per-ASID FILES row, so cross-ASID File transfer needs descriptor migration; deferred with writes/seek), `Child` is refused (delegating reap rights is a process-model question, not a transfer one). Deferred: revocation trees; the bandy Ring-3 delegation wrapper (a Ring-3 arc); arbitrary-recipient transfer; `File` payloads (descriptor migration); UnaFS `grants:*` on open (K2/K3). **Adversarial review (five lenses + refuter panels, 29 agents): 2 distinct CONFIRMED must-fixes, both fixed in-arc:** (1) the original XREVOKE was the arc's one non-tx-exact transition (find-then-store on a reusable record — a stale revoke could land on a freed-and-reclaimed record and kill an unrelated sender's fresh transfer, or mint one born-revoked); fixed by moving the revoked flag INTO the record's TX word (`XFER_REVOKED_BIT`, bit 63) so the revoke is a tx-exact CAS like every other transition, and the separate flag word (with its born-revoked window) no longer exists. Handles installed by spawn/open/endow are ROOTS; a root gets its node LAZILY at its first use as a grant/transfer source. **Revoke = mark one node; staleness is discovered at USE:** `handle_resolve` walks child → root (bounded by the ledger size; cycles impossible — a parent is claimed strictly before its child and edges never change) and fails `Denied` if ANY ancestor is revoked. This keeps U7's load-bearing invariant — **no revoke path ever writes another ASID's row** — with revoke O(1) and resolve paying the bounded walk. **`CAP_REVOKE` is real now:** `SYS_CAP` REVOKE of a handle CARRYING it marks its node before the row clear, killing the whole derivation subtree — every re-grant and every re-transfer whose chain passes through it, however deep, exactly once, idempotently (a second revoke of the same index is an honest `-ECHILD`); WITHOUT the right the drop keeps U5's ownership semantics (row-local; derived copies survive). **XREVOKE composes:** the transfer record captures its derivation node (+ the node's publish-time id — an ABA guard: a node dropped-and-reclaimed before the revoke lands no longer matches and is left alone; it had no children, so nothing escapes), and a won tx-exact XREVOKE marks it — so revoking the ROOT transfer kills the received cap AND everything the recipient re-granted or re-transferred onward (the U7 escape, closed); post-revoke laundering stays refused, now tree-deep (grant/xfer check ancestor staleness, not just the direct record). **Node lifetime (the documented choice):** a node frees when its owning handle/pending-slot drops AND it has no live children; dropped-with-children = a **TOMBSTONE** (still walkable, bit intact) until the subtree drains, then the free CASCADES up through drained tombstoned ancestors — every free is arbitrated by a state-exact CAS on the id word (racing freers resolve to one winner), and every walkable node is PINNED (a live handle pins its own node; a live child holds `KIDS > 0` on its parent), so no walk reads a reclaimed node. Ledger exhaustion is `-EAGAIN` at mint/transfer time with reserve/unwind on every failure path (the U4 discipline). **(2) Generation-tagged inboxes.** A per-ASID generation word (`ASID_GEN`) bumps at the TOP of `clear_handle_row` — strictly before the U7 inbox sweep — so it covers even a deposit that lands after the sweep passed its slot. `sys_xfer` stamps the recipient's current generation into the deposit; `sys_recv` delivers only on an exact match (a stale stamp = a deposit aimed at a PREVIOUS tenant: discarded, record freed, node dropped, never delivered); the sender's post-check re-reads the word and retracts on any change. The U7-documented residual TOCTOU (recipient exit + ASID recycle + new-tenant consume inside the deposit-live→post-check window) is now **structurally closed from both sides**. Evidence — the `el0-u8tree` fixture (single slot, register-only; witness `0xF`): a grant→re-grant chain carries authority pre-revoke; the parent revoke (with `CAP_REVOKE`) returns 0, its double revoke exactly `-ECHILD`, and BOTH descendant copies are `-EACCES` at their next use; a right-less revoke stays local (its derived copy still writes) with its own double-revoke errno. `u8_kernel_check` drives the REAL `sys_xfer_from`/`sys_recv_for` code paths (the syscall bodies, parameterized on ASID — no duplicate logic) over scratch ASID rows: an S→R1→R2 re-transfer chain where the root XREVOKE makes R1 AND R2 stale + refuses the laundering mint; a deposit stamped before the generation bump is never consumable by the recycled ASID's tenant (its record frees; a late XREVOKE gets `-ENOENT`); and afterwards the handle rows, inboxes, transfer records AND the derivation ledger are all provably clear (the teardown/tombstone-cascade proof): `:: U8: revocation trees — parent revoke kills re-grant + re-transfer, generation-tagged inbox, ledger clean -> PASS ::`, with every prior verdict **byte-identical** (sorted scratch-worktree baseline diff vs `225ae48` — only the U8 lines and the binary-growth VBAR/ELR shift differ), 19/19 prior PASS + U8, CAPSTONE 6/6, 0 faults; `./arroyo test-arm 22` byte-identical; `./arroyo check` both arches, **zero x86 files touched**. **Of U7's deferred list this closes exactly: revocation trees, generation-tagged inboxes, real `CAP_REVOKE` semantics.** **Still deferred:** the bandy Ring-3 delegation wrapper (a Ring-3 arc); arbitrary-recipient transfer; `File` payloads (descriptor migration, with writes/seek/dirs); real Socket net syscalls; UnaFS `grants:*` on `SYS_OPEN` (K2/K3); the x86 U7x/U8x twins. The U7 RCsc-codegen footnote is respected, not fixed: the new ledger keeps the same STLR/LDAR ordering discipline. **Post-merge hardening (`deriv_drop` SeqCst fence, both arches):** the tombstone/free handshake — DROPPED store + KIDS load (parent-drop side), KIDS fetch_sub + DROPPED load (child-drop side) — was a store-buffering/Dekker pair under Release/Acquire; a concurrent parent-vs-child drop could store-buffer both sides into declining the free, leaking a ledger node (fail-closed → `-EAGAIN`, no UAF). The four ops are promoted to `SeqCst` (one total order forbids the double-stale outcome; the both-free case stays arbitrated by the `DERIV_ID` CAS). Surfaced by the U8x pre-merge concurrency lens.

U9, CAPSTONE 6/6, 0 unexpected faults. **The first write the EMMC2 driver has ever issued to a card — the metal-risk write path is now proven on silicon.**).** Gives U6b's read-only `File`+`CAP_READ` its write half — the last rung before UnaFS `grants:*` on open. **The mechanism, bottom-up.** (1) **EMMC2 sector write** — `emmc2::write_block_512` is the exact mirror of the polled CMD17 read path with three deltas: `WRITE_SINGLE_BLOCK` (`cmd(24)`, host→card so `CMD_DAT_DIR_READ` is OMITTED), the Buffer-**Write**-Ready status bit (`INT_WRITE_RDY = 1<<4`, distinct from the read path's bit 5), and a FIFO that PUSHES 128 LE words (a short buffer zero-pads to the full 512-byte block — the controller demands exactly one sector or it stalls); same `send_command`, same bounded CNTPCT deadlines, same W1C/error discipline. A chain that ends before `size` yields a short write, never a write outside the chain. `sys_write`'s only gate is `handle_resolve`, so proving that resolve `Err`s IS the `-EACCES`): `:: U9: real File writes — open-RW+seek+write+readback OK, RO-write/wrong-kind/revoked-cap all -EACCES, on-disk sector changed + size unchanged -> PASS ::`, with every prior verdict **byte-identical** (sorted scratch-worktree baseline diff vs `e8db35c` — only the U9 lines + the binary-growth VBAR/ELR shift differ), all prior PASS + U9, CAPSTONE 6/6, 0 faults; directory mutation; write-back caching (every write is a synchronous RMW straight to the card); and **UnaFS `owner`/`grants:*` enforcement on `SYS_OPEN`** (this arc's RW mode bit is the local precursor;
