- [x] **UnaFS owner/grants — the by-NAME namespace ACL, enforced at `SYS_OPEN` (U6, 2026-07-08, QEMU-verified; ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-10 attended bench — pure syscall + in-kernel metadata, rides the next Pi 4 boundary).** Closes the LAST documented capability gap: `SYS_OPEN`/`O_CREAT`/`SYS_UNLINK` were gated on the HANDLE capability, but the by-NAME namespace itself was not ACL'd — any process could open/create/unlink any name. **Secure-by-DEFAULT (owned-by-default):** an `O_CREAT` of a NEW name records the creating principal as the file's OWNER (the file is PRIVATE); the new `O_PUBLIC` mode bit (bit2) opts a create OUT to world-access. An open of an EXISTING owned file is admitted only for the owner or a principal the owner GRANTED; everyone else is `-EACCES`. A file with NO owner row (pre-existing / host-created / `O_PUBLIC`) is PUBLIC — byte-identical to the pre-U6 behaviour (the whole prior regression is preserved: the two cross-process U11 fixtures tag their shared file `O_PUBLIC`, one mode bit each). **M1 — the `SYS_OPEN` ACL:** an in-kernel `OWNED_FILES` table (a bounded `SpinMutex<[_;16]>` keyed by the FAT file-identity `(dir_lba, dir_off)`, mirroring `OPEN_FILES`; owner + up to 4 grants, ALL fenced by the `(ASID, ASID_GEN)` incarnation — the recycle fence the file-id/xfer/derivation machinery already uses) checked in `sys_open`'s "nothing-claimed-yet" window (a denial is a clean `-EACCES`); a private create that cannot record an owner FAILS CLOSED (`-ENOSPC`, undoing the fresh dir entry). The row is cleared at unlink (FAT may recycle the slot) and at OWNER teardown (the file reverts to PUBLIC — no persistent principal keeps owning it — which also keeps the bounded table self-cleaning); its lock is IRQ-masked via the M2b `IrqGuard` (taken in BOTH syscall and teardown contexts). **M2 — `SYS_FGRANT` delegation:** the owner grants (a `CAP_READ|CAP_WRITE` subset) or revokes (`rights = 0`) access to another principal named OWNER-SCOPED by a `Child` handle it holds (the `SYS_XFER` idiom — no raw pid/ASID from EL0); ownership is checked BEFORE the grantee handle is resolved (a non-owner is `-EACCES` regardless of its argument). The grant is an ACL edge on the FILE — nothing is delivered to the grantee's table; it opens the name and the ACL admits it, and a handle it ALREADY holds survives a revoke (the ACL gates ACQUISITION, not held caps). **F1 — delete is OWNER-only (adversarial-review fix):** `SYS_UNLINK` is now gated on OWNERSHIP, not merely the handle's `CAP_WRITE` — a content `CAP_WRITE` grantee (which legitimately opened the file RW) must NOT be able to `unlink` it, else it could `unlink` + `O_CREAT` the name to STEAL ownership and lock the real owner out; a PUBLIC file (no owner row) keeps the prior `CAP_WRITE`-gated unlink. This is the in-kernel enforcement SEAM the on-disk UnaFS `owner`/`grants:*` attributes will feed once K2/K3/K4 land (no on-disk owner format exists yet, and `fat.rs` is shared/off-lane). Verdict `:: U6-grants: … non-owner -EACCES, owner grant admits R|W, grantee unlink -EACCES (delete owner-only), non-owner grant -EACCES, revoke re-denies -> PASS ::` (23 PASS, CAPSTONE 6/6, `test-arm` MISSION SUCCESS). **Lane:** additive on `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs`; zero x86 files touched. **Flagged, deferred (not a defect — the honest scope):** the owner/grants store is in-kernel, VOLATILE, boot-scoped (there are no persistent principals yet — the model's own documented gap), so ownership is meaningful only within a boot and only while the owner process lives; a persistent form (and retroactively assigning owners to pre-existing/host files) awaits the on-disk UnaFS attributes (K2/K3/K4). The x86 twin (U6x) and a `SYS_FGRANT` that endows the grantee with a directly-usable handle (vs. an open-by-name) remain future. 🔬 QEMU-green, metal-pending.
- [x] **Teardown-last-close REAPER — a deferred-free queue + a kernel reaper task closes M2a's honest-scope leak (U11 M2b / U12b, 2026-07-08, QEMU-verified; ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-10 attended bench — the reaper freed a real teardown-orphaned chain on the card).** Closes the ONE honest-scope gap M2a left open. M2a proved cross-process unlink-defers-free for the EXPLICIT-close path (the chain frees at the last `SYS_CLOSE`, in syscall context), but a program that EXITS holding the last cross-process open of an `unlink_pending` file cannot free its chain at teardown — that runs **IRQ-masked, on the dying task's own kernel stack, TTBR0 already on the boot root, immediately before `switch_context`**, where a multi-sector polled SD write is illegal. M2a therefore LOGGED that as a transient lost-cluster leak (benign — refcount stays exact, the chain stays allocated and is never handed out, NO UAF/disclosure — but not reclaimed until reboot). **M2b actually frees it, in a block-I/O-legal context, without weakening the teardown discipline.** **(1) An I/O-free deferred-free QUEUE** — `DEFERRED_FREE`, a bounded (`NDEFERFREE = 16`) fixed ring of chain heads guarded by its OWN `SpinMutex` (SEPARATE from `OPEN_FILES`, so the teardown push never contends with live open/close on the refcount table). `clear_files_row`'s last-close-of-`unlink_pending` branch now PUSHES the chain head (`deferred_free_push` — lock + array write + unlock: **I/O-free, the SAFE twin of M2a's teardown decrement**) instead of logging the leak. A FULL queue degrades honestly to the M2a behavior (log the leak + leave the clusters allocated) — the push **never blocks, spins, or does I/O**. **(2) A kernel REAPER task** — `orphan_reaper`, a forever service task spawned at BOOT via the EXISTING `sched::spawn(name, fn, arg, cpu)` service-task API (in `main.rs`'s aarch64-baremetal service block, alongside `input`/`render`; **NO `sched.rs` change — the scheduler hook the M1/M2 notes feared turned out to be unnecessary**, and a lazily-spawned reaper is impossible anyway: a spawn allocates a heap `Box<Task>` and takes `RUN_QUEUES`, both illegal from the IRQ-masked teardown push, so the reaper must pre-exist). It drains the queue: **pop one head UNDER the lock, RELEASE the lock, THEN `free_orphan_chain`** (mount + all-FAT-copies free — block I/O, legal here: EL1, IRQs enabled, its own stack, never teardown context); it `yield_now`s when empty so it never hogs its core under QEMU's cooperative scheduler, and is co-located with the demo VERDICT core so the launcher's bounded `yield_now` poll cedes it CPU to drain deterministically. **The invariants the design holds:** a teardown-orphaned chain reaches the queue ONLY via `openfile_decref_at` returning `Some(fc)` (last-close-of-pending, the row already cleared to EMPTY), so it is queued ONCE and freed ONCE by the reaper; the explicit-close path frees inline and NEVER queues (no chain both freed inline and queued); no I/O runs under any lock and the queue lock is never held across `mount()` (so a teardown push can never spin IRQ-masked on it — bounded); SMP-safe (a teardown on core X pushes, the reaper on core Y drains — one `SpinMutex`, no torn reads, no lost/duplicated entries); M2a's explicit-close + same-process paths stay byte-identical PASS. Evidence — a two-process fixture (`el0-u11reap-a`/`-b`, the u11defer choreography): A creates + writes + holds `DEFER2.BIN` open; B opens + `SYS_UNLINK`s it (deferred — A holds it); A reads its ORIGINAL bytes back (chain alive), then **EXITS WITHOUT CLOSING** — so teardown is the last close. The launcher proves the same three checkpoints as u11defer, but CHECKPOINT-3 is a bounded `yield_now` poll of the FAT until the REAPER has freed the chain (all FAT copies) + it is re-allocatable (`first_free == f0`); the serial shows `U11-defer: reaper freed teardown-orphaned chain @cluster N` and the M2a `"teardown … left allocated (leak)"` line does NOT appear for `DEFER2.BIN` (reaped, not leaked): `:: U11-reap: teardown-last-close reaper — A exits holding the unlinked file open, its chain freed by the reaper (all FAT copies) + re-allocatable, no teardown leak -> PASS ::`; **22 PASS** (21 prior byte-identical + U11-reap — sorted scratch-worktree baseline diff vs `d57520f`, a single appended line), **CAPSTONE 6/6**, only the 3 expected M6b kills, 0 unexpected faults. `./arroyo test-arm 22` green (baremetal-gated); `./arroyo check` both arches, `./arroyo kernel8` compiles; **zero x86 files**. **Lane:** aarch64 `syscall.rs` (the queue + reaper + the `clear_files_row` push + the reap fixture/launcher) + the aarch64-baremetal service-spawn block of `main.rs` (additive, cfg-scoped) + docs; zero x86 files; `SpinMutex` reused from `sched.rs`; **NO `sched.rs`/`fat.rs` change** (the reaper calls M2a's existing `free_chain`). 🔬 Metal-pending (QEMU proves the reaper in full; rides the next Pi 4 boundary alongside U10/U11-M1/M2). **Seat coalesce-review fix (2-lens: double-free/leak · SMP/lock discipline, + refuters):** the queue machinery is freed-exactly-once and torn-read-free, but one CONFIRMED should-fix — `DEFERRED_FREE` is a bare `spin::Mutex` held PREEMPTIBLY by the reaper task (I-unmasked, `SCHED_ACTIVE` on) yet taken IRQ-MASKED by the teardown push, so a timer preempt of the reaper mid-hold + a same-core teardown push spinning on it deadlocks that core (dormant in a healthy ≥2-AP boot where reaper and EL0 fixtures sit on distinct cores; **live in the single-AP fallback** the pi4's 3/4-core boot variance can produce). Fixed in-lane by a local RAII `IrqGuard` masking IRQs across both `deferred_free_push`/`pop` (a proper IRQ-safe spinlock at any core count) — **still no `sched.rs` change**; re-gated 22 PASS byte-transparent. **Flagged, deferred to a future SMP-hardening arc (out of this lane):** the reaper is a permanent second cross-core FAT writer, and `fat::set_fat_entry`'s read-modify-write has no FS-mutation lock spanning the sector RMW — a latent lost-update / **cluster-aliasing (information-disclosure)** race should UnaOS ever run uncoordinated concurrent FAT writers (not live today: the reaper's free is await-verdict-sequenced strictly after all writers exit). Its fix touches `fat.rs`/the block layer and also covers the pre-existing U9/U10 multi-core-syscall FAT-write path.
- [x] **Cross-process unlink-defers-free — a GLOBAL open-file refcount + deferred chain-free (U11 M2 / U12, 2026-07-08, QEMU-verified; ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-10 attended bench).** Closes the SECOND (and last) of U10's two review notes — **cross-process unlink-while-open** (POSIX unlink-defers-free). U10 freed a file's cluster chain the instant `sys_unlink` ran; if ANOTHER process held the file open, its live descriptor kept pointing at a chain a later `create_in_root` + `write_grow` could first-fit-reuse — a cross-file read/write + **information disclosure**. U10 was the first arc to free a chain under a live descriptor, so this is U10-new. **The fix defers the chain-free to the file's LAST close across ALL processes.** (1) **A global (cross-ASID) open-file refcount table** — `OPEN_FILES`, a fixed `NOPENFILE = 16`-row table, one row shared by every open of a file across every ASID (`refcount` = its live descriptor count), guarded by a single **`SpinMutex`** (the scheduler's low-level spinlock reused from `sched.rs`, NOT the sleeping `Mutex`, which YIELDS — illegal in the IRQ-masked teardown decrement path). The lock is held ONLY across the short table mutation (±refcount, read the stashed chain head); the `mount()`/`free_chain` block I/O is ALWAYS the caller's, after the lock drops — **never under the lock, never in teardown context.** (2) **`sys_open` increments, `files_free` decrements** — the increment is done BEFORE `files_alloc`, so every increment pairs with EXACTLY one decrement (the descriptor's `files_free` at close/teardown, or a one-line `openfile_decref_at` on the `files_alloc`-full unwind), with no path where a decrement lands on a row this open never incremented (which the after-`files_alloc` ordering could race a concurrent same-file open into, on SMP); a full table on a NEW file identity is a clean **`-ENFILE` (`-23`)** with reserve/unwind claim-last on every failure path. (3) **`sys_unlink` marks + defers** — `fat::mark_dir_deleted` writes the directory entry `0xE5` FIRST (the NAME is gone immediately — a re-open is `-ENOENT`; a failure here changes nothing → `-EIO`), then the refcount row is marked `unlink_pending` + the chain head stashed, then `files_free_by_dir` drops all the caller's descriptors naming the file (each decrementing). If the caller is the SOLE opener the last decrement reaches 0 and frees the chain NOW (all FAT copies, in syscall context) — **byte-identical to U10's immediate delete, so `el0-u10delete` stays byte-identical PASS**; if ANOTHER process still holds it open the refcount stays > 0 and NOTHING is freed — that process keeps reading its ORIGINAL bytes until its last close. (4) **`sys_close` frees at the last close** — the decrement that drops an `unlink_pending` file's refcount to 0 returns the stashed chain head, freed via `fat::free_chain` (all FAT copies) AFTER the lock drops (block I/O legal in syscall context). (5) **`fat::delete_located` split** into `mark_dir_deleted` (the `0xE5` write) + `free_chain` (the all-FAT-copies chain free), reusing the existing `set_fat_entry`/`chain_clusters` writers; `delete_located` remains their pre-validated composition for the immediate path (the U10 "bad chain → nothing changed" contract holds byte-for-byte, and the dir-`0xE5`-FIRST-then-free-chain order is preserved). **Every U10 FAT-safety invariant holds when the deferred free finally runs** (all-FAT-copies, dir-first order, bounded free-search); the deferral loses no clusters and frees none twice (the row is cleared to EMPTY the instant refcount hits 0, so a second decrement can't re-return it) — on the same-process, cross-process, AND FAT-slot-recycle sweep paths (the last of these fixed post-landing by the seat coalesce review; see the review note below). Evidence — a **two-process** fixture (`el0-u11defer-a`/`-b`, the U7 GO-word choreography with the launcher as the single sequencer): A creates + writes + reads `DEFER.BIN`; B opens + `SYS_UNLINK`s it while A holds it open; the launcher re-mounts the FAT at THREE checkpoints — after B's unlink the NAME is gone but the chain (cluster `f0`) is STILL allocated in all FAT copies; A seeks + reads its ORIGINAL 16 bytes back (the deferred chain is alive); after A's last `SYS_CLOSE` the chain is FREED in all FAT copies + re-allocatable (`first_free == f0`). `:: U11-defer: cross-process unlink-defers-free — name gone at unlink, reader keeps original bytes, chain freed (all FAT copies) + re-allocatable at last close -> PASS ::`; **20 PASS** (19 prior byte-identical + U11-defer — sorted scratch-worktree baseline diff vs `d0d12ef`, only the appended line + the binary-growth VBAR/ELR diagnostic shift differ), **CAPSTONE 6/6**, only the 3 expected M6b kills, 0 unexpected faults, no leak lines. `./arroyo test-arm 22` green (baremetal-gated); `./arroyo check` both arches, **zero x86 files** (the `fat.rs` split is additive — x86 never calls the new writers). **Adversarial 3-lens review (refcount pairing / double-free+leak+FAT-safety / SMP+teardown lock discipline) with per-finding refuters: lock-across-I/O, teardown-I/O, SMP incref ordering, and deadlock lenses all clean; 1 real should-fix caught + fixed in-arc** — the refcount row was originally keyed only on `(dir_lba, dir_off)`, a directory SLOT which FAT recycles for a new file the moment a delete `0xE5`'s it, so a file created in an unlinked-but-still-open file's reused slot could conflate the two files' refcount + deferred free (a lost-cluster leak / mis-triggered free — **not** a UAF/disclosure: refcount stayed exact, so refcount-0 always means no live reader). This is exactly the M1 descriptor-slot-reuse hole ONE LEVEL UP, which generation tags do not cover. Fixed by (a) excluding `unlink_pending` rows from the open-time join (a `0xE5`'d file cannot be re-opened by name, so a key match on a pending row is a DIFFERENT file that reused the slot → claim a fresh row) and (b) recording each descriptor's row INDEX in a `FILE_OPENROW` sidecar so every decrement/mark hits that EXACT row, never a re-searchable key; a targeted re-verify confirmed the fix closes it with no new leak/double-free/stale-index path. **A subsequent SEAT COALESCE-REVIEW caught a SECOND, distinct slot-recycle bug on the EXPLICIT `sys_unlink` SWEEP side (refuted 0/3), fixed in a follow-up commit:** the above fixed the incref/JOIN side, but `files_free_by_dir` still matched descriptors by the recyclable `(dir_lba, dir_off)` — so when ONE process held an unlinked file F open AND created a new file G in F's recycled `0xE5` slot, a `SYS_UNLINK` of G swept BOTH descriptors and both reached `refcount == 0`, but the loop kept only the LAST orphan chain head (`orphan = Some(fc)` overwriting) → the earlier chain leaked. A benign lost-cluster leak on the EXPLICIT path (refcount stayed exact; no UAF/double-free/disclosure), DISTINCT from the teardown-only honest-scope leak below (that one is M2b's job). Fixed by freeing EVERY orphan head the sweep produces — `files_free_by_dir` is `sys_unlink`-only (a block-I/O-legal context; teardown uses `files_free` directly + logs), so it frees each head inline via `free_orphan_chain`, with the `OPEN_FILES` lock never held across the I/O. Proven by a deterministic kernel-side check (`u11defer_check_double_orphan`, the `u11_check_gen_rebind` style): it physically recycles a slot (create F, `0xE5`, create G — asserting G reused F's EXACT slot), reproduces the two-descriptor/two-pending-row state on a scratch ASID, runs the real `files_free_by_dir` sweep, and a fresh mount confirms BOTH chains free in all FAT copies — `:: U11-reuse: sys_unlink slot-recycle — two files sharing a recycled dir slot BOTH free their chains (all FAT copies) -> PASS ::`. Now **21 PASS** (the prior 20 byte-identical + U11-reuse), CAPSTONE 6/6, `./arroyo check` both arches, zero x86 files. **Honest scope — M2a (explicit-close path) landed; the teardown-last-close gap is now CLOSED by M2b (above).** The teardown DECREMENT is done (a short, I/O-free `SpinMutex` critical section, safe IRQ-masked), but a program that EXITS holding the LAST `unlink_pending` open cannot free its chain in teardown (a multi-sector polled SD write on the IRQ-masked, about-to-be-freed teardown stack is unsafe), so M2a LOGGED it as a transient lost-cluster leak; **M2b now reaps it** — `clear_files_row` pushes the chain head onto an I/O-free `SpinMutex` deferred-free queue and an `orphan_reaper` kernel service task frees it in a block-I/O-legal context (it needed NO `sched.rs` hook — the existing `sched::spawn` service-task API sufficed). **Lane:** aarch64 `syscall.rs` + `fs/fat.rs` (the split — additive) + docs; zero x86 files; `SpinMutex` reused (not reimplemented); no `sched.rs` change. 🔬 Metal-pending (the refcount + deferred-free are QEMU-provable in full).
- [x] **Open-file LIFECYCLE — `SYS_CLOSE` + GENERATION-tagged file-ids (U11 M1, 2026-07-08, QEMU-verified; ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-10 attended bench).** Gives a `File` descriptor a real end-of-life and a stable identity across slot reuse, closing the FIRST of U10's two review notes: the **same-process sibling-handle rebind on slot reuse**. The hole: U10's per-task FILES row was a bare `+1`-biased slot index with NO generation, so after `files_free_by_dir` freed a slot (an unlink invalidating a multiply-opened file's descriptors), a later `sys_open` first-fit-REUSED that slot and a lingering sibling handle — still `KIND_FILE`, still `CAP_WRITE`, still carrying the old file-id — resolved to the SAME slot and silently REBOUND to the different file (U10's immediate `FILE_USED=false` guard lapsed the moment the slot went live again). **The fix, standard slotmap/generation-index:** a per-slot `FILE_GEN[asid][idx]` counter; the `File` handle's value word now packs `(gen << 32) | (idx + 1)`; a SINGLE seam `file_desc_validate(asid, file_id) -> Option<idx>` decodes it and enforces range + `FILE_USED` + **gen == the slot's current gen**, and EVERY File consumer (`sys_read`/`sys_write_file`/`sys_seek`/`sys_unlink`/`sys_close`) funnels through it (replacing five open-coded `idx = id-1; if idx>=NFILE || !FILE_USED` checks with one) — so a stale handle to a reused slot is `-EACCES` (a gen mismatch, EVEN when `FILE_USED` is true again for the different file), never a rebind. `files_free` (the one free primitive — `SYS_CLOSE`, `sys_unlink`'s `files_free_by_dir`, and `sys_open`'s reserve/unwind all route through it) and `clear_files_row` (teardown) **bump the gen on every free**, so the very next reuse lands on a fresh generation. **`SYS_CLOSE = 17`** gives the descriptor its end-of-life: it resolves the handle for NO right (`handle_resolve(asid, handle, 0)` — a close is always permitted on a handle you hold), frees the descriptor slot (bumping the gen → any lingering sibling to that slot goes stale) and clears the handle → `0`; a `Console`/`Socket`/`Child` kind is `-EINVAL` with the object table UNTOUCHED (not closeable this arc — never corrupted); an unresolvable / already-closed / stale-slot handle is `-EBADF` (`-9`) — so a double-close returns cleanly and a use-after-close is denied. **`handle_resolve`'s contract is UNCHANGED** — it still returns `File(raw value word)`, so the scaffold File handles resolved directly by the U6 (`File(0x100)`) and U9 (`File(fid+1)`, derived caps) kernel checks are byte-identically unaffected; the gen validation lives one layer out, in `file_desc_validate`, which only the real File-I/O consumers call. And because a FRESH open at generation 0 packs to exactly `idx + 1` (the pre-U11 bare file-id), nothing observable changes for programs that never `SYS_CLOSE`: open→read/write→(implicit teardown) is byte-identical to U10, and teardown drops the descriptor exactly as a close would. **Evidence** — one register-only fixture (`el0-u11close`, witness `0x1F`) drives the ops through the syscall surface: create + grow-write `A11.BIN`, open it a SECOND time (a sibling descriptor), `SYS_UNLINK` via the first handle (frees BOTH A11 descriptors + clears the first, leaving the sibling lingering on a freed slot), REUSE the freed slots by opening + writing a DIFFERENT file `B11.BIN`, then a read through the STALE sibling is `-EACCES` (the slot is LIVE again for B11, so the only possible denial is the stale generation — NOT a rebind onto B11's bytes), and `SYS_CLOSE` → `0` / double-close → `-EBADF` / close→re-open→read round-trips B11's content. A kernel-side `u11_check_gen_rebind` gives the mechanistic proof in isolation on a scratch ASID row (claim slot → mint file-id → free (bump gen) → first-fit RE-claim the same slot → the OLD file-id is rejected while the slot is `FILE_USED`-live, the FRESH one resolves), and a fresh `mount()` confirms `A11.BIN` GONE + `B11.BIN` present with its written content. `:: U11: open-file lifecycle … -> PASS ::`; **19 PASS** (18 prior + U11), **CAPSTONE 6/6**, only the 3 expected M6b kills, 0 unexpected faults; every prior verdict **byte-identical** (sorted scratch-worktree baseline diff vs `ddefc40` — a single appended U11 line, no VBAR/USER_REGION shift observable in any verdict). `./arroyo test-arm 22` green (baremetal-gated); `./arroyo check` both arches, **zero x86 files** (the design is the pi4 lead; its x86 twin U11x is a later rmbp arc). Adversarial 3-lens review (correctness / byte-identical-regression / SMP-ordering) with per-finding refuters. **Honest scope — M1 (generation tags + `SYS_CLOSE`) lands and closes hole 1.** The SECOND U10 note — **cross-process unlink-while-open** (a file open in ANOTHER process when unlinked leaves that process's descriptor stale; POSIX unlink-defers-free) — **stays DEFERRED to the next arc (U11 M2 / U12)**, a global cross-ASID `(dir_lba, dir_off)`-keyed open-file refcount + a `fat::delete_located` split (mark-`0xE5`-only / free-chain-only). It was NOT half-landed here by design: its key constraint is that a program which exits WITHOUT closing must still trigger the deferred chain-free at TEARDOWN, and teardown (`exit` → `teardown_user_slot` → `clear_handle_row`) runs **IRQ-masked, on the dying task's own kernel stack, with TTBR0 already repointed to the boot root, immediately before the context-switch away** — doing multi-sector polled SD I/O there (plus a new global Mutex-guarded refcount table needing its own SMP-safety proof) is arc-deep and most likely wants a reaper rather than inline teardown I/O. The `O_CREAT` ambient-namespace gap (open/create/unlink are handle-cap-gated but the by-NAME namespace is not ACL'd) remains the future UnaFS `owner`/`grants:*` arc, NOT this one. **Lane:** aarch64 `syscall.rs` only (no `fat.rs`/`block.rs`/`arroyo` change — the fixture creates its own files); zero x86 files. 🔬 Metal-pending (pure syscall lifecycle; the generation tag + `SYS_CLOSE` are QEMU-provable in full).
- [x] **File GROWTH + CREATE + DELETE — the FAT writer gets an ALLOCATOR (`fat::write_grow`/`create_in_root`/`delete_located`, `SYS_OPEN` `O_CREAT`, `SYS_UNLINK`) (U10, 2026-07-08, QEMU-verified; ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-10 attended bench — real cluster allocation, both-FAT mirror + directory RMW on the card).** Lifts the three restrictions U9's `write_at` kept, in rising order of risk, so `CAP_WRITE` can **extend and create** files. **The FAT-safety invariants are the whole game, and each is load-bearing:** (1) **every FAT mutation writes ALL `num_fats` copies** — `set_fat_entry` RMWs each copy at `fat_start + f*fat_sz + sec` (neighbours preserved; FAT32 reserved high-nibble preserved), so the mirror can never diverge on a successful op; a one-FAT write would be a corrupt volume and is structurally impossible. (2) **The free search is bounded** — `alloc_cluster`/`first_free_cluster` scan `[2, count+2)` reading each FAT sector at most once, return `-ENOSPC` (new `FatError::NoSpace`) when full, and never return a reserved/bad/out-of-range cluster (only one whose entry reads `0`). (3) **No stale-byte disclosure** — a newly allocated cluster is **zero-filled BEFORE it joins a chain**, so no bytes from a previously-freed file can leak into a grown/created region; partial-sector RMWs preserve their untouched bytes, and every newly-readable byte (`[size, end)`) is covered by the caller's data (the seek gate keeps `start <= size`, so there are no sparse holes). (4) **Crash-consistency on grow** — `write_grow` allocates + zero-fills + links the FAT + writes the data FIRST, then bumps the directory `size` (offset 28) / `first_cluster` (20 hi, 26 lo) **LAST**; the dir `size` is the reader's source of truth, so a crash mid-grow leaves a consistent smaller file, never a size claiming unwritten clusters (a partially-linked new cluster is at worst a benign lost cluster). (5) **Crash-consistency on delete** — `delete_located` marks the directory entry `0xE5` FIRST, then frees the whole chain (all copies); a crash after the mark only orphans clusters (benign), and can NEVER leave a live entry pointing at freed — and possibly re-allocated — clusters (which would alias another file's data). **The enforcement point is unchanged and inherited for free:** grow, create, AND delete are all reachable ONLY through `sys_write`'s single `handle_resolve(asid, fd, CAP_WRITE)` CHECK (create via an `O_CREAT` open that endows the write cap; `sys_unlink` resolves the same CAP_WRITE), so an RO-opened File, a U8-revoked cap (the derivation/revocation walk lives INSIDE `handle_resolve`), a wrong-kind handle, or an absent handle is `-EACCES` and can never mutate the volume — no new enforcement code. `sys_write_file` splits on `len <= size - offset`: the in-place branch is **byte-identical to U9**; the grow branch (capped `GROW_WRITE_MAX = 8 KiB`) routes to `write_grow`. The read path stays byte-identical: `fat_entry` now delegates to the new `pub fat_entry_copy` (one FAT-offset site), and `scan_dir_sector` shares its 8.3 parse with the new locator via `classify_dir_slot`/`DirSlot` (single source of truth). **Evidence** — three register-only single-slot fixtures (`el0-u10{grow,create,delete}`, witnesses `0x1F`/`0xF`/`0x1F`) drive the ops through the syscall surface; each launcher folds the kernel-side proof via a **fresh `mount()` re-read**: growth shows the on-disk `size` grew + the appended bytes on the card + the original cluster intact + both FAT copies consistent along the 2-cluster chain; create shows `FRESH.BIN` present with the right size + content + exactly ONE entry (idempotent `O_CREAT`, no duplicate); delete shows `DELME.BIN` GONE + its data cluster free in ALL FAT copies + the first-free cluster unchanged from before the run (re-allocatable), and — opening the file TWICE then unlinking via one handle — a read through the SIBLING handle returns `-EACCES` (no stale in-kernel reference to the freed chain). An independent on-disk re-parse of the image confirmed all three. `:: U10: file growth … -> PASS ::`, `:: U10-create: … -> PASS ::`, `:: U10-delete: … -> PASS ::`; every prior verdict **byte-identical** (sorted scratch-worktree baseline diff vs `ca1b765` — only the U10 lines differ, plus the binary-growth `VBAR_EL1` shift `0xad800`→`0xb1000` and the identity-mapped `USER_REGION` address it drags along, which shift the M6b EL0-fault diagnostic ELR/FAR; the M6b **verdict** line is byte-identical), 18 PASS, CAPSTONE 6/6, only the 3 expected M6b kills. `./arroyo test-arm 22` green (baremetal-gated); `./arroyo check` both arches, **zero x86 files** (the writers are additive — x86 never calls them). Adversarial multi-lens review (FAT-copy mirroring, allocation bounds/zero-fill, grow write-order/overflow, delete crash-safety, cap-gating, dir-parse, x86-neutral/panic-freedom). **Honest scope — grow + create + delete land.** Deferred: subdirectories, LFN (long names), rename, write-back caching (every mutation is a synchronous RMW to the card), root-directory-chain extension (root-dir full → `-ENOSPC`), and UnaFS `owner`/`grants:*` on the by-NAME namespace ops — open/create/unlink are cap-gated on the *handle* (read/write/delete authority) but the by-name namespace itself is not ACL'd yet (the future UnaFS layer). Deletion invalidates ALL of the caller's descriptors naming the file (a same-process double-open leaves the sibling fail-safe `-EACCES`, verified by the delete fixture); the remaining gap is CROSS-process — a file open in another process when unlinked here leaves that process's descriptor stale, which needs a global open-file refcount (POSIX unlink-defers-free) deferred with `SYS_CLOSE`. An `O_CREAT` that then fails to claim a kernel handle leaves a harmless 0-length entry (no kernel leak); names are strict 8.3 (trailing-dot / multi-dot / non-representable → `-EINVAL`). **Lane note:** the U9 widening stands (`fat.rs` shared + the FAT image builder in `arroyo`; `block.rs` untouched this arc); all FAT writers are aarch64-only by call site. 🔬 **This is the first arc that ALLOCATES + MUTATES the FAT and directory on a real card — the metal-risk beyond U9's single in-place sector (multi-sector zero-fill, both-FAT mirrored writes, directory RMW). The launchers' fresh-mount re-read is the proof; the boundary re-copies pristine `GROW.BIN` between runs.**

Both paths now check RESP0 against the SD Physical Layer §4.10.1 error mask (`R1_ERROR_MASK = 0xFFF9_8008`) immediately after `send_command` and before the PIO FIFO phase, symmetrically (the U9 mirror discipline), logging one diagnostic line and returning `BlockError::Io` — so the FAT layer above (whose U9→U11/U6 integrity proofs assume the block layer tells the truth) sees the failure instead of corrupt success. Read path (CMD17) unchanged — reads have no programming phase. `drivers/emmc2.rs` only. Gate: 23 PASS + CAPSTONE 6/6 (healthy path un-regressed — QEMU deasserts busy instantly, CMD13 returns clean R1 on every write in the full FAT-mutating battery), `check` both arches, `test-arm` MISSION SUCCESS. Not live today (the reaper's free is await-verdict-sequenced strictly after all writers exit); closed before SMP scheduling widens.
    - **M1 — lost-update CLOSED.** A new aarch64-only `FAT_MUTATION` spinlock (`fs/fat.rs`), acquired IRQ-masked via `arch::without_interrupts` (non-preemptible → a proper IRQ-safe spinlock at any core count, the reaper's `IrqGuard` discipline), serializes the whole all-copies `set_fat_entry` RMW. The lock span is bounded to a single FAT-sector RMW — never a free-search, a data-cluster loop, or `mount()` — which is safe because the aarch64 storage path is polled (no scheduler yield). aarch64-only on purpose: x86's FAT path `hlt`s awaiting an async xHCI transfer event, so masking IRQs across it would hang (`with_fat_lock` is a zero-cost passthrough there — x86 behaviour byte-identical).

Two cores can never both claim the same cluster → no two files aliasing one cluster (disclosure/corruption). ORDER REORDERED: the claim now precedes the zero-fill (zeroing an unclaimed cluster could scribble a winner's linked data); the disclosure invariant holds — the cluster is EOC-reserved but UNLINKED during the fill, so no reader path can walk onto stale bytes; a zero-fail after claim orphans the cluster (benign lost cluster, chkdsk-reclaimable), never aliases.

STRICT lock order: `NAMESPACE ⊃ {FAT_MUTATION, DIR_MUTATION, OPEN_FILES, OWNED_FILES, DEFERRED_FREE}` — inner locks taken freely under it, NAMESPACE never acquired while an inner is held (no inner critical section calls `ns_lock`).

- [x] **FAT directory create/remove — `create_dir` / `remove_dir`, the lock-correct directory-mutation seam (FATDIRS, 2026-07-12, QEMU-verified; the attended money-shot rides JD7's Orin panel bench).** JD7 (the Orin panel's `mkdir`/`rmdir`) needs directory-mutation logic beyond JD6's thin additive wrappers, and a dir-mutation bug in arch-shared `fat.rs` hits all three platforms — so the seam lands as a dedicated pi4-lane arc (the F2/F3/K* lock lineage is this track's expertise), which JD7 consumes call-never-edit. Two new public `fat.rs` methods next to JD6's dir-aware twins, with **ZERO edits to any existing fn** (the JD6 additive-exception pattern): `create_dir(parent_first_cluster, name)` and `remove_dir(parent_first_cluster, name)`, plus one private `init_subdir_cluster`. They COMPOSE the reviewed F2/F3-locked primitives (`alloc_cluster` compare-and-claim under `FAT_MUTATION`; `create_in_dir`/`locate_in_dir`; `write_dir_entry_fields`/`mark_dir_deleted`/`free_chain` under `DIR_MUTATION`/`FAT_MUTATION`).
    - **Crash ordering (fail-safe, invariant 2).** `create_dir` fully allocates + `.`/`..`-initializes the child cluster BEFORE it links the parent, and the parent link itself is two writes — `create_in_dir` writes a 0-cluster DIR entry, then `write_dir_entry_fields` publishes the child cluster (the last write). Every crash window fails toward a leaked (chkdsk-reclaimable) cluster or the JD6-ledgered `FstClus==0` corner (a 0-cluster dir entry — malformed, `cd` into it lists the root), NEVER toward a live entry pointing at a cluster that later gets freed/aliased. `remove_dir` marks the parent entry `0xE5` FIRST, then frees the child chain (the U10/`delete_located` order) — a crash leaves the directory gone with a benign leaked cluster, never a live entry over a freed chain. The child-init write needs no lock (the cluster is a zero-filled UNLINKED orphan, unreachable until publish — the `alloc_cluster` zero-fill discipline).
    - **Locking span + honest residual (invariant 3 + 5).** Every SECTOR mutation is SMP-atomic via the existing per-RMW locks, and `DIR_MUTATION` is NEVER widened past its documented single-sector span (holding it across a directory SCAN or across `free_chain`'s block I/O would break its IRQ-masked-non-preemptible span rule). The internal locking is therefore sound WITHOUT the syscall-layer `NAMESPACE` lock — required, because EL1 shell callers (the panel) reach `fat.rs` directly, not through `sys_open`/`sys_unlink`. That leaves ONE honest-scope residual, the same class as F3's item (4) / the two-cores-mid-syscall interleave: **`remove_dir`'s emptiness-scan → `delete_located` is not atomic against a concurrent `create_in_dir` INTO the same target directory** (a file linked between the scan and the free would be orphaned in a freed chain). **EXCLUDED_BY_SEQUENCING today** — the only FS mutators are the single-threaded EL1 panel shell, EL0 syscalls (serialized by the syscall `NAMESPACE` lock), and the await-verdict-sequenced reaper; none races another FS mutator. The fix when concurrent EL1 FS mutators appear is a `fat.rs`-level namespace lock spanning both the emptiness-scan→delete and `create_in_dir`'s scan→write — a future seam change that WOULD have to touch `create_in_dir`, so it is out of this additive grant (flagged to the seat). **JD7 caller update (2026-07-12, jetson lane):** with the Orin panel now landing an EL1 `create_dir` CALLER (`mkdir`) as well as `remove_dir`, this residual is symmetric — an EL1-panel-vs-EL0 create/create INTO the same directory (two `create_in_dir` scan→write sequences racing for the same free slot) is the SAME EXCLUDED_BY_SEQUENCING class, closed by the SAME future namespace lock (the ledger above framed it `remove_dir`-only because at FATDIRS-authoring time no EL1 create caller existed yet). Still excluded today: the panel shell is the only EL1 FS mutator and runs single-threaded; EL0 creates ride the syscall `NAMESPACE` lock; the two do not run as concurrent FS mutators on the tegra panel.

So `-ENOTDIR` (a file target) and a root-like `first_cluster==0` target both map to `Unsupported`, and `-ENOTEMPTY` maps to `IsDirectory`. Enriching `FatError` for full POSIX-errno fidelity is a future jetson-lane seam change JD7 can carry (it owns the errno map). `NotFound`/`NoSpace`/`Io`/`BadChain`/`NoDisk` map exactly. `create_dir` mirrors `create_in_dir`'s no-de-dup contract (the caller confirms absence first).

Two new public `fat.rs` methods next to the FATDIRS block, with **ZERO edits to any existing fn** (the FATDIRS additive-exception pattern): `rename_entry(parent_first_cluster, old_leaf, new_leaf)` (rewrite the 8.3 name in place — a SINGLE dir-sector RMW; `first_cluster`/`size`/`attr` unchanged) and `move_entry(src_parent, leaf, dst_parent, new_leaf)` (relink a file entry into a new parent over the SAME `first_cluster`/`size`, then `0xE5` the source WITHOUT freeing the chain), plus one private single-sector `write_dir_entry_name`. They COMPOSE the reviewed F2/F3-locked primitives (`locate_in_dir`/`create_in_dir`; `write_dir_entry_fields`/`mark_dir_deleted` under `DIR_MUTATION`) — no `alloc_cluster`, no `free_chain`: the data clusters move BY REFERENCE.
    - **Crash ordering (fail-safe, invariant 2 — NEVER lose the chain).** `move_entry` publishes the DESTINATION entry FIRST (`create_in_dir` writes a 0-cluster entry, then `write_dir_entry_fields` publishes the shared chain head + size), and only THEN marks the source entry `0xE5`. A crash/power-loss between the two leaves a benign **DUPLICATE**: two directory entries pointing at the SAME chain (the source's original name and the destination's new name). Both are readable; the operator removes the unwanted one BY ITS ENTRY (`rm OLDNAME`). The reverse order (`0xE5` the source first) could ORPHAN the chain if the crash landed before the destination published it — forbidden. If `write_dir_entry_fields` fails after `create_in_dir` succeeds, the destination is left a benign 0-length file and the source chain is intact; if the final `mark_dir_deleted` fails, the benign duplicate stands — every window fails toward a leaked/duplicate name, NEVER a lost or aliased chain. FAT is not journaled, so the window is fundamental; the bias is fail-SAFE. `rename_entry` is a single dir-sector name-field RMW — no cross-write window at all.
    - **Directories (invariant 3).** `rename_entry` renames a directory IN PLACE (allowed): only the parent's entry name field changes; the directory's own `.` and its children's `..` point at `first_cluster` values a rename does NOT touch, so they stay correct. `move_entry` of a directory ACROSS parents is REFUSED (`IsDirectory`) — it would require rewriting the moved directory's `..` entry to the new parent, out of this additive arc's scope.
    - **Locking span + honest residual (invariants 4 + 5).** Every SECTOR mutation is SMP-atomic via the existing per-RMW `DIR_MUTATION` span, and `DIR_MUTATION` is NEVER widened to span both of `move_entry`'s two dir-sector RMWs at once (its documented single-sector contract; cross-sector atomicity of the destination-publish and the source-`0xE5` is EXCLUDED_BY_SEQUENCING for EL1 callers). The internal locking is therefore sound WITHOUT the syscall-layer `NAMESPACE` lock — required, because EL1 shell callers (the panel) reach `fat.rs` directly. The residual is the SAME class as FATDIRS's `remove_dir` TOCTOU / F3 item (4): the composite `locate → mutate` sequences are not held under one lock, so a concurrent FS mutator into the same directory could race. **EXCLUDED_BY_SEQUENCING today** — the only FS mutators are the single-threaded EL1 panel shell, EL0 syscalls (serialized by the syscall `NAMESPACE` lock), and the await-verdict-sequenced reaper; none races another FS mutator. The fix when concurrent EL1 FS mutators appear is the SAME future `fat.rs`-level namespace lock FATDIRS flagged — spanning `create_in_dir`'s scan→write — so it is out of this additive grant (flagged to the seat).
    - **ACL re-key note (invariant 5, flagged for the jetson `mv` arc + the K-line).** `fat.rs` is ACL-blind by layering — the `OWNED_FILES` ACL keys a private file by its directory-entry location `(dir_lba, dir_off)` (aarch64 `syscall.rs`). A rename/move CHANGES that key: `rename_entry` moves the name within the same slot (key preserved — an in-place rename does NOT strand the ACL), but `move_entry` writes a NEW slot and `0xE5`s the old, so the ACL row keyed on the old `(dir_lba, dir_off)` would be STRANDED (the moved file silently reverts to public) if an OWNED file were moved via a future EL0 path. This arc builds NO EL0 plumbing; the EL1 panel runs as ASID 0 (the PUBLIC principal), so a panel-driven rename/move touches no ACL row and the witness proves only the public-file reality. A future EL0 rename/move syscall MUST re-key the `OWNED_FILES` row (and the persisted `UNAFS.ATR` `dir_lba`/`dir_off` fields) to the new location, or refuse an owned target — the SAME class of key-follows-the-entry work the K-line already does for persist/unlink. Ledgered here + carried into the jetson `mv` (JD10) brief.
    - **Error-fidelity note (flagged for the jetson `mv` arc, jetson lane).** Like FATDIRS, `rename_entry`/`move_entry` reuse EXISTING `FatError` variants (adding one would break shell.rs's exhaustive `fat_errno` match — kernel-core, the jetson lane). Commit on `hw-pi4` (the seat assigns the integration hash at merge).
- [x] **On-disk UnaFS owner/grants — `UNAFS.ATR` persistence LANDED + cross-reboot enforcement now LIVE (K1 M1–M4 + K2, 2026-07-10/11, QEMU-verified; true power-cycle survival metal-pending).** U6's `OWNED_FILES` ACL is in-RAM and BOOT-SCOPED — the owner is an `(asid, gen)` incarnation, so a power-cycle reverts every private file to PUBLIC. K1 makes ownership SURVIVE REBOOT by persisting owner + grants as on-disk attributes inside the FAT volume; **K2 turns the proven-but-gated mechanism LIVE** (see the K2 bullet). **M1 landed the FORMAT + (de)serializers + reserved-file helpers + a round-trip self-test (enforcement-inert). M2.2/M2.3/M2.4 landed the real volume fingerprint, the write-through persist, and the mount-rebuild + gated cross-reboot admission; M3 proves the whole survive-reboot round-trip and M4 the fail-closed torn-row rule. The persistent-principal model — a TCB-level policy decision ABOVE this aarch64 lane — was PROPOSED at M1 and RATIFIED by Peter + the orchestrator seat (2026-07-10) before M2 proceeded.**
    - **PRINCIPAL-IDENTITY (the arc's load-bearing decision — RATIFIED, then implemented).** A persisted owner cannot be an `(asid, gen)` (boot-scoped; `asid = slot+1` is reassigned every boot). It must be a PERSISTENT PRINCIPAL, and UnaOS has none: EL0 programs are UNTRUSTED blobs loaded BY NAME with no code-signing / manifest / image hash, there is no RTC / wall-clock and no uid registry — nothing durable to project ownership onto. **RATIFIED model (Peter + orchestrator seat, 2026-07-10; launcher policy v1 is seat-owned):** a launcher-assigned, KERNEL-STAMPED principal — identity is stamped onto an ASID at spawn by trusted launcher/boot policy and NEVER self-asserted by a blob (no syscall says "I am principal K"). ONE principal per 8.3 program name, stamped by the kernel from the name the loader RESOLVED (`load_program_into_slot` → `SLOT_PPID[asid]`); no EL0 path may set or change it. Name collisions (two blobs, same name) were BY DESIGN the same principal under PROGRAM_NAME — that residual is **CLOSED by IMG-SIG (2026-07-11): the loader now mints `kind = IMAGE_SHA256`** (see the IMG-SIG bullet below). Recorded on disk as a 32-byte kind-tagged, string-projectable `PrincipalRecord` (`kind` u8, `len` u8, `value[30]`); `kind = PROGRAM_NAME` (`"prog:<name>"`) remains valid (kernel-internal manual stamps still use it), `kind = IMAGE_SHA256` is the loader's mint since IMG-SIG, `KERNEL_PID` is still reserved; `kind = NONE` = anonymous / public-only. The string projection maps 1:1 onto native unafs `owner` / `grants:<name>` keys at K4. **Honest scope of the claim:** this protects a DIFFERENT on-box software principal reading/writing/deleting another's private file ACROSS A REBOOT (the real U6 threat, extended over the power-cycle) and blocks runtime identity spoofing (identity is kernel-stamped, not blob-chosen); it does NOT and cannot claim tamper-resistance against an OFFLINE writer of the unencrypted card (FAT is plaintext — forging a CRC'd row buys nothing that `cat`-ing the sector doesn't), NOR image-substitution without code-signing, NOR rollback/replay of the attr file (no monotonic anti-replay counter is defensible without protected storage the Pi 4 lacks). No HMAC is added and none is claimed — the CRC is integrity/torn-write detection, NOT authentication.
    - **FORMAT (`UNAFS.ATR`, a hidden|system root file — attr `0x06`, so foreign OSes hide/skip it; internal magic, not the name, is the identity).** A 512-byte header (magic `UNAATR1\0` — deliberately NOT the native unafs superblock magic, so a future kernel tells the FAT bridge apart and migrates-then-deletes at K4; `version`, self-describing `header_len`/`row_stride`/`row_count`, a `committed` flag, a volume binding, `header_crc32` over `[0..508)`) followed by 16 fixed 256-byte rows (2 per sector, none straddling → a single-row update is exactly ONE device-sector RMW). **16 on-disk rows MIRROR the 16-row in-RAM `OWNED_FILES` bound — no unbounded growth.** Each row: the 8.3 name (the DURABLE identity key, re-resolved at mount), `first_cluster`/`size` (corroborating validators), a `(dir_lba, dir_off)` runtime hint, the 32-byte owner `PrincipalRecord`, 4 grants (`{PrincipalRecord, rights u32}` — matches `NFGRANT`), and a `row_crc32` over `[0..252)` covering owner AND grants TOGETHER. Two independent CRCs (header, per-row) and DELIBERATELY no whole-file CRC — so a single-row grant change never has to rewrite (and torn) the header sector. **In-place single-row `write_at` (one sector) for every steady-state mutation; a whole-file `write_grow` ONLY at creation, where a torn write is benign (no ACL exists yet).**
    - **FAIL-CLOSED (the read-side rule M2's mount-rebuild inherits).** On ANY doubt about a row — bad magic / version / geometry / volume binding / header CRC / row CRC / (M2) a name that no longer resolves — revert that file (or the whole volume) to PUBLIC, the well-defined pre-U6 baseline. **The asymmetry "fail-closed on grants, fail-open on ownership is not a thing" is STRUCTURALLY foreclosed:** owner and all its grants live in ONE row under ONE `row_crc32` and install-or-drop ATOMICALLY, so a row is never half-trusted and an owner/grant is never forged from garbage. A wrong volume binding (a FOREIGN volume or a REFORMAT — a different `BS_VolID`/cluster count) → all-public, so its rows never attach to THIS volume's directory slots. (A full byte-for-byte clone preserves the fingerprint and is NOT rejected — offline tampering is out of scope, per the honest-scope claim above.) HONEST residual (accepted this arc): fail-closed-to-PUBLIC of a torn OWNER row is a confidentiality downgrade on crash (a private file becomes world-readable); the old-row-survives write-commit-order mitigation is an M2 write-path concern. What is forbidden — fail-OPEN, admitting a specific unauthorized principal from a torn/garbage row — never happens.
    - **What M1 BUILT (gate-green, in-lane in `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs`, ZERO `fat.rs`/x86 edit):** the format consts + `PrincipalRecord`/`AtrRow`/`AtrGrant` types + `atr_crc32` + row/header (de)serializers with the fail-closed rules; reserved-file helpers (`atr_ensure`/`atr_write` steady-state row / read-all) composed ENTIRELY from `fat.rs`'s EXISTING public API (`find_located`/`create_in_root`/`write_grow`/`write_at`/`read_at`); and `k1_atr_selftest` — an in-RAM codec round-trip (populated owner+multi-grant row byte- and field-equal; a CRC-corrupt row → dropped-to-public; wrong magic / CRC / binding / uncommitted headers → rejected) PLUS an on-disk round-trip (create the empty image, write one SYNTHETIC row via `write_at` UNDER the F3 `NAMESPACE` lock — the exact M2 steady-state seam — read it back byte-equal, then clear it, leaving `UNAFS.ATR` a valid EMPTY / all-public image). It runs LAST in the demo chain and emits `:: K1-atr: … codec PASS … on-disk helpers disk PASS … ENFORCEMENT-INERT … ::` — NOT a `-> PASS` line, so the 23-fixture count stays byte-equivalent. **The round-trip uses SYNTHETIC principals ON PURPOSE — the live owner `(asid, gen)` is the one thing that must NOT be persisted.**
    - **Lock discipline.** The one-time file CREATE (`create_in_root` + a multi-cluster `write_grow`) runs WITHOUT the namespace lock (file setup, not an ACL mutation — holding `ns` across a multi-cluster grow would extend the IRQ-masked span, the F3 ns-latency concern). The steady-state single-row `write_at` (one sector, no inner lock) runs UNDER `ns_lock` — respecting the order `NAMESPACE ⊃ {FAT_MUTATION, DIR_MUTATION, OPEN_FILES, OWNED_FILES, DEFERRED_FREE}`; never held across `mount()`.
    - **What M2.2/M2.3/M2.4 BUILT (the enforcement model, in-lane in `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs` + one read-only `fs/fat.rs` accessor).** **M2.1** (behaviour-neutral, prior commit): a by-name `load_program_into_slot(name)` + the `SLOT_PPID[asid]` kernel principal-STAMP from the loader-resolved name (`current_principal()`/`slot_ppid_of()` read it), cleared at teardown. **M2.2:** `fs::fat::volume_fingerprint() -> (BS_VolID, count_of_clusters)` (read-only; parsed at mount, offset `0x27` FAT16 / `0x43` FAT32), the real volume binding replacing M1's `cluster_size`/`num_fats` placeholder. **M2.3 (write-through persist):** `OwnedFile` gains `owner_ppid`, `FileGrant` gains `ppid`, captured at create (`owned_set_owner`) and grant (`owned_grant`) from principals snapshotted BEFORE any `OWNED_FILES`/`ns` hold; the syscall handler persists AFTER the in-RAM update (`sys_open`-create → `atr_persist_row`, `sys_fgrant` → `atr_persist_grants`, `sys_unlink` → `atr_clear_row`), gated on `owner_ppid.kind != NONE` so an anonymous owner does ZERO disk I/O; `atr_ensure` now self-heals a stale/foreign-binding header (regrow). **M2.4 (mount-rebuild + gated admission):** `atr_rebuild_into_owned` reads + binding-validates `UNAFS.ATR` and, for each committed named-owner row, RE-RESOLVES the file BY NAME (`find_located` — defeats the recycled-slot hazard), cross-checks `first_cluster`, and installs an `OwnedFile` with the NO-LIVE-OWNER sentinel (`owner_asid = u64::MAX`) + the persisted principals; `owned_access_ok`/`owned_is_owner`/`owned_unlink_permitted` gain an ADDITIVE branch admitting a NAMED caller-principal (owner-by-name = full authority incl. delete/re-grant; grantee-by-name = rights-checked) AFTER the unchanged `(asid, gen)` checks — purely structural (a NONE principal never matches), so anonymous callers are inert. **The real-boot rebuild is GATED on `by_name_spawn_multivalued()` (FALSE today — one launchable named program), because a persisted owner that could never be re-acquired but denies everyone would only BRICK the file; enforcement goes live the instant a second launchable named program lands.** The `owned_access_ok` ppid branch itself is NOT gated on that global, so it is inert in the battery yet directly testable.
    - **What M3/M4 PROVE (kernel-side, the `u11_check_gen_rebind` idiom — no EL0 fixture; each emits its own UNCOUNTED `<noun> PASS` witness line, so the 23-fixture count is byte-equivalent).** **M3 (`k1_persist_launcher`, `:: K1-persist: … PASS [w=0x3fff] ::`):** stamp two scratch principals via the REAL `slot_ppid_stamp`/`slot_ppid_of` machinery, persist an owned+granted file (both `atr_persist_row` and `atr_persist_grants`, persisting `first_cluster = 0` exactly as `sys_open`-create does), simulate a reboot (drop the in-RAM ACL), remount, `atr_rebuild_into_owned`, then a 14-assertion witness: owner-by-name admitted RW, impostor + anonymous DENIED, grantee admitted R / denied W, public stays public, owner-by-name recognized as owner + has delete authority (`owned_unlink_permitted`) + CAN re-grant AND re-REVOKE a rebuilt grantee by-name (`owned_grant`, F1+F2), impostor denied both delete and grant, and a NAMED owner's row SURVIVES its owner's teardown as a sentinel (F2 MF2). **M4 (`k1_corrupt_launcher`, `:: K1-corrupt: … fails closed to PUBLIC … PASS ::`):** persist a named owner, TEAR one byte of its on-disk row (busting the row CRC), remount + rebuild — the torn row is DROPPED so an ANONYMOUS caller is ADMITTED (the file is public — the fail-closed baseline, never a forged owner). Both fixtures are FULLY self-cleaning (delete_located frees the grown chain; UNAFS.ATR restored to empty) so the stateful metal card accumulates nothing. **M4 also denies EL0 `SYS_OPEN` of `UNAFS.ATR` outright** (case-insensitive, before any lookup) — the kernel owns the ACL store; no blob may read every principal or forge a row.
    - **F1 (adversarial-review catches, folded into this arc):** (1) `owned_grant` had a live-`(asid, gen)`-only owner gate, so a rebuilt-row (by-name) owner was admitted by `owned_is_owner`/`owned_unlink_permitted` but DENIED re-grant/revoke by `owned_grant` (`-EACCES`) — fixed by threading the caller's principal into `owned_grant` and adding the same by-name owner branch; M3 now exercises it (bits 10/11). (2) `atr_ensure` regrew (WIPING all rows) on any `false` from the header check, conflating a transient block-READ error with a genuine binding MISMATCH — split into `atr_header_status` (tri-state), so `atr_ensure` regrows ONLY on a real mismatch and BAILS the (non-fatal) persist on a read error, preserving the rows. (3) M3 now persists `first_cluster = 0` to FAITHFULLY match production.
    - **F2 (seat security-tier review, 6/6-refuter must-fixes — all fail-OPEN, latent while the gate is off, fixed before K2 flips it):** (MF1/MF3) `owned_grant`'s REVOKE + GRANT/UPDATE arms matched a grantee slot ONLY by live `(asid, gen)`, so a post-reboot revoke/update of a REBUILT grantee (slot `asid == OWNER_ASID_PERSISTED`, addressed by a live Child asid 1..=8) matched nothing — REVOKE returned success while the grant stayed admitting AND was re-persisted (irrevocable), UPDATE claimed a SECOND slot per principal (order-dependent rights). Fixed: both arms also match by `g.ppid == grantee_ppid` for a rebuilt slot (structural — a NONE grantee never matches; the update re-binds the slot to the live grantee). M3 now witnesses revoke-after-rebuild (bit 12). (MF2) owner teardown (`owned_clear_owner_asid`) WIPED the in-RAM row but touched no disk, so `sys_unlink`'s `owned_owner_ppid` gate read NONE and SKIPPED the disk clear — a stale owner row survived that a FUTURE same-name file would adopt at mount (prog:X creates N → X exits → anyone unlinks N → victim creates N → reboot → prog:X owns victim's file). Fixed: teardown of a NAMED owner CONVERTS `owner_asid` to the sentinel (keeps `owner_ppid` — the file persists by principal, RAM/disk stay consistent, the unlink-time `atr_clear_row` fires); an ANONYMOUS owner still wipes-to-public (battery byte-identical). M3 witnesses it (bit 13). Also folded: `sys_unlink` now clears the ATR row BEFORE the `0xE5` name delete, so a crash between them fails toward PUBLIC instead of stranding a stale owner row.
    - **K2 (make-enforcement-LIVE — 2026-07-11, QEMU-verified + ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED on real Pi 4 silicon). Turns K1's proven-but-gated cross-reboot ACL into LIVE enforcement, end-to-end through REAL disk-loaded programs.** Four milestones, all in `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs` (+ the seat-granted build lane) with ZERO x86 change: **M(a)** landed the honest precondition — TWO more launchable named programs on the card (`K2OWN.BIN`, a private-`O_CREAT` owner; `K2IMP.BIN`, a non-owner) built as extra `[[bin]]`s of `crates/user-blob` and carried by `arroyo kernel8`; three distinct 8.3 names now mint three distinct `prog:<NAME>` principals via the sole mint path. **M(b)** added `atr_persist_grow` (the grow twin of `atr_persist_grants`): on a GROW in `sys_write` of a NAMED-owner file, re-persist the row's real `first_cluster`/`size`, so the rebuild's identity cross-check ENGAGES instead of the create-time-0 name-primary path — a defense-in-depth strengthening of the offline-tamper / non-`sys_unlink`-delete corroboration; NO-OP (zero disk I/O) for anonymous owners (the whole battery), so byte-equivalent. **M(c)** flipped `by_name_spawn_multivalued()` → `true`, so `atr_maybe_boot_rebuild` (at the head of `u7_launcher`) reinstalls persisted rows at REAL boot — the bricking hazard is gone now that a persisted owner can be re-acquired by re-spawning its named program. On QEMU (fresh FAT) `UNAFS.ATR` is absent at that rebuild, so it is a no-op and the battery stays byte-equivalent; the flip's live effect is metal (a real power-cycle where a prior boot left a real row). **M(d)** `k2_liveenf_launcher` PROVES it end-to-end through real programs (the last demo in the U7 chain): spawn `K2OWN.BIN` (auto-stamped) → it privately creates+owns+grows `K2PRIV.BIN` at EL0 (create persists, M(b) grow-repersists the real cluster); SIMULATE a reboot (`owned_clear` + remount + `atr_rebuild_into_owned` → the row is reconstructed purely from disk with `owner_asid = sentinel`); re-spawn `K2OWN.BIN` → a FRESH incarnation is re-ADMITTED purely BY NAME (the sentinel can never match a live `(asid,gen)`); spawn `K2IMP.BIN` (a distinct real principal) → REFUSED `-EACCES`. A 7-bit witness (`:: K2-liveenf: … rebuild+enforce PASS [w=0x7f] ::`, uncounted) covers create+stamp / in-RAM owner / disk-survive+rebuild / rebuilt-owned-by-name / owner-re-admitted-by-name / impostor-denied / grow-repersist-landed. Fully self-cleaning (delete `K2PRIV.BIN` + clear the attr row) so the STATEFUL metal card leaves no bricking row. **F2 (13-agent adversarial review, 1/9 confirmed):** the pre-flight "programs absent" skip returned BEFORE the stale-`K2PRIV.BIN` cleanup, so with the gate live a reinstalled row could be stranded if a program went missing after an interrupted run — fixed by cleaning a stale `K2PRIV.BIN` unconditionally first (bounded: nothing outside the demo opens it). **Honest residual:** name-collision = same principal until `IMAGE_SHA256`. **✅ METAL-CONFIRMED (2026-07-11 attended bench, real Pi 4, this-session-driven, Peter physical): Part A one-boot** — `MBENCH PASS 25/25` required witnesses, 0 forbidden hits (23 PASS + CAPSTONE 6/6 + `K2-liveenf … PASS [w=0x7f]` + K1-persist/corrupt + F2/F3 locked 240000/240000; zero R1/CMD13/AARCH64-EXCEPTION; zero A72 EC=0 heal lines). **Part B genuine two-boot power-cycle** (the `UNAOS_K2_LEAVE` knob, M(e)): boot-1 left `K2PRIV.BIN` persisted (owner `prog:K2OWN.BIN`, fc=0x12) on the card → REAL power-cut → boot-2 the LIVE `atr_maybe_boot_rebuild` reinstalled the row from the card's `UNAFS.ATR`, owner re-admitted BY NAME, impostor refused `-EACCES`, self-cleaned — `:: K2-metal: BOOT-2 … SURVIVED a real power-cycle … PASS [w=0x07] ::`. So the true cross-reboot power-cycle survival is no longer simulate-only — it holds on real silicon (logs `~/unaos-bench/pi-serial-2026-07-11-112106.log` [Part A] + `…-112529.log` [Part B]).
    - **K3 (revoke-persist commit-ordering — 2026-07-11, QEMU-verified; the fail-OPEN revoke residual RETIRED).** The revoke leg was previously best-effort in the FAIL-OPEN direction: after the in-RAM revoke succeeded, a crash or a swallowed disk error during the `atr_persist_grants` re-persist left the OLD grant on disk, so the revoked grantee was re-admitted at the next mount. K3 reverses the commit order for a REVOKE of a NAMED grantee on a NAMED-owner file: `sys_fgrant_revoke_2phase` computes the POST-revoke grant set from the current in-RAM snapshot, writes that NARROWED row to disk FIRST (via the extracted `atr_write_grant_row`), and commits the in-RAM removal ONLY if the durable write held. A persist failure is FAIL-CLOSED — the in-RAM grant is left intact (still enforced this boot) and the caller gets `-EIO`/`-ENODEV`, so RAM and disk never silently diverge in the fail-open direction (no false success). **Scope (seat 3-lens review, 2026-07-11): this closes the SINGLE-ACTOR crash/swallowed-error fail-open.** A pre-existing SMP window remained (unchanged by K3, same class as the S6-lock residual): a concurrent full-row re-persist (`atr_persist_grow` from a writer core, or a second owner-incarnation grant) that snapshots RAM between the revoke's disk-narrow and its in-RAM commit can resurrect the revoked grant on disk — closable only by spanning snapshot→disk→commit under one lock. **CLOSED by K5 (see the K5 bullet below): the two-phase revoke now holds `ns` across snapshot→disk-narrow→in-RAM-commit, and every full-row re-persister (`atr_persist_grants`/`atr_persist_grow`) takes its OWNED_FILES snapshot UNDER that same `ns`, so the two serialize and no stale (grant-still-present) snapshot can land after the disk-narrow.** The order is unchanged (in-RAM, then best-effort persist) for a WIDEN (grant/update — a lost persist just drops the new grant, already fail-closed) and for an ANONYMOUS (NONE) grantee (it persists as an inert NONE-principal row a rebuild never re-admits), so the whole 23-fixture battery is byte-equivalent. Proven by `k3_revoke_check` (kernel-side, the `k1_persist_check` idiom): a named-owner file with two grantees, one revoked through the production two-phase path, survives a simulated reboot (revoked grantee DENIED, kept grantee still admitted) and a FORCED persist failure fails closed (`-EIO`, in-RAM grant intact) — witnessed `:: K3-revoke: … PASS [w=0x7f] ::`. **Folded K2-review family items:** `sys_unlink` now aborts (`-EIO`) BEFORE the `0xE5` name delete if the durable `atr_clear_row` failed (a swallowed clear can no longer strand a stale owner row — the same commit-ordering discipline, fail-closed); the K2 launchers' `cleaned` probe matches `NotFound` specifically rather than `.is_err()` (a transient read error no longer overclaims self-cleaned). **STILL DEFERRED (post-K3):** the torn-row write-commit residual (a torn OWNER row is a crash-window confidentiality downgrade to PUBLIC — accepted honest residual, demonstrated fail-safe by M4; **K2 seat-review addendum 2026-07-11:** `atr_persist_grow` re-exercises it once per NAMED-OWNER grow, so the torn-row window and the snapshot-vs-write TOCTOU recur per grow rather than only at grant time — both degrade toward PUBLIC, the fail-safe direction); (K4) migrate-then-delete onto native unafs attributes — **CLOSED by K6 (see the K6 bullet): the sidecar is retired as the live store**. The first-CREATE of `UNAFS.ATR` (`atr_ensure`) is not `ns`-serialized (the F3 span rule forbids `ns` across a multi-cluster grow) — a benign latent SMP first-create race, untriggered today (single EL0 core; no named persist in the battery); **CLOSED by K5 (see below): a dedicated lock-free CAS gate (`ATR_CREATING`) serializes the create-if-absent DECISION without holding a lock across the grow.** **DONE by K2 (moved off this list):** flipping `by_name_spawn_multivalued()` true (M(c)); re-persisting a row's `first_cluster`/`size` on GROW (M(b)). **DONE by K3:** the fail-OPEN revoke leg. **DONE by IMG-SIG:** code-signing → `kind=IMAGE_SHA256` (the name-collision residual, closed).
    - **IMG-SIG (code-signing — 2026-07-11, QEMU-verified; the name-collision residual RETIRED).** Graduates the persistent principal from PROGRAM_NAME ("same 8.3 name = same principal", the last honest identity residual K1/K2/K3 carried) to the program's IMAGE: `load_program_into_slot` — the SOLE mint path — now stamps `PrincipalRecord::image_of(&bytes)` = `kind = IMAGE_SHA256` over the SHA-256 of the loaded image, not `program_name(name)`. Two byte-identical images share a principal (a re-spawn is re-admitted; a grown/persisted owner is re-acquired across a reboot), two DIFFERENT images under the same 8.3 name do NOT (a swapped/trojaned blob installed under a trusted name is refused `-EACCES`). **Implementation, in-lane (`arch/aarch64/syscall.rs` only), no new crate:** a no_std FIPS 180-4 SHA-256 (`sha256`/`sha256_compress`, bitwise, no heap, only the 64 round constants); `PrincipalRecord::image_sha256` stores the 30-byte digest PREFIX in the fixed `value[30]` field — 240-bit identity, collision-infeasible, and NO format bump (widening `value` would be one), so the on-disk row layout, CRC, and codec are byte-transparent (the raw digest bytes ride the same path the name string did; the native `owner` string projection is the 240-bit PREFIX form `sha256:` + 60 hex (67 chars) — NOT a 71-char full digest, which cannot be rebuilt from disk — derived by the K4-ready codec below). The K2 launcher/metal fixtures now compute the expected owner via `image_principal_of_file` (mount+read+hash, no slot) instead of a name literal. **Enforcement is principal-agnostic** — `owned_access_ok`/`owned_is_owner`/… compare `PrincipalRecord` by value, so the whole ACL machinery, the K1/K2/K3 fixtures (which MANUALLY stamp PROGRAM_NAME on scratch ASIDs and so are unaffected), and the 23-fixture battery are byte-equivalent. **Honest scope:** this is image IDENTITY, NOT authentication — SHA-256 is a plain digest, so it does NOT resist an OFFLINE attacker who recomputes the digest of a substituted image and rewrites the plaintext `UNAFS.ATR` row (the FAT card is unencrypted; no HMAC/signature is added or claimed — that needs a key in protected storage the Pi 4 lacks). What it closes is the ONLINE / same-boot substitution and the cross-reboot "same-name-different-code" adoption: a program the kernel loads and hashes gets an identity bound to its actual bytes. The K3 SMP concurrent-repersist window and the torn-row confidentiality-downgrade residual are UNCHANGED (IMG-SIG touches identity, not commit-ordering). **Cross-version migration (seat 3-lens review, 2026-07-11): legacy PROGRAM_NAME persisted rows are NOT migrated.** A card whose `UNAFS.ATR` was written by a pre-IMG-SIG kernel rebuilds its rows verbatim, but no spawn under this kernel can ever mint a PROGRAM_NAME principal — the owner is permanently un-re-acquirable (fail-CLOSED: owner-inaccessible AND owner-undeletable) until the card is re-prepped off-card (delete `UNAFS.ATR` + the owned file). Correct-direction failure (deny, never adopt); no production rows exist — only bench fixtures. Proven by `image_sig_selftest` (last in the U7 chain, read-only): FIPS KATs for `""`/`"abc"`/a 56-byte padding-overflow message; constant-buffer discrimination (byte-identical → equal principal, one-byte-different → distinct, IMAGE ≠ PROGRAM_NAME by kind); and real-image discrimination (`K2OWN.BIN` vs `K2IMP.BIN` hash to distinct stable IMAGE_SHA256 principals) — witnessed `:: IMG-SIG: … residual closed) PASS [w=0x7f/0x7f] ::` (uncounted).
    - **K4-ready (native-attribute projection codec — 2026-07-12, QEMU-verified; the IN-LANE slice of K4, landed ahead of the native unafs mount).** K4 proper ("migrate-then-delete onto native unafs attributes") reads each committed `UNAFS.ATR` row and re-stores its owner/grants as NATIVE unafs typed attributes (`owner` = the principal string, `grants:<grantee>` = the rights), then deletes the FAT-bridge sidecar. That migration is gated on a native unafs FILESYSTEM existing in the kernel — the ROADMAP §2 "BeFS" kernel-convergence chain (no_std port → block adapter → read-only mount → journaled writes + a minimal VFS for FAT/UnaFS coexistence), NONE of which is in-tree yet (`fs/mod.rs` mounts only FAT; `unaos/libs/fs/unafs` is a std Ring-3 crate). So K4-ready lands the part that does NOT need the mount: the deterministic 1:1 CODEC the migration will use, PINNED + KAT'd now. **What landed (all in `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs`, zero x86):** `principal_native_string` (a `PrincipalRecord` → its native `owner` string: `PROGRAM_NAME` → its stored `prog:<name>` verbatim; `IMAGE_SHA256` → `sha256:` + 60 lowercase hex of the 30-byte digest PREFIX; `NONE`/reserved → `None`, fail-closed); `grant_native_key` (`grants:<grantee>`); `rights_native_value` (`rw`/`r`/`w`/`-`); a no-heap `hex_lower_into`; and `classify_volume_magic` — the "tell the FAT-bridge sidecar (`UNAATR1\0`) from a real native unafs volume (`UNAFS`, mirrored from `unaos/libs/fs/unafs/src/superblock.rs`) from anything else (a FAT boot sector)" primitive the FORMAT bullet names. **THE 240-BIT-PREFIX RULE (load-bearing correctness):** an `IMAGE_SHA256` principal stores only the 30-byte (240-bit) digest PREFIX (`value[30]` is a hard cap), enforcement compares those 30 bytes, and `image_of` mints the same prefix — so the native `owner` string MUST be the 67-char prefix form (`sha256:` + 60 hex), NOT the 71-char full digest (unreconstructable from disk). Projecting a full digest would make a MIGRATED owner mismatch a FRESH mint of the same program → un-re-acquirable; the prefix form keeps migrated == fresh-mint byte-for-byte. (This CORRECTS the earlier IMG-SIG note that called the canonical form "71 chars, derived at K4".) **Proof:** `k4_ready_selftest` (last in the U7 chain, read-only, in-RAM, SYNTHETIC principals — no card, no disk, so byte-equivalent): an 8-bit witness over each projection + the discriminator + THE MIGRATION LANDMINE (a row round-tripped through the REAL `atr_serialize_row`/`atr_parse_row` codec projects IDENTICALLY to fresh mints) → `:: K4-ready: … PASS [w=0xff] ::` (uncounted). **DONE by K6 (2026-07-15):** the native mount landed via the BeFS chain (K3-mount/K4-write) and K6 executed the migrate-then-delete pass — see the K6 bullet; the ratified policy already stands (legacy `PROGRAM_NAME` rows are not migrated — only `IMAGE_SHA256` rows the kernel can re-mint). The torn-row and SMP concurrent-repersist residuals are UNCHANGED (K4-ready touches projection, not commit-ordering).
    - **K5 (revoke/re-persist SMP-window + first-create race — 2026-07-14, QEMU-verified; the two LEDGERED UnaFS-ATR persistence residuals RETIRED/narrowed).** Closes the two persistence races K3's review ledgered, both in `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs` only, zero x86, byte-equivalent battery. **M1 (the K3 SMP concurrent-repersist window).** A concurrent full-row re-persist that snapshotted OWNED_FILES between a two-phase revoke's disk-narrow and its in-RAM commit could write the still-present grant back to disk, resurrecting the revoked grantee at the next mount. FIX: the revoke now holds `ns` across snapshot→disk-narrow→in-RAM-commit (one span), and every full-row re-persister (`atr_persist_grants`, `atr_persist_grow`) takes its OWNED_FILES snapshot UNDER that same `ns` (gated on a light NAMED-owner probe first, so the anonymous battery path takes NO `ns` and does ZERO disk I/O — byte-identical). **Lock-legality argument:** NAMESPACE is the legal spanning lock because (a) the order `NAMESPACE ⊃ OWNED_FILES` is respected — `owned_snapshot_row`/`owned_grant` take-and-release OWNED_FILES WITHIN the hold, so no inner lock is ever held when `ns` is acquired (deadlock-free); (b) the disk op is the single-sector M1 `write_at` seam, NOT a multi-cluster grow, so the F3 ns-latency rule (which forbids only grows under `ns`) is not violated; the two re-persisters and the revoke then serialize globally, and no stale snapshot can land after a disk-narrow. **M2 (the `atr_ensure` first-create race).** Two SMP cores could both `create_in_root`/regrow `UNAFS.ATR`. FIX: a dedicated lock-free CAS gate (`ATR_CREATING`) serializes the create-if-absent DECISION and double-checks under the gate, WITHOUT holding a lock across the multi-cluster grow (the winner grows; a contender BAILS its persist rather than spins) — so the F3 rule holds and nothing IRQ-masked spans the grow. **Honest residual:** a persist that loses the CAS is deferred this pass (never persisted twice, never a double-create) — degradation is always toward fail-safe (not-yet-persisted), and the read-only fast path means steady-state persists never contend the gate. Proven by `k5_lockspan_check` (kernel-side, deterministic, the `k3_revoke_check` idiom): reproduces the OLD resurrection at the decomposed-primitive level (a stale pre-revoke snapshot written after the disk-narrow re-admits the revoked grantee — the window is REAL), then shows the PRODUCTION revoke + re-persist stays narrowed across reboot, the kept grant survives, and the create gate is not leaked — witnessed `:: K5-lockspan: … PASS [w=0x3f] ::` (uncounted). The full cross-core timing race is metal-latent (like the F3 witness); this proves the window + the production fix deterministically on one core.
    - **K6 (native-attr migration — 2026-07-15, ✅✅ METAL-CONFIRMED same day; the FAT-bridge sidecar RETIRED as the live store).** **Metal verdict (attended bench, repartitioned card): MBENCH PASS 33/33, 0 forbidden, single boot — `K6-migrate [w=0xff]` on silicon; the K1-persist/K2-liveenf/K3-revoke/K5-lockspan witnesses proved the NATIVE store on metal (forced-fail `-EIO` leg included); the Option-A ns-span WATCH remains open pending an instrumented CNTPCT measurement (behavioral proxy this sitting: no stall).** Retires the security-K4 residual that rode the ledger since IMG-SIG: the ACL's durable form lived in `UNAFS.ATR` — a plaintext file on the FAT volume the ACL is supposed to supersede, unprotected by the K4 journal. The U6 owner/grants ACL now persists as NATIVE typed attributes on the unafs volume (per the VFS verdict: a fixed two-mount dispatch {FAT, UnaFS}; unafs is a DEDICATED attribute volume — the kernel its only client, no user namespace; a real GPT/MBR partition, not an image-in-FAT). **The store (`fs/unafs.rs`):** one unafs root file per owned FAT file, `acl-<dir_lba>-<dir_off>` (the OWNED_FILES key → O(1) resolve), attributes `name` (the durable FAT re-resolve key) / `fc` (identity corroboration) / `owner` (`principal_native_string`) / `grants:<grantee>` (`rights_native_value`); every mutation is a journaled `set_attribute`/`remove_attribute`/`unlink` through the single coherent `with_unafs` mount — the ACL now inherits the K4 write-path ordering (leak-not-dangle) instead of raw FAT sector RMW. A power cut anywhere leaves BOTH copies, never neither; a re-run converges. Ratified policy: only `IMAGE_SHA256` rows migrate; legacy `PROGRAM_NAME` rows stay in the sidecar fail-closed un-migrated (at K6 still enforcing via the ATR fallback rebuild — **that fallback is DELETED by K7, see the K7 bullet: legacy rows no longer enforce, a legacy-only card is PUBLIC-ONLY**; card re-prep clears them). Rebuild order: migrate → rebuild from NATIVE → fall back to remaining sidecar rows — a row always enforces from one store or the other. **Live paths (M3):** create/grow/grant/revoke/unlink persist native (`native_persist_create`/`_grow`/`_grants`, `native_write_grant_row_locked`, `native_acl_clear`); the dead sidecar writers are DELETED; production never creates `UNAFS.ATR` (the `atr_ensure` create-gate is off every live path); the EL0 `SYS_OPEN` deny of `UNAFS.ATR` stays (defense in depth on stale cards); `sys_unlink` keeps its durable-clear-before-`0xE5` ordering against the native store (plus a legacy `atr_clear_row` sweep). **K3/K5 property mapping (explicit):** K3's two-phase durable-first revoke → phase 1 is now `native_write_grant_row_locked` (fail-closed `-EIO`, in-RAM intact — proven by the reworked `k3_revoke_check` incl. the forced-failure knob); K5's anti-resurrection lock-span → at K6 the SAME `ns` hold spanned snapshot→native-write→in-RAM-commit and every full-row re-persister snapshotted under that `ns` (proven by the reworked `k5_lockspan_check`, control + fix legs against the native store); **re-based by K5B onto the `with_unafs` MOUNT hold (see the K5B bullet): the same fusion property, with `ns` no longer held across the disk write.** **NS-SPAN LATENCY (Option A verdict, Maestro 2026-07-15):** the spanned disk op is no longer the single-sector `write_at` seam but a journaled MULTI-SECTOR unafs write — the IRQ-masked `ns` span is deeper than the F3-era bound. Accepted on the record: the K5 mechanism is preserved verbatim; the deeper masked window is a benchable WATCH item (the K6 bench measures the worst-case masked window around the fused revoke/re-persist), not a correctness risk. Lock order gains `NAMESPACE ⊃ MOUNT`. **Honest scope (unchanged from IMG-SIG):** no offline tamper resistance — the unafs partition is unencrypted/unsigned like the FAT card; an offline attacker can rewrite native attribute bytes. What K6 closes is the SIDECAR exposure class: the ACL no longer lives as a plaintext FAT file writable through the very namespace it polices, and its writes are journaled/ordering-protected. The 4096↔512 torn-write gap of the crate is a ledgered residual (format LOCKED) the ACL now shares. **Proof:** `k6_migrate_selftest` (LAST in the U7 chain, self-cleaning) — codec round-trips, native write/read/clear, plant-IMAGE+legacy → migrate exactly the IMAGE row (verify, sidecar-delete), both-copies power-cut window → re-run converges, no-op leaves the legacy row → `:: K6-migrate: … PASS [w=0xff] ::` (uncounted; REQUIREd in `pi4-regression.spec`, 33/33).
