    - **K7 (wholesale legacy-sidecar ENFORCEMENT removal — 2026-07-15, ✅✅ METAL-CONFIRMED same day; the "legacy rows can enforce" machinery deleted).** **Metal verdict (attended sitting, two staged boots): production knob-off boot = MBENCH PASS 33/33, 0 forbidden — the K7-F1-reworked `K1-corrupt` proved fail-closed on silicon (torn row: nothing native, no `OWNED_FILES` owner); knob-on boot = 33/33 again + the NS-SPAN measurement. ⚡ THE OPTION-A WATCH TRIPPED: `NS-SPAN: worst revoke=38000366 grants=38475785 grow=23764161 ticks (freq 54000000)` = ~704 / ~712 / ~440 ms of IRQ-MASKED hold at the fused persist sites — three orders of magnitude past the F3-era µs-scale span. HARD-WON: the near-identical QEMU magnitude was NOT TCG slowness — the span is genuinely dominated by polled SD I/O inside the fused journaled multi-sector write (per-sector CMD24 program time × the journal+attributes+sync sequence). A ~0.7 s masked window suppresses that core's timer tick and spins any `ns`-contending core IRQ-masked behind it. Per the Option-A standing order this triggered the bounded-span redesign (K5B) as a data-justified arc — the K5 anti-resurrection property re-established under a shorter fusion. **NARROWED by K5B (2026-07-15, ratified NARROW ruling — see the K5B bullet): the NAMESPACE freeze is GONE (`ns` is no longer held across any ACL disk I/O — before: revoke=38000366 grants=38475785 grow=23764161 ticks @54 MHz; after: 0 by construction — opens/unlinks/creates on other cores no longer stall behind a persist), but the ~0.7 s per-core IRQ-masked window across the polled SD write REMAINS inside the `with_unafs` MOUNT hold (the K4 coherence keystone; contended only by other ACL persists, rare) — that per-core mask stays a LEDGERED residual whose true closure is out of the pi lane (crate batched-sync, drop-boxed to Peter).** K6 retired `UNAFS.ATR` as the live store but left a transitional FALLBACK: at boot, after the native rebuild, `atr_maybe_boot_rebuild` still called `atr_rebuild_into_owned` to adopt any remaining (un-migrated, legacy `PROGRAM_NAME`) sidecar rows as owners — so a legacy row still ENFORCED. K7 DELETES that fallback leg — and **K7-F1 (same day, LC-pi lens must-fix) deletes `atr_rebuild_into_owned` ENTIRELY**: the lens proved the "fixture-only" retention was in fact a live leak — `k1_corrupt_check` (reached every boot via `u7_launcher`) still called it on the real card mount, and it installs EVERY valid committed sidecar row into the GLOBAL live `OWNED_FILES` (the header binding is per-VOLUME, so a legacy card passes it) — legacy rows still enforced on exactly the card class K7 exists to fail closed (QEMU blind: fresh FAT has no legacy rows).     - **K5B (bounded-span redesign of the fused ACL persist — 2026-07-15, the NAMESPACE-UNFREEZE; the K7-tripped ~0.7 s NS-SPAN residual NARROWED, not closed — ratified NARROW verdict, Maestro + Peter informed).** **The data (K7 metal sitting):** the fused persist sites held the `NAMESPACE` lock IRQ-masked for `revoke=38000366 grants=38475785 grow=23764161` ticks @54 MHz — ~704/~712/~440 ms — because the K5/K6 mechanism held `ns` across the journaled multi-sector native write (polled SD I/O dominates). During that hold EVERY `ns`-contending core (any open/unlink/create anywhere) spun IRQ-masked behind it. **The verified physical fact the redesign rests on:** the IRQ mask over the disk I/O is created by `with_unafs` itself (the K4 coherence keystone wraps its whole closure — including the polled SD write — in `without_interrupts`); at the persist sites `ns` was held AROUND that call, so the disk write was masked under BOTH locks. Moving serialization off `ns` therefore unfreezes the NAMESPACE but canNOT unmask the disk I/O — unmasking it would hold the non-reentrant MOUNT spinlock across a preemptible region (same-core re-entry deadlock: exactly the hazard `with_unafs` exists to exclude), and deferring the write would break K3 durable-first. Both are STOP-class protections, untouched. **THE K5B INVARIANT (the fresh anti-resurrection argument — what the review lenses attack):** *for any FAT file's ACL, the OWNED_FILES snapshot that produces a durable row, the journaled native write of that row, and the in-RAM ACL mutation that the row reflects all occur under a single uninterrupted MOUNT (`with_unafs`) hold; therefore any two ACL persisters execute strictly serially, and no persister can observe an in-RAM ACL state inconsistent with the last row it durably wrote — no stale (grant-still-present) snapshot can land on disk after a revoke's narrow.* **Exclusion argument:** the K5 resurrection required a concurrent full-row re-persist to snapshot OWNED_FILES AFTER the revoke's disk-narrow but BEFORE its in-RAM commit. Under K5B the revoke's in-RAM commit executes INSIDE the same MOUNT hold as its snapshot+write, and every re-persister (`native_persist_grants`/`native_persist_grow`; `native_persist_create` writes a fresh row) snapshots INSIDE its own MOUNT hold — so a re-persist runs either entirely before the revoke (its full row is overwritten by the revoke's later narrowed write) or entirely after it (it snapshots the already-narrowed in-RAM set and writes the narrowed row). The straddle is structurally impossible, not merely improbable. **Lock-order update:** the order gains `MOUNT ⊃ OWNED_FILES` (OWNED_FILES is a verified leaf — take-and-release, never nests another lock; no path takes OWNED_FILES then MOUNT), composing with the existing `NAMESPACE ⊃ MOUNT` into the strict chain `NAMESPACE ⊃ MOUNT ⊃ OWNED_FILES` with no inversion anywhere (`sys_unlink` takes ns→MOUNT; the persisters take MOUNT alone). `ns` is NO LONGER HELD across any ACL disk write — the persist sites do not take it at all (pre-K5/K3-era revoke never needed ns for namespace correctness; only the anti-resurrection serialization did, and MOUNT now carries that). K3 durable-first is preserved verbatim: persist failure → `-EIO`, in-RAM grant intact; a mount error splits like `native_acl_clear` — no-volume/no-storage media commits in-RAM (nothing durable exists to diverge from, the pre-K5B semantics), a real mount/parse failure fails closed `-EIO`. **HONEST BOUND (the NARROW ruling, inseparable from the headline):** K5B removes the ~0.7 s NAMESPACE freeze — `ns`-hold across ACL persists is 0 by construction, so opens/unlinks/creates on other cores no longer stall behind a persist — BUT the ~0.7 s per-core IRQ-masked window across the polled SD write REMAINS, relocated wholly into the `with_unafs` MOUNT hold (the holding core still misses timer ticks for the write's duration; only MOUNT contenders — other ACL persists, rare — spin behind it). That per-core mask is a LEDGERED RESIDUAL whose true closure is OUT of the pi aarch64 lane (crate-side batched sync to cut the sector count of `set_attribute` sequences — `libs/unafs` is format/API-LOCKED — or a scheduler-core preempt-disable-without-IRQ-mask primitive); flagged as a drop-box item, does not gate this arc. **Proof:** the reworked `k5_lockspan_check` — control leg demonstrates the resurrection with the commit deliberately DECOMPOSED from the MOUNT hold (a stale pre-revoke snapshot written after the production revoke re-admits the revoked grantee: the window class is real), fix leg shows the production revoke + production re-persist stays narrowed across a simulated reboot, kept grant intact, create-gate not leaked; the K3 forced-fail leg stays green. **Measurement (dual-span, one line):** the NSSPAN knob's emit now carries BOTH facts inseparably — the ns-hold at the sites (0, not taken) AND the measured worst-case masked `with_unafs`-hold per site — same CNTPCT fetch-max methodology, so the metal after-number pairs against the K7 before-number in the same units. QEMU smoke (NOT the verdict — TCG timing): `with_unafs`-hold worst `revoke=22653750 grants=22917187 grow=19416187` ticks @62.5 MHz — same magnitude as before, as designed (the mask is relocated off NAMESPACE, not removed). **✅✅ METAL (2026-07-15 attended sitting, the authoritative pair, ONE dual-fact sentence per the ruling): the `ns`-hold across ACL persist went `revoke=38000366 grants=38475785 grow=23764161` ticks @54 MHz (before, K7 sitting) → 0/0/0 (after — not taken; the NAMESPACE unfrozen on silicon, opens/unlinks/creates never stall behind a persist again) BUT the per-core IRQ-masked `with_unafs`-hold measured worst `revoke=38077992 grants=38616777 grow=23392723` ticks @54 MHz ≈ statistically unchanged — the polled SD write still masks the holding core, the ledgered NARROWED residual, exactly as ruled (no STOP). Verdict boot MBENCH 33/33, 0 forbidden (MOUNT-fused K5-lockspan control+fix legs + K3 forced-fail, first silicon run); log `~/unaos-bench/pi-serial-2026-07-15-k5b-sitting.log`.** Knob-off byte-inert at the same commit: rider-stripped and rider-present `kernel8.img` both `eb78f9a0…`.

That crate machinery now EXISTS — UNAFS-BATCH (main `50e8875`): autocommit-off staging + ONE commit lands a whole transaction in one root flip. K9 adopts exactly that shape at the ACL-persist choke point `native_acl_write_on` (through which ALL three fused sites — `sys_fgrant_revoke_2phase`, `native_persist_grants`, `native_persist_grow`, plus create/rename — funnel): the row's create/resolve + stale-grant removal + `name`/`fc`/`owner`/`grant` rewrites are STAGED with `set_autocommit(false)` and landed with a SINGLE `commit()`, instead of one root flip per attribute. **What this cuts:** the SECTOR/FLIP COUNT inside the K5B masked window, not the window's existence — the IRQ mask is still `with_unafs`'s (the K4 keystone, STOP-class, untouched), the disk write is still polled/synchronous, K3 durable-first is untouched. So the per-core mask is NARROWED (fewer polled-write cycles inside it), not removed; the true zero-mask closure would still need a scheduler preempt-disable-without-IRQ-mask primitive (separate residual). **The K5B invariant is preserved verbatim:** staging lives ENTIRELY inside the caller's single `with_unafs` MOUNT hold — the snapshot → staged write → in-RAM commit fusion is unchanged; only this write's internal flip count shrinks. WHAT is written is byte-identical (the staging body is the pre-K9 write body verbatim). **K3 durable-first preserved and STRENGTHENED on the success path:** one commit = an ATOMIC row (a crash lands the OLD row or the WHOLE new row, never a per-attribute partial as the pre-K9 path could); on staging FAILURE no `commit()` fires → no root flip → nothing durable changes → the old row stands → caller `-EIO`, in-RAM intact. **A scope-guard shape enforces the toggle:** the staging body owns every early return, the wrapper has NO early return between `set_autocommit(false)` and the unconditional `set_autocommit(true)`, so a staging failure cannot leak autocommit-OFF onto the process-wide cached mount (production always enters autocommit-ON — the K8a witness is the only other toggler and never nests a persist). **HONEST RESIDUAL (pre-existing, documented not introduced):** a mid-op I/O/`NoSpace` failure leaves uncommitted in-flight blocks on the shared cached mount that a later persist's commit would flush — the crate exposes no PUBLIC in-place unwind (`txn_unwind` is private; `create_files_batch` cannot express create-or-replace-with-removal), so the batch's "reload from committed root" is not expressible from this raw-op composition. The pre-K9 autocommit-ON path shares this EXACT class (a failed op's own writes are equally left uncommitted-in-flight); K9 does not weaken it -- and STRICTLY IMPROVES the immediate-durable state: pre-K9 root-flipped after each attribute, so a mid-row failure left the already-written attributes DURABLE (a partial row, a possible fail-open widen), whereas K9 stages so nothing partial ever commits (dual-lens B verdict, R20: a same-class-but-smaller-window carryover, not a new leak; no path makes K9 more fail-open than base). Closure option (b) IS NOW LANDED (K9-PARITY, R20, 2026-07-17 — see the dedicated bullet below); option (a), a crate-side public rollback primitive out of the pi lane, remains a drop-box (no longer needed for correctness — (b) closes the residual — but a cleaner in-place unwind if the crate ever grows one). **Measurement (QEMU, TCG — NOT the verdict; the NSSPAN knob gains an `NS-SPAN-K9` line carrying worst flips/blocks per single ACL row persist):** pre-K9 per-op regime `flips=5 blocks=35` → post-K9 staged `flips=1 blocks=19` (5× fewer root flips, ~1.8× fewer blocks per persist); the masked `with_unafs`-hold tick window also fell `revoke=13941875 grants=14525438 grow=11812625` → `revoke=10398500 grants=9939438 grow=9640188` ticks @62.5 MHz (~25–30 % even under TCG — fewer barrier/flush/root-flip cycles inside the mask). **Metal must confirm** (rides the next Pi sitting, NOT this arc's gate): the after-flips=1 on silicon and a real-SD masked-window number against K5B's metal before-pair `revoke=38077992 grants=38616777 grow=23392723` ticks @54 MHz — the flip cut should translate to a materially shorter masked window where each avoided flip is a polled root-sector write + barrier. **Proof (QEMU gate):** MBENCH 45/45, 0 forbidden — K1-persist/K2-liveenf/K3-revoke (incl. forced-fail `-EIO` leg)/K5-lockspan (control+fix)/K6-migrate/K8a/K8b/K8c all PASS UNCHANGED (their PASS lines are the invariant proof; the spec count did not drop). Lane: `fs/unafs.rs` (`native_acl_write_on` split into a scope-guard wrapper + `native_acl_stage_row`) + `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs` (the `NS-SPAN-K9` emit); `libs/fs/unafs` READ-ONLY (untouched). The production staging change is always-on (NOT knob-gated); the `NS-SPAN-K9` flip/block capture is `nsspan`-gated and adds only its own code (same source file compiles both ways — no downstream line-number shift).
    - **K3-BIT5 (K3-mount root-listing assertion made native-store-aware — 2026-07-18, QEMU-reproduced; metal `MBENCH 45/46 → 46/46` target).** The `K3-mount` witness's bit5 asserted `entries.len() == 2` on the unafs root — a pristine-volume assumption that the native ACL store (K6) VIOLATES BY DESIGN. Boot-time `native_migrate_from_sidecar` (run at the head of `u7_launcher`, BEFORE the witness) materialises each committed `IMAGE_SHA256` sidecar row into an `acl-<dir_lba>-<dir_off>` FILE in that same root (see the K6 store description); so on any metal card carrying a live owner row the root legitimately holds 3+ entries and bit5 dropped — the exact silicon signature `[w=0x1df]` (bit5 clear, bits 0-4/6-8 all set: both fixtures resolve + read byte-exact, only the count assertion fails). QEMU (fresh-per-build empty `UNAFS.ATR` → zero migration) never reproduced it; a two-boot same-image run confirmed the completed write-side chain self-cleans its OWN scratch, isolating the residue to the legitimate `acl-*` store. **Adjudication (b), not (a): the assertion was over-strict, not a fixture leak.** A migrated owner row is durable, by-design security state — cleaning it would destroy real ACL data. **Fix (`fs/unafs.rs`, do-it-right — no mask-widening, protection preserved):** bit5 now requires both staged fixtures present + readable AND every OTHER root entry to be an `acl-`-prefixed native ACL file; a leaked scratch fixture (`K4TEST`/`K8CUT`/`K8BSNAP`/`K8CSNAP`/`K8BCHURN*`, none `acl-`-prefixed) STILL fails bit5, so the self-clean discipline the bit exists to protect is genuinely intact — only the native store, which legitimately lives in that root, is tolerated. **Proof (QEMU):** injecting an `acl-40-20` file into the K3 fixture image reproduced `K3-mount FAIL [w=0x1df]` pre-fix and `PASS [w=0x1ff]` post-fix; the clean image stays `[w=0x1ff]`; `check` both arches, `kernel8-test 35` 0 FAIL, `test-arm 22`/`test 22`/`UNAOS_WITNESS=1 test 22` unregressed. Lane: `fs/unafs.rs` (bit5 predicate + doc) only; `libs/fs/unafs` untouched, zero x86 caller. Metal accrual: the `46/46` clean MBENCH rides the next Pi sitting.
    - **K9-PARITY (mid-staging-failure discard — the K9 lens-B residual CLOSED in-lane, 2026-07-17, QEMU-proven; metal rides the next Pi sitting).** K9-MASKCUT's honest residual: a staged ACL persist that fails PARTWAY leaves UNCOMMITTED in-flight blocks on the shared, process-wide cached `with_unafs` mount, and a LATER unrelated persist's `commit()` would flush that orphaned residue as a partial-durable row. K9-PARITY closes it entirely within the pi lane (`fs/unafs.rs` + the witness in `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs`; `libs/fs/unafs` untouched). **Mechanism:** the staging wrapper `native_acl_write_on`, on ANY failure (staging aborted OR the single `commit()` errored), calls `request_mount_discard()` (a module-static `MOUNT_DISCARD` flag); `with_unafs` consumes that flag AFTER the closure returns but BEFORE releasing the MOUNT lock, and drops the cached mount (`*guard = None`) — so the discard is atomic w.r.t. every other persister (no SMP window in which another core observes or commits the dirty mount; a bare post-hold `force_remount()` would race there). The next `with_unafs` re-mounts fresh from the committed root. **Why this is safe (CoW):** the root never flipped on the failing path (K3 durable-first: nothing durable changed), so the on-disk committed tree is ground truth; the in-flight blocks are a power-cut-equivalent LEAK, never a dangle — exactly the residue class the CoW format already tolerates. The flag is set and consumed strictly within one serialized hold (every `with_unafs`, including reads, consumes it), so it can never leak into a later or read-only hold. **Invariants preserved verbatim:** the K4 IRQ-mask keystone, the K5B snapshot→write→in-RAM-commit fusion (the failing path commits nothing in-RAM — fail-closed), and the K5B/K4 mask itself are all untouched; the discard only reloads the committed root, exactly as the existing `force_remount` durability primitive already does. **Witness (`k9_parity_check`, `:: K9-parity: … PASS [w=0x7f] ::`, 7 bits, REQUIREd in `pi4-regression.spec`):** drives a real mid-staging failure via a test-only `TEST_FAIL_MIDSTAGE` knob that aborts `native_acl_stage_row` AFTER the inode + name/fc/owner have staged (a near-complete, uncommitted row — the worst-case residue). It proves: (0) a clean control row for file A commits; (1) the mid-staged persist of file B fails CLOSED; (2) a subsequent real commit (re-persist A) lands; (3) after a simulated reboot A's row is intact; (4) B has NO durable row — the key discriminator that FAILS pre-fix (A's later commit would have flushed B's residue) and PASSES post-fix; (5)–(6) a clean re-persist of B now succeeds and reads back correct — the volume fully recovers. This fills the gap the K9 note named: the pre-existing K3 forced-fail leg fires BEFORE staging, so a true mid-staging failure was unwitnessed until now. Self-cleaning (no owned row left on the metal card). **Metal:** rides the next Pi sitting for confirmation (not this arc's gate); the discard is QEMU-observable via bit 4.
    - **BANDY-1 (the on-UnaOS SMessage bus — transport principal model, 2026-07-16, ✅✅ METAL-CONFIRMED same day: one attended boot off `main 49f2333`, MBENCH 42/42 + 0 forbidden, CAPSTONE 6/6, all bus witnesses PASS on silicon — midden's round-trip `[w=0xf/mw=0x3ff]` is the first real on-UnaOS program on real hardware; log `pi-serial-2026-07-16-111850.log`).** ROADMAP §3b arc 1: a NEW EL0-facing kernel attack surface — two syscalls (`SYS_MSEND`/`SYS_MRECV`) and a parser (`arch/aarch64/bus.rs`, the native v1 frame) — built on the K-line principal machinery. **The principal model:** every request is stamped INSIDE the kernel with the sender's kernel-held `PrincipalRecord` (the IMAGE_SHA256 mint from `load_program_into_slot` — the silicon-proven K-line stamp); the frame's principal field must arrive ALL-ZERO from EL0 and a caller-supplied (nonzero) principal is rejected `-EINVAL`, never overwritten — there is NO path where caller bytes reach the stamped header (proven by `BANDY-STAMP`). **Fulfillment authority = the invoker's grants (no new model, no impersonation primitive):** the kernel fulfills `ls`/`cat`/`cp` SYNCHRONOUSLY in the caller's own SVC context, re-entering the EXISTING gates — `bus_cat` is the `sys_open` existing-file sequence verbatim (UNAFS.ATR deny, mount errnos, NAMESPACE hold, `-ENOENT`/`-EISDIR`, `owned_access_ok(CAP_READ)` `-EACCES`); `bus_cp`'s create records the invoker as PRIVATE owner exactly as `sys_open(O_CREAT)` (owner row + native persist, fail-closed `-ENOSPC` undo). **The EQUIVALENCE WITNESS (`BANDY-EQ`, the arc's security core):** midden itself drives BOTH legs at EL0 through the production paths — a file denied via direct `SYS_OPEN` is denied via the bus with the BYTE-SAME errno, and allowed ↔ allowed. **Replies** are stamped with the RESERVED kernel principal kind (`PRIN_KERNEL_REPLY`, kind 4) — fail-closed EVERYWHERE a grantee/owner can appear: refused at `owned_set_owner`/`owned_grant`/`owned_access_ok`, un-projectable to the native store (no `principal_native_string` arm → can never persist or rebuild), and un-mintable by the reverse codec (witnessed). **Decode ceilings (the BEFS-HARDEN budget rule):** body ceiling 4 KiB → max forced allocation 4148 B per frame, refused from the header alone; per-ASID mailboxes bounded at depth 16 (a deliberate cap, STOP-tripwired like USER_SLOTS) with the full-mailbox refusal `-EAGAIN` BEFORE fulfillment (a side-effectful verb can never run and lose its reply) and no cross-ASID starvation leverage; teardown drains the mailbox and every queued reply is generation-fenced (a recycled ASID's next tenant can never read its predecessor's replies). All bus staging is HEAP-allocated — hard-won this arc: a >4 KiB kernel-stack staging array overflows an SVC/chain-task stack into the heap and dies as an EC=0 wild-jump. **AI-OFF is first-class (§3b principle 4):** trivially satisfied on metal — no vein/AI layer exists in the kernel; every verb is principal-attributed and ACL-checked with no ambient authority. **Honest scope:** v1 verbs are root-directory only with bounded payloads (cat 512 B sanitized, cp 4 KiB create-new-only `-EEXIST`, ls 3.8 KiB truncated); the kernel is the only fulfiller (handler/vessel fulfillers and capability-handle transfer over the bus are LATER arcs); the wire is the UnaOS-native v1 (Peter's ruling — host bandy migrates in a HOST arc); QEMU-green — the metal leg rides the next attended bench. Witnesses: `BANDY-CODEC` (frozen goldens + fail-closed decode), `BANDY-STAMP [w=0xff]`, `BANDY-RT [w=0xf/mw=0x3f]`, `BANDY-EQ` — all REQUIREd in `pi4-regression.spec` (37/37).
    - **BANDY-2 (the write-side bus verbs — `write`/`rm`/`mv`, 2026-07-16, ✅✅ METAL-CONFIRMED same day, same boot as BANDY-1: `BANDY-CODEC2`/`WR`/`EQ2`/`ACL` and the lens-2 `BANDY-GRANT [w=0x7f]` grant-preservation fix all PASS durable on silicon).** ROADMAP §3b arc 2: the bus verb set grows to the DESTRUCTIVE side, additive to the frozen v1 wire (new verb tags 4/5/6 + a typed `write` `[name_len][name][content]` payload; `rm` reuses the `cat` body, `mv` the `cp` body — frozen by `BANDY-CODEC2` goldens from the first commit, the BANDY-1 goldens/witness left byte-identical). **Fulfillment mirrors the direct paths byte-for-byte, under the invoker's stamped principal, no new model:** `bus_write` is `sys_open(O_CREAT)+write` (create private-to-invoker, or a truncate); `bus_rm` is `sys_unlink` by name; `bus_mv` is the `fat.rs rename_entry` in-place twin. **The DESTRUCTIVE-verb ACL discipline (inherited verbatim from the K-line):** DELETE/RENAME/TRUNCATE are OWNER-only authorities (`owned_unlink_permitted` — a content `CAP_WRITE` grantee can read/write but NEVER destroy, else it could `rm`+recreate a name to steal ownership; a PUBLIC file carries no destroy protection); the persisted native ACL row is cleared DURABLE-FIRST — before the `0xE5` name delete — so a crash fails toward PUBLIC rather than stranding a stale owner row a future same-name file could re-adopt (the K1-F2 failure class), with `-EIO` aborting the delete if the durable clear did not land; `mv` re-binds the persisted row's stored NAME after the rename (owner + grants preserved, or clears it on a re-bind failure — never a stale-named row); every native persist/clear runs OUTSIDE the NAMESPACE hold (the K5B rule) and a concurrent reader's cluster chain is deferred-freed at its last close, never underneath a live open (the U11 defer, taken by-name). **write = create-or-truncate; TRUNCATE of an existing OWNED file is OWNER-only** (the in-lane FAT primitives grow but never shrink in place, and a grantee delete-recreate would steal ownership — honest scope: grantee in-place truncate needs a `fat.rs` shrink primitive, a later arc). Truncate is implemented DESTROY-before-recreate but **PRESERVES the grant set** (lens-2 fix): the grant rows are snapshotted before the destroy, restored onto the recreated row under the same ns hold, and re-persisted to the native store after the ns drop — a content rewrite is not a revoke, byte-equivalent with the direct twin which mutates in place (witnessed by `BANDY-GRANT`: grantee still admitted via bus AND direct gate after the truncate, grant durable). The LOSS edge remains honest: a failed recreate (`-ENOSPC`/`-EIO`, or a power-cut between the halves) LOSES the original file — it fails toward ABSENCE, never disclosure (the name is gone, the ACL row already durably cleared). And a write to an existing PUBLIC file RE-PRIVATIZES it to the writer (ownership capture on a shared name — the `sys_open(O_CREAT)` private-by-default policy applied uniformly; no escalation over existing public semantics, since a public file was already anyone's to delete-and-recreate, but the consequence is stated here). **Equivalence extended (`BANDY-EQ2`, the security core):** midden drives the destructive verbs at EL0 — `rm`/`mv`/`write` of a foreign-owned file are denied-via-bus with the BYTE-SAME `-EACCES` as a direct `SYS_OPEN(RW)`. **ACL integrity (`BANDY-ACL`):** the denied destructive verbs leave the foreign owner row INTACT (no stale-owner strand, no same-name re-adoption), create no stolen name, and midden's own write-side fixtures self-clean. **Honest scope:** root-directory files only; write content ≤ 4 KiB (`BUS_CP_MAX`; the `-E2BIG` arm is unreachable from the wire while `BUS_BODY_MAX == BUS_CP_MAX` — the frame ceiling refuses first; defensive backstop only); `mv` is in-place rename (cross-directory move deferred); the TOCTOU between the by-name find and the mutate is closed by a re-verify under the NAMESPACE hold (same-name-same-slot); no fulfiller registration (BANDY-3). AI-OFF first-class (no vein on metal). Witnesses `BANDY-CODEC2 [w=0x3f]`, `BANDY-WR`, `BANDY-EQ2`, `BANDY-ACL` (+ midden's own `mw=0x3ff`) — all REQUIREd in `pi4-regression.spec` (41/41).
    - Gate (K1+K2+K3+IMG-SIG+K4-ready+K5+K6+K7): `kernel8`/`kernel8-test` 23 PASS byte-equivalent (the UNCOUNTED witness lines `:: K1-atr: … ::`, `:: K1-persist: … PASS ::`, `:: K1-corrupt: … PASS ::`, `:: K2-liveenf: … rebuild+enforce PASS [w=0x7f] ::`, `:: K3-revoke: … PASS [w=0x7f] ::`, `:: K5-lockspan: … PASS [w=0x3f] ::`, `:: IMG-SIG: … PASS [w=0x7f/0x7f] ::`, and `:: K4-ready: … PASS [w=0xff] ::` are the only additions — PASS/FAIL space-flanked so the count stays 23) + CAPSTONE 6/6 + F2/F3 witnesses locked 240000/240000, zero R1/CMD13; `check` BOTH arches (x86 unchanged); `test-arm` MISSION SUCCESS. Lane: `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs` (+ K2's seat-granted build lane: `arroyo`/`crates/user-blob`); zero x86 behavioural change. 🔬 QEMU-green — the true cross-reboot power-cycle survival (a real prog:X owner file surviving a power-cycle, admitted-by-name on the next boot, impostor denied) is metal-attended (QEMU same-boot SIMULATEs the reboot). K2 metal-verify rides the next attended bench: one boot on the 16 GB `UNAOS` card should show 23 PASS + CAPSTONE 6/6 + `K2-liveenf … PASS [w=0x7f]` + the K1 lines, zero R1/CMD13; a pristine card (delete stale `UNAFS.ATR`) is the pre-condition for the power-cycle money-shot.
- [x] EL0 first-entry FP/SIMD scrub (M6f Part 0, 2026-07-02, QEMU-verified; exercised transitively on metal 2026-07-04 — every M6f fixture entered through the scrubbed trampoline and ran correctly on silicon) — `user_task_trampoline` zeroes all of `v0-v31` (32× `movi vN.2d,#0`), resets `FPSR`/`FPCR`, and zero-inits `TPIDR_EL0`/`TPIDRRO_EL0`, immediately after the x0–x30 scrub and before the first `eret`. `CPACR_EL1.FPEN=0b11` makes the whole FP file EL0-readable and the `+neon` kernel autovectorizes, so this closes the leak the GPR-only scrub left. Scope is honest: this covers **first entry only** — the preempt-RESUME path restores each task's own saved `v0-v31`/FPSR/FPCR from its `__vec_irq` frame, so no cross-task leak there either. Exercised transitively (every M6f EL0 fixture enters through the scrubbed trampoline and runs correctly); there is no negative FP-residue read test yet, so "no kernel FP value is architecturally readable at entry" rests on the code path, not a fixture that reads the file.

#### x86_64 W^X map census (WXAUDIT, 2026-08-06 — ring-3 half enforced; kernel half NX-enforced since WXN-x86 M1/M2, `W` column report-only)

Measured, not asserted: `memory::wx_audit_root` walks the live CR3 hierarchy at the end of `arch::init`
(after `syscall::init` has set `EFER.NXE` and `CR4.SMEP`, so the numbers describe an actually-enforcing
kernel) and classifies every present leaf by its FOLDED path permission. QEMU/TCG boot, `UNAOS_WITNESS=1`:

```
:: WXAUDIT-0: classifier fires on W+X, clears RO-X, honours parent NX, voids on NXE=0 -> PASS ::
:: WXAUDIT x86: leaves=527865 user=0 user_WX=0 kern_WX=527859 (1048567 MiB) tables=1034 nxe=1 walk=9415kcyc ::
:: WXAUDIT-SLOT: ring-3 window W^X verified (slot 0, leaves=4, wx=0, nxe=1) ::
```

| Region | Leaf | U/S | W | NX | W∧X? |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Bootloader identity map, 0 → 1 TiB (kernel text, rodata, heap, stacks, BSS, page tables, **and all high MMIO incl. the xHCI BAR at 768 GiB**) | 2 MiB | supervisor | yes | no | **yes (ring-0 RWX)** |
| `USER_BASE` window page 0 — program code (`build_slot`, U1b B4) | 4 KiB | user | **no** | no | no |
| `USER_BASE` window pages 1..3 — data + two stack pages | 4 KiB | user | yes | **yes** | no |
| `USER_BASE` FB info page (WINX-1, `map_slot_fb_info`) | 4 KiB | user | **no** | yes | no |
| `USER_BASE` FB window surfaces (WINX-1, `map_slot_fb_win`) | 4 KiB | user | yes | **yes** | no |

**Every ring-3-reachable page satisfies W^X, and that is now CHECKED rather than assumed** — `user_WX=0`
in the boot census, and `wx_check_slot` re-verifies each slot's live leaves at every `alloc_user_space`
(the boot line reads `user=0` only because no address space exists yet that early; the per-slot line is
where the ring-3 claim is actually made).

**The one W∧X coincidence is the kernel's own map, and it is larger than aarch64's.** The bootloader
identity-maps the full 1 TiB physical address space in 2 MiB supervisor leaves with `W=1, NX=0`, so the
kernel's `.text` (which must be executable) shares one RWX attribute set with its heap, stacks, BSS, page
tables, **and every MMIO aperture**. `EFER.NXE` is on and honoured (`nxe=1`) — it is simply that no kernel
leaf sets the NX bit. Consequence, stated plainly: SMEP does not help here (these are supervisor pages, not
ring-3 pages), so any kernel write primitive is one jump away from execution. The fix is the x86 twin of the
deferred aarch64 WXN refactor — a linker-symbol-driven split into RO-executable text/rodata and NX
data/heap/stack/MMIO, plus re-marking the MMIO windows NX (`map_mmio_window` is the natural seam) — and is a
separate, review-gated arc. **It is not enabled here; WXAUDIT reports it.**

**Cost.** The walk reads 1034 table pages (~529 k entries) once per boot: `walk=9415kcyc` raw rdtsc under
TCG. The figure is printed on the line precisely so a future map change that inflates it is visible rather
than absorbed; the `TABLE_BUDGET` cap (4096 tables) bounds it, and exhausting the budget prints `TRUNCATED`,
which is explicitly not a pass.

**Addendum (Boot W, 2026-08-06, metal) — "It is not enabled here" above is now historical.** The
table and the QEMU census line describe the map as WXAUDIT first found it, and they are kept as
written because that is the pre-sweep baseline every later number is read against. **WXN-x86 M1
(ledger entry above) has since landed the first milestone of the kernel-half split**: a PDPT-level
NX sweep runs between `syscall::init` and this census, so on Boot W the same line reads
`kern_WX=1535 (2048 MiB)` in place of `kern_WX == leaves`, and the first row's `NX: no` now holds
only inside the two spared 1 GiB regions — the kernel image's and the AP trampoline's. The rest of
the first row is supervisor-NX. What is still open, and still report-only, is the `W` column: the
kernel's `.text` remains writable and `CR0.WP` is clear on all eight cores, which is M3's work.

**Addendum 2 (Boot Y, 2026-08-06, metal) — M2 has since closed the two spared GiBs.** With the
huge-leaf splitter landed (ledger entry above) the same census line reads `leaves=66558 …
kern_WX=305 (1 MiB) tables=1029 … l2=65534 l3=1024` — one 2 MiB leaf split into 512 4 KiB leaves,
and the first row's `NX: no` now covers only the 304 pages of the kernel image's non-writable
`PT_LOAD` union plus the single AP trampoline page at `0x8000`. The identity map is ~99.5 %
supervisor-NX. Everything the paragraph above says about the `W` column is unchanged and still
current: `.text` is writable, `CR0.WP` is clear on all eight cores, and that is M3.

**Addendum 3 (2026-08-07) — the `W` column has since moved, so read Addenda 1 and 2 as dated
observations, not as current state.** Two milestones have landed against it. **M3a** (metal-confirmed,
Boot AA) armed `CR0.WP` on all eight cores, so the sentences above that say "`CR0.WP` is clear on all
eight cores" describe Boots W and Y and no boot since — the line now reads `wp=8 wp_mask=0xFF`.
**M3b** (metal-confirmed, Boot AI) clears `W` from the kernel's executable pages behind
`UNAOS_WXNRO=1`, which makes `.text` genuinely RX and gives M3a's `WP` something to bind. Two
consequences for anyone reading a capture: **`kern_WX` stops tracking the image size** once M3b is
armed — it becomes the count of executable leaves that are *still* writable, whose floor is **1** (the
AP trampoline page at `0x8000`, which must stay writable until the last AP is up; that lifecycle is
M3c). And the census identity `kern_WX == keep_x` is replaced by
`kern_WX == keep_x − already_ro − cleared`, which degenerates to the old form on any boot with the arm
off — i.e. on every default build. With the arm off, everything Addenda 1 and 2 say about the `W`
column still holds. **With the arm on it does not, and Boot AI is the reading to use:**
`kern_WX = 1 (0 MiB)` against `keep_x = 338`, `cleared = 337`, `already_ro = 0` — `at=ktext` and
`at=kimg` both `w=0 fw=0 fx=1`, `at=ap8000` still `w=1`. **On an armed boot the sentence "`.text` is
writable" is false**, and `kern_WX` should be read as the residue count, not as the size of the
image.

**Addendum 3 (Boot Z, 2026-08-06, metal) — the census line moves with the image, and that is
the point.** Boot Z's media carries CFU-2 and the kepler decomposition instrument — 14 pages of
new code — and the same line reads `leaves=67069 … kern_WX=319 (1 MiB) tables=1030 … l2=65533
l3=1536`. **Nothing about coverage changed:** `keep_x = xpages + 1 = kern_WX = 319` still closes
three independent ways, and the first row's `NX: no` still covers exactly the kernel image's
non-writable `PT_LOAD` union (now 318 pages) plus the single trampoline page at `0x8000`. Read
`kern_WX` as the size of the executable image rather than as a leak count — it will keep
tracking code growth until M3b clears `W` from `.text`, at which point the W∧X figure and the
code-size figure finally decouple. Everything the paragraph above says about the `W` column is
unchanged and still current.

#### aarch64 W^X map audit (H1, M6c — report only; WXN not enabled)

Walk of the EL1&0 boot page tables as built in `arch/aarch64/boot.rs` (`build_l1`,
`protect_user_code`; TTBR0_EL1 → one L1 of 1 GiB blocks, with L1[0] demoted to L2_USER/L3_USER for
the 4 KiB EL0 window). `AP[7:6]`: `0b00` = EL1-RW/EL0-none, `0b01` = EL1+EL0 RW, `0b11` = RO at both
ELs. `UXN` = bit 54 (EL0 execute-never), `PXN` = bit 53 (EL1 execute-never).

| Region (VA == PA) | Descriptor | Attr | AP | EL1 | EL0 | UXN/PXN | W∧X? |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 0–1 GiB general RAM (L2_USER blocks + L3_USER pages outside `USER_REGION`) | 2 MiB block / 4 KiB page | Normal | `0b00` | RW+X | — | 0/0 | **yes (EL1 RWX)** |
| `USER_REGION` code page `[0x100000,0x101000)` after `protect_user_code` | 4 KiB page | Normal | `0b11` | RO, no-X | R+X | 0/1 | no |
| `USER_REGION` data/stack `[0x101000,0x104000)` | 4 KiB page | Normal | `0b01` | RW, no-X | RW, no-X | 1/1 | no |
| 1–2 GiB RAM (L1[1]) | 1 GiB block | Normal | `0b00` | RW+X | — | 0/0 | **yes (EL1 RWX)** |
| 2–3 GiB RAM (L1[2]) | 1 GiB block | Normal | `0b00` | RW+X | — | 0/0 | **yes (EL1 RWX)** |
| 3–4 GiB Device (L1[3]) | 1 GiB block | Device | `0b00` | RW, no-X | — | 1/1 | no |

Transient: the code page is a data page (`0b01`, UXN=PXN=1, RW no-X) during the blob copy, then
`protect_user_code` flips it to the row above — so it is never W∧X, at either EL, at any point.

Since M6d the two `USER_REGION` rows are also **non-global (`nG=1`)** and replicated per task: the shared
window is the ASID-0 boot context, and up to 8 per-task slots (`boot::alloc_user_slot`, ASID 1..8) map the
same two VAs to their own frames with identical AP/UXN/PXN. The W^X properties of both rows are unchanged
(nG affects only TLB ASID-tagging, not access permission); the VAs shown are illustrative and shift with
the kernel image size (the pool adds ~224 KiB BSS). Kernel rows stay Global (`nG=0`).

**Every EL0-reachable page already satisfies W^X**: the user code page is R+X but read-only, and the
data/stack pages are RW but execute-never. The only W∧X coincidences are in the **kernel's own coarse
RWX identity RAM** (0–3 GiB, `AP=0b00`, PXN=0): the kernel image `.text` (which must be executable)
shares one RWX attribute set with the heap/stack/BSS/data (which must be writable).

**SCTLR_EL1.WXN feasibility.** With `WXN=1` the EL1&0 regime forces "writable ⇒ execute-never":
EL1-writable regions become PXN, EL0-writable regions become UXN. The EL0 window is unaffected (the
code page is not writable at either EL; data/stack are already XN). But the kernel executes from the
`AP=0b00`, PXN=0 identity RAM above — under WXN that RAM becomes EL1-non-executable and the next
kernel instruction fetch faults, so the kernel cannot boot. Enabling WXN therefore requires FIRST
splitting the identity map into a read-only-executable kernel text/rodata region and RW-execute-never
data/heap/stack/BSS regions (a linker-symbol-driven map, mirroring the 4 KiB EL0 code/data split M6b
already performs). That refactor is a separate, review-gated change; **WXN is not enabled in M6c.**

- [x] **Fail-CLOSED block-backend selection — no silent device substitution (USBFALL F1, 2026-07-26, QEMU-verified; metal-pending).** Found by the P64-day pi4 USB-write/FAT lock-span audit (`pi4-usbwrite-audit-r23s1o`). On `aarch64 + baremetal` the canonical `Default` backend is the microSD: `emmc2::probe()` runs on the BSP synchronously and `register_sd` flips `BACKEND` to `BACKEND_SD` before any FAT-writing consumer exists. **If the card fails identify, `BACKEND` stayed `BACKEND_XHCI`** — and a later-enumerated USB stick populates the global `BLOCK_DEVICE` through `publish_usb_geometry`, after which **every `BlockSource::Default` WRITE silently landed on whatever stick was plugged in**: one physical device substituted for another, with no error, no witness, and no way for a caller to tell. That is an integrity failure in both directions (the boot volume's writes go missing; a foreign, possibly untrusted, removable device is scribbled on by the boot filesystem). Closed by `drivers::block::guard_default_write_backend`, called first in `write_block`: with no SD registered it returns `BlockError::NotReady` and emits `:: USBFALL: no SD backend registered — refusing Default WRITE … ::` once. `FatBackend::read_only()` reflects the same condition, so a `Default` mount on a no-SD Pi answers "read-only volume" up front (`VfsError::Unsupported`) instead of failing late with an opaque `Io` on every write. The guard was `baremetal`-gated on the premise that on x86 the enumerated USB device **is** the legitimate canonical backend, so there was nothing to substitute away from. **SDHC-4b killed that premise and Boot AI-2 proved it dead on metal — see the FRGUARD row below.** On QEMU-virt aarch64 (no SD backend compiled) the gating and the byte-identity still hold. **Residual, stated honestly: READS still fall through to the USB path.** A read cannot corrupt the wrong device and the USB mount reaches the stick through its own `read_block_usb` handle regardless, but it does mean a no-SD Pi can still *read* a `Default`-sourced volume off a stick — so a substituted device could still supply CONTENT (the M6g/M7 loader's untrusted-program posture already governs what that content may do; it is size-bounded, EL0-contained, and **not** signature-checked — see the shared code-signing item). Metal-only evidence still owed: whether a bench card has ever failed `emmc2::probe` identify.
- [x] **Fail-CLOSED `Default` writes on x86 — the boot-medium substitution (FRGUARD, GR21, 2026-08-07; QEMU-reproduced both polarities, metal-pending).** SDHC-4b gave x86 an internal SD backend that registers as handle `Sdhc` and deliberately leaves the global `BLOCK_DEVICE` empty, so an x86 machine can boot from a volume that is **not** the `Default` target. **Boot AI-2 is the metal proof:** booted from the internal card, the global slot stayed empty for 296 s, the operator hot-plugged a Panasonic card to READ it, that card claimed `Default` unopposed, and 15.7 s later the flight recorder created and wrote a 262 656-byte `/UNAOS.LOG` onto it (`reserved … reused=false @cluster 2` — the internal card's FAT32 cluster 2 is its root directory and cannot hold a file, so the target was provably the USB medium). Closed by re-keying `drivers::block::default_writable` for `x86_64 + sdhcblk` onto the **boot volume's identity**: the UEFI loader already reads `BS_VolID` off LBA 0 of its own loaded-image device (`read_boot_volume_serial`, LoadedImage → DeviceHandle → BlockIO) and passes it in `BootInfo::boot_volume_serial` — the same handoff INSTALL-SELF uses. A `Default` WRITE is refused **only** in the state where the boot serial is positively located on the `Sdhc` handle and absent from the `Default` slot (`BM_SUBSTITUTED`); a serial of 0, a serial found on neither handle, and no registered `Sdhc` card all FAIL OPEN with a witness, because a guard that silently kills the flight recorder is worse than the hazard. Guards **both** `write_block` and `write_blocks` — `fs/fat.rs::write_sectors` routes to the counted path, which is what the recorder's `write_grow`/`write_at` actually use. **Two refuted predicates are recorded here so they are not re-proposed:** (a) "an `Sdhc` handle exists" alone — Boots AH and AI-1 also had one (the operator's 29 MiB card sat in the internal slot) and their writes were correct; (b) "the device was queued by a hot-plug edge rather than the boot port scan" — on the rMBP the boot stick is on a USB3 port that trains ~1.6 s late, **misses the initial CCS scan and is recovered via CSC on 30 of 30 captured boots** (`ccs-margin LATE port=5 … result=CCSMARGIN-LATE`), so that axis carries no signal and would have refused the recorder on every normal boot. **Residual:** READS are unguarded (same reasoning as USBFALL F1), and the guard cannot act on a machine whose boot medium is unreachable through the block layer — which is the normal QEMU state, where the boot ESP is a separate `ide-hd`.
- [x] **Fail-CLOSED shell file verbs on a read-only volume — one writability predicate, asked before the first mutation (FATVERB, GR24, 2026-08-09; QEMU-proven both polarities, metal-pending).** LAUNCH-AR moved the shell's three EXEC legs to `fs::fat::mount_program_source()` because a machine booted from the internal SD reader has an EMPTY global `BLOCK_DEVICE` slot; FATVERB moved the remaining twenty-five FAT verbs in `shell.rs` to the same handle, because a shell whose `ls` and whose `vug` disagree about which volume is the volume is not a shell. That fix creates the hazard this row records: the handle they now all bind can be `Sdhc`, which is READ-ONLY outside the reserved flight-recorder extent (SDHC-4b/4c), so a `rm`/`write`/`mv` that followed the read verbs onto it would have begun a REAL directory mutation and met the refusal several sectors down inside `fat::write_sector` — leaving a multi-step verb (`write` is delete-then-recreate; `mv` is a rename across directories) part-way through on a volume it was never allowed to touch. Closed by asking the block layer FIRST: `fat::BlockSource::write_veto()` is consulted by `shell::open_write_volume` before any directory entry, cluster chain or FAT sector is read for modification, and a refusal is loud on both sinks (`:: [fatverb] rm -> REFUSED READ-ONLY (source=sdhc label=… reason=… ; handles=…) ::`, short form on the panel). **One predicate, not three.** `write_veto` lives on the SOURCE, not on a mounted volume, and both prior copies now forward to it — `vfs::FatBackend::read_only()` (which carried its own `Default` arm) and the shell gate — so the VFS and the shell cannot disagree about whether a handle is writable. Its `Default` arm defers to `drivers::block::default_writable()`, the same predicate `write_block` enforces (USBFALL F1 / FRGUARD above), so it cannot drift from the guard it predicts; `Usb` is a flat assertion that USB-WRITE F3's BOT WRITE(10) path is verified and routed; `Sdhc` states SDHC-4c's standing answer for the only spans a file verb can name. **Verification, and it is a real one.** `fatverb.readvol` and `fatverb.writegate` run from the storage-ready service pass — NOT with the other shell fixtures at main.rs step 5, where an earlier cut of them read `handles=global=absent sdhc=absent` and passed on all-false inputs, vacuous forever. Each leg DRIVES A REAL VERB (`ls_path`; `fs_rm` against an unresolvable name, so the gate is exercised without mutating anything) and reads the binding that verb stamped, requiring the stamp counter to ADVANCE — so a verb that stops routing through the helpers FAILS the leg instead of inheriting someone else's answer. Proven both ways on x86 QEMU: armed, `exec=sdhc read=sdhc gate=refused-ro handles=global=absent sdhc=present` with both legs PASS and the refusal printed; with `ls_path` and `fs_rm` deliberately bypassing the helpers, `readvol -> FAIL (… read_ran=false read=never …)` and `writegate -> FAIL (gate_ran=false gate=never …)`. A compile-time source law over `shell.rs` makes a re-introduced default-handle mount a BUILD ERROR on every `arroyo check` leg. **Residual:** the source law matches the path spelling and does not catch a `use`-imported bare call; the refusal is a policy answer, so an operator on the internal reader has no writable volume at all until one is mounted — that is the honest state of that boot, not a regression.
- [ ] **USBFALL F1 does not cover the COUNTED write path on aarch64 (pre-existing, found during FRGUARD, GR21 — Pi lane).** `guard_default_write_backend` is called from `write_block` only. `fs/fat.rs::write_sectors` (every whole-sector run since MULTIBLK) routes to `block::write_blocks`, which carries no guard on `aarch64 + baremetal` — so a `Default` multi-sector FAT write on a no-SD Pi still lands on the substituted stick, exactly the failure F1 was opened to close. The x86 FRGUARD arm guards both entry points; the aarch64 arm was left untouched because that arc's contract was byte-identical aarch64 decisions. **Fix is one `#[cfg]`'d call at the head of `write_blocks`; owner: Pi lane.**
- [ ] FAT mutation lock span on the `Usb` source — bounded, but expensive (USBFALL F2, 2026-07-26). `fat.rs`'s `FAT_MUTATION`/`DIR_MUTATION` holds are IRQ-masked and non-preemptible, and were justified by "the aarch64 I/O is polled". That premise is **source-blind**: since USB-WRITE, a `BlockSource::Usb` sector RMW runs `write_block_usb → … → pump_until_bot_done`, whose deadline is `hw_wait_budget() * 3` (450 M CNTVCT ticks, ~8 s at 54 MHz) and whose pump body executes `wfi` under the `PSTATE.I` the hold itself masked. **Not a deadlock** (WFI wakes on a pending physical interrupt with I set) and the volume stays consistent, but a failing transfer means a multi-second hold (×`num_fats`) with the scheduler stopped and EL0 frozen — availability, not integrity. USBFALL states the cost per-source in the `with_fat_lock` LOCK SPAN paragraph and routes every `FatFs` site through `with_fat_lock_src`/`with_dir_lock_src` so a new source must answer it; the span is unchanged. Open until either the BOT deadline is bounded for lock-held transfers (xHCI lane) or the span is narrowed for `Usb`.

- [x] **Syscall-return register hygiene — the cross-arch audit (2026-08-03, [read-from-code], both arches).** Question: can kernel register or stack state reach a user task through a syscall return? **aarch64: NO, structurally, twice over** — `__vec_svc` banks `ELR/SPSR/SP_EL0` onto the *task's own kernel stack* at entry and restores from that same frame before `eret` (the entry comment names the hazard: "per-core, and a blocking syscall would clobber them"; the IRQ path banks unconditionally with the masked epilogue — M6e), so a task blocking mid-syscall keeps its user SP in its own frame; and `RESTORE_GPRS` overwrites the full register file from the task's own entry frame, so kernel values cannot ride an `eret` — no scrub needed because the restore *is* the scrub. All 16 `eret` sites are per-task-frame epilogues; first entry sets `SP_EL0` per-task with the full GPR/FP scrub (see the M6f entry above). **x86: both halves were real bugs, found and fixed by the x86 seat the same day** — the LSTAR stub parked the user RSP in a *per-CPU* slot (cross-task stack aliasing on `sysret` after a mid-syscall block; caught on metal as a fault in the *wrong program*), now on the task's own kernel stack converging on the aarch64 shape; and the sysret six-register scrub was incompletely applied (a commit *titled* complete that wasn't — the overclaim crashed VUG.ELF on metal; fixed, disassembly-proven). **Standing law from the pair (MERGE-R23): the arches satisfy the same invariant by opposite mechanisms — never port a syscall-return "fix" across; porting either arch's remedy to the other breaks it.**

- [x] **aarch64 EL0 return-path GPR audit + the residue WITNESS (ERET-SCRUB, R23S1Z, 2026-08-04, QEMU-verified; metal-pending).** The aarch64 half of the entry above was `[read-from-code]`; this is the same question re-asked from the vectors outward, and then **observed from EL0** instead of argued. **Every EL0 return path, enumerated from the vector table** (`arch/aarch64/exceptions.rs`): `__vec_svc` (syscall return, `eret` at :95), `__vec_irq` (preempt/IRQ return, :299), `__vec_sync` (the JB1e heal resume, :354), `__vec_serror` (the drain resume, :381) and `user_task_trampoline` (the FIRST `eret` into a task, `arch/aarch64/sched.rs`:1339). The three boot `eret`s (`boot.rs`:1088, `boot_virt.rs`:248, `boot_tegra.rs`:175) are EL2→EL1 drops and never reach EL0. **Result: the class of registers that carry kernel residue to EL0 is EMPTY, so nothing was scrubbed and nothing needed to be.** The four vector paths each pair `SAVE_GPRS`/`SAVE_FP` (exceptions.rs:166/212) with `RESTORE_FP`/`RESTORE_GPRS` (:235/:185), and `RESTORE_GPRS` reloads **all** of x0–x30 from the interrupted context's own frame — every register is class (a) *restored-from-frame*, x0 alone is class (b) *deliberate return value* (`aarch64_svc_handler` writes the x0 slot and only the x0 slot: `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs`, `*frame.add(0) = ret`). `SP_EL0`, `FPSR`/`FPCR` and `v0–v31` are banked and restored on the same frame; `TPIDR_EL0`/`TPIDRRO_EL0` are never written by the syscall path, so they keep the task's own values. First entry is the one path with no frame to restore from, and it is scrubbed rather than restored (M6d GPRs + M6f FP/TPIDR — see both entries above). **Note (correction to the 2026-08-03 entry): there are 8 `eret` instructions in the aarch64 tree, not 16 — the 16 is the vector table's entry count.** **The witness is the contribution.** The M6f entry above states its own limit in as many words ("rests on the code path, not a fixture that reads the file"); that gap is now closed by two EL0 fixtures in the shared window's code page (`arch/aarch64/syscall.rs`, `__eret_prog_entry` / `__eret_prog_svc`, verdict `eret_scrub_verdict`), both register-only so they honour the M6e shared-stack tripwire, both exiting on their own sentinel status so no other family's counters move. `el0-eretentry` ORs x0–x30, **both** 64-bit lanes of v0–v31, `FPSR`/`FPCR` and `TPIDR_EL0`/`TPIDRRO_EL0` at its first EL0 instruction and reports the OR. `el0-eretsvc` plants a distinct sentinel in every GPR the ABI does not overwrite, mirrors one into all of v0–v31, records `SP_EL0`, then crosses **`SYS_YIELD`** — chosen because it context-switches *inside* the handler, so an unrelated task's Rust and NEON run between `__vec_svc`'s save and its `eret` — and reports a per-register mismatch bitmap (bit N = xN did not come back; bit 27 also covers `SP_EL0`, bit 31 the whole vector file; bit 0 is never set, x0 being the return value). Both PASS on the value **zero**, so both verdict arms additionally require that the report HAPPENED and that neither fixture was killed — a witness that never spoke reads FAIL, never a silent pass. **Convictability was proven red-side, not assumed:** with `movz x5, #0xDEAD` substituted for one `mov x5, xzr` in the trampoline scrub and a `*frame.add(5) = 0xDEAD_BEEF` added to the SVC handler, the fixtures reported exactly `or=0xdead` and `bitmap=0x20` (bit 5 — the precise register poisoned), both REQUIREs dropped and both dedicated FORBIDs fired; the injections were then reverted. Spec: two REQUIREs + three FORBIDs in `unaos/scripts/specs/pi4-regression.spec`, FAIL wording matched against the emitter's literal text per the same-day convictability rule. Gate: `kernel8-test 210` → **MBENCH PASS, 93/93, 0 forbidden hits**. **Preserved and FLAGGED, not scrubbed:** `__vec_serror`'s drain-resume tail (exceptions.rs:379–381) is the one resuming path that does **not** bank/restore `ELR`/`SPSR`/`SP_EL0` the way `__vec_svc`/`__vec_irq`/`__vec_sync` do. It is not a register leak — `RESTORE_GPRS`/`RESTORE_FP` still reload the whole file — and it is sound today because `aarch64_serror_drain_check` is short, allocation-free and cannot context-switch, and entry masked all of DAIF. But it is a divergence from the discipline the other three follow, and it would become a real defect the moment that handler is allowed to do substantial work or block. Ledgered here rather than changed: touching the drain tail was outside this arc's question, and a wrong change there is worse than the divergence. **Standing law re-affirmed (MERGE-R23):** this is an aarch64-native audit of aarch64 code; the x86 remedies (the SYSRET six-register scrub and the first-entry `iretq` scrub, entries above) are **not** ported here and must not be — aarch64 satisfies the invariant by restoring, x86 by scrubbing.

### Ring-0 parser audits (today's practical attack surface)
- [ ] Network stack: header-length/bounds audit (Ethernet/ARP/IP/TCP options/DHCP options)
- [ ] USB: descriptor parsing bounds (config/interface/endpoint/HID report walks; hub paths)
- [ ] FAT: BPB/dirent/FAT-chain bounds and loop guards (partially hardened during the read-only arc — re-verify and record)
- [x] unafs RO mount (UNAFS-K3): bound every disk-derived size/count/offset vs volume geometry (superblock `bitmap_blocks`, inode `size`, extent arithmetic, codec lengths; `try_reserve` not `with_capacity`, size-limited decode) — **DONE (BEFS-HARDEN, 2026-07-13)**: `Superblock::validate` at the parse boundary; `try_reserve` on the bitmap load and `read_from_extents`; `checked_*` extent arithmetic + past-volume extent rejection; bounded bulk hole fill and free walks; checked spilled-extent sums on the query/get_attribute paths (the QSIM-flagged sites); two-tier bincode decode budgets (8 KiB block records / 4 MiB extent-backed records, pinned under the kernel heap by a regression test). 23 hostile-volume fixtures assert `Err`-not-panic (`unaos/libs/fs/unafs/tests/hostile_volume.rs`); golden format KATs byte-identical

#### unafs kernel WRITES — the K4 write-path honest scope (UNAFS-K4, 2026-07-14; ✅ METAL-CONFIRMED 2026-07-14 attended Pi 4 bench — `K4-write PASS [w=0x7f]` on 3 cold boots with a GENUINE power-cut between each, boot-2 remount CLEAN: the journaled write was durable across real power loss)

The UNAFS-K3 mount became read-WRITE at K4: `fs/unafs.rs`'s `write_sector` routes to
`drivers::block::write_block` (emmc2 CMD24 + R1/CMD13 status checks). Stated honestly:

- **Coherence.** Writes make a per-call mount a corruption hazard — two live mounts hold two
  independent in-RAM allocation state (the refcount map + inode map since K8a; bitmap + journal
  cursor before it), so a block one frees the other can re-hand-out. Closed structurally: exactly
  ONE process-wide, IRQ-masked mount (`with_unafs`, the F3 `NAMESPACE`-lock discipline) is the
  sole path for read AND write, so there is one authoritative in-RAM state and every operation is
  serialized. A pure read never mutates, so it never triggers any drop-time write-back — reads
  stay genuinely read-only.
- **Torn-write safety (as landed at K4, superseded at K8a).** At K4, crash safety was by write
  ORDERING, not journal replay: the WAL was intent-logging only (dirty detection, no rollback),
  and per-mutation ordering (new-extents-first, single-block metadata swap, free-last) bounded a
  power cut to a LEAK, never a dangling reference. **K8a replaced this whole mechanism:** every
  mutation is now one copy-on-write transaction made visible by a single atomic 512 B root-sector
  flip — a power cut yields the old committed tree or the new one, never a hybrid, and there are
  no leak windows (an uncommitted transaction's blocks were never committed as allocated). The
  WAL is deleted from the format; there is no dirty-mount state to detect.
- **The 4096↔512 atomicity gap — ✅✅ FORMALLY RETIRED (2026-07-16 attended sitting: K8a
  METAL-CONFIRMED — pristine-v3 boot MBENCH 43/43 + 0 forbidden AND a real v2→v3 MIGRATED
  volume boot 43/43, K8a-cow power-cut-before-flip convergence + refcount persistence proven
  on silicon both boots; log `pi-serial-2026-07-16-k8a-sitting.log`).** The residual as ledgered at K4: the crate's "single-block metadata
  swap is the atomic point" held only at 4096 B granularity, while the medium writes 512 B
  sectors — one `write_block` = eight sequential, individually-non-atomic `write_sector`s, so a
  power cut mid-block could tear a metadata swap unless the record fit the first sector. **K8a
  dissolves the class BY FORMAT (the design-doc motive):** the copy-on-write path never
  overwrites a committed block — every mutation writes FRESH blocks, and the only load-bearing
  in-place write left on the volume is the commit's root flip, which is ONE 512 B sector
  (compile-time assert + golden KAT pin the root record ≤ 512 B; `BlockAdapter::
  write_sector_in_block` lowers it to a single hardened device sector write). Even a tear inside
  THAT sector is survivable: A/B generation-stamped, checksummed root slots — a commit writes the
  INACTIVE slot, mount picks the newer VALID one, so a torn root write costs the crashed commit,
  never the volume. The old ordering discipline (leak-not-dangle) and the WAL are both
  superseded: there is no dirty-mount state; a power cut anywhere yields the previous committed
  tree, whole and refcount-consistent. Sector-level "journaling or a format change" was the named
  closure — the format change happened (v3; the K8 design pass LIFTED the KAT freeze and pinned
  new goldens). Remaining honest bound: the flip's atomicity now rests on the medium's own
  512 B-sector write atomicity (the SD/eMMC contract), which is exactly the atomicity unit of
  record the design fixed.
- **Proof (K4, retained).** `:: K4-write: … PASS [w=0x7f] ::` (uncounted) — create + write a
  scratch file, force a genuine remount, byte-verify the durable write, delete, remount (delete
  durable), negative path, refcount-consistent tree (bit6 was "clean journal" pre-K8; the WAL is
  gone, the bit now proves fsck-clean). Metal-confirmed 2026-07-14 pre-K8 (3 cold boots, genuine
  power-cuts); re-proof on the K8 format rides the K8a sitting.
- **Proof (K8a).** `:: K8a-cow: … PASS [w=0x7f] ::` (uncounted; REQUIREd in
  `pi4-regression.spec`) — root generation advances per mutation; a POWER CUT BEFORE THE ROOT
  FLIP (the crate's autocommit-off crash seam: fresh blocks written to the real block device,
  root never flipped, then a genuine remount) converges to the OLD tree, fsck-clean; the
  committed twin of the same mutation is byte-durable across a remount; the freshly loaded
  refcount map agrees with recomputed reachability (REFCOUNT PERSISTENCE); self-cleaning; and
  the commit-path bench counters are live (CNTPCT ticks + blocks written per commit — the vaire
  benchmark ruling). Host twins in `unafs/tests/recovery_logic.rs` additionally prove the TORN
  ROOT SECTOR fallback (garbage the newest slot → mount lands on the previous tree) and the
  crash-safe reclaim-queue drain. QEMU-proven via `if=sd` write-back; the metal power-cut proof
  (incl. the pre-K8 card migration via `tools/unafs migrate`) rides the next attended Pi bench.
- **K8a dual-lens review ledger (2026-07-16 — zero must-fix; the one should-fix [>2 GiB format
  slice-panic] and the flush-contract pin were fixed in-arc `6dd2501` + lens re-verified).
  Standing NOTEs, each real but bounded:**
  - *Refmap write amplification (leaning should-fix; the delta-refmap is the named closure).*
    Every commit rewrites the ENTIRE refcount map (all leaves + a fresh index), so blocks
    written per commit is LINEAR in volume size (≈ block_count/1024 + ~6: measured 10 blocks
    at 16 MB, 70 at 256 MB, 262 at 1 GB). The K6 ACL persist path multiplies it — 4+ commits
    per row (create + 3 attrs + one per grant), all inside ONE IRQ-masked `with_unafs` hold of
    synchronous per-sector eMMC writes. Correct today (the staged volume is ≤ 8 MB); a
    delta/dirty-leaf refmap persist collapses it. Same family as the K5B ~0.7 s mask residual.
  - *fsck double-reference blindness.* The reachability walk dedups into a set, so a HOSTILE
    v3 image with two inodes' extents over one physical block reports clean; a later CoW
    write decrefs the shared block to 0 while the other inode still points at it. Within the
    accepted attacker-owns-the-medium boundary; unreachable via any API (the allocator never
    shares); ledgered as an fsck-scope limit, not a defect.
  - *Frozen-view same-transaction NoSpace edge.* Blocks freed earlier in a transaction stay
    unallocatable until that commit's freeze, so a free-then-realloc op on a near-full volume
    can spuriously `NoSpace` despite sufficient net free space. Inherent to single-generation
    CoW; documented behavior.
  - *Witness tear-case scope.* The kernel `K8a-cow` power-cut leg proves UNFLIPPED-ROOT
    convergence on the real block device; the intra-root-sector TEAR fallback is proven only
    by the host KAT (acceptable: an intra-sector tear can only damage the inactive slot).
  - *Volume cap.* v3 geometry is capped at 2 GiB (`MAX_BLOCK_COUNT` = 524,288 — the
    one-indirect-level map bound); format AND mount refuse larger volumes with a clean
    Geometry error. Lifting the cap = adding an indirection level = a future format change.
- **Retention-aware allocator + snapshot authority (K8b).** A snapshot is a RETAINED ROOT; the
  allocator invariant is now load-bearing across multiple roots: **a block is free ⇔ it is
  reachable from NO retained root AND not from the live root.** `snapshot_create` enforces this by
  increfing EVERY block the committed root reaches through its inode map (the imap index + leaves,
  each inode block, each data/spilled-attribute extent), so a block a snapshot lives on carries one
  refcount per referencing root and can never be reallocated while any holder lives. `snapshot_drop`
  is the exact inverse: it decrefs the identical block set (via the enqueue+drain path), freeing a
  block iff no remaining root reaches it. The K8a security properties are UNWEAKENED — no committed
  block is ever overwritten (allocation still requires current == frozen == 0), and the single
  512 B root-flip stays the only in-place write; K8b only lets refcounts exceed 0/1. Crash safety:
  the index-removal + queue-enqueue is ONE atomic commit, so a power cut mid-drop leaves the entry
  on the persistent reclaim queue for the next mount's eager drain — no block lost, none
  double-freed. **fsck extended (the standing STOP-class note in `fsck.rs` is discharged):** the
  reachability walk now spans the live root AND every retained root, so a snapshot-only block is not
  mis-reported as a leak; repair rebuilds TRUE multi-root refcounts (a flat count-of-1 rebuild would
  under-count shared blocks, and a later drop could then free a block another root still lives on).
  **Authority.** Snapshot drop is destructive and follows the BANDY owner-only-destructive ruling:
  `SnapshotEntry::drop_permitted(invoker, kernel_principal)` admits the recorded creator principal
  or the kernel. The library records the creator and exposes the decision; the enforcement point is
  the ACL/verb layer. The only day-one drop surface is the kernel shell (`usnapdrop`), which runs at
  kernel authority — the trivial `invoker == kernel_principal` case — so no new privileged path is
  opened; the creator is recorded (`"kernel"` from the shell) for when a per-principal surface
  arrives. v1 retention cap 16 is a clean policy refusal (`SnapshotCapReached`), not a format bound.
- **Proof (K8b).** `:: K8b-snap: … PASS [w=0x7f] ::` (uncounted; REQUIREd in `pi4-regression.spec`)
  — retain the committed tree, overwrite the live file, byte-verify the snapshot's OLD data blocks
  still hold the OLD bytes read straight off the block device (never-overwrite + block sharing),
  confirm a churn of fresh allocations never lands on the retained block set, drop → eager
  reclamation frees the snapshot-only blocks (free count rises) fsck-clean and re-allocatable, and a
  POWER CUT MID-DRAIN (the enqueue-only half + a genuine remount) converges (the queue resumes and
  empties on the next mount). Self-cleaning. Host twins in `unafs/tests/snapshot_logic.rs` add the
  tight-volume no-reuse pressure test, the two-snapshots-share-blocks / drop-one-keeps-the-other
  case, the cap-16 refusal, and the owner-or-kernel authority matrix. ✅✅ METAL-CONFIRMED
  2026-07-17 (attended sitting, single boot: 44/44 + 0 forbidden on silicon, K8b-snap
  [w=0x7f] incl. the power-cut-mid-drain convergence leg).
- **Snapshot reads inherit the live ACL — revocation reaches the past (K8c).** K8b proved the
  retained bytes SURVIVE; K8c governs who may READ them. Ruling of record (Peter, 2026-07-16, "we
  want high security"): a snapshot read is authorized by the **LIVE object's CURRENT ACL**,
  re-evaluated at read time — NOT by the ACL that was in force when the snapshot was taken.
  Snapshots preserve bytes, never authority. ONE evaluator at the verb layer
  (`read_authz(fs, live_id, principal)` in `fs/unafs.rs`): EVERY snapshot-read surface (`usnapcat`,
  `usnapls`, `snapshot_read`, the host mirror) defers to it, keyed on the stable logical inode id of
  the LIVE object. **Honest scoping (lens A fold, 2026-07-16):** `read_authz` enforces the same
  SEMANTICS as the live syscall path — current-ACL; grant rights honored, not just grant presence (a
  `grants:<p>` row admits a read only if its `rw`/`r`/`w` value carries the READ right, decoded by
  the syscall layer's own `rights_from_native` and tested against its `CAP_READ` bit — a write-only
  grantee reads neither live nor snapshot); fail-closed on a deleted object — but it is a
  kernel-verb-layer evaluator DISTINCT from the syscall layer's OwnedFile/FileGrant machinery.
  **Ledgered follow-up: unify the two evaluators** so the verb layer and the syscall layer share one
  implementation, not two implementations of one ruleset. Consequences, each proven by the witness:
  **(a)** a principal who cannot read the live object cannot read any snapshot of it — including a
  grantee whose grant lacks the read right; **(b)** dropping a grant (or changing the owner) on the
  live object *retroactively* closes every retained copy to that principal — revocation is total and
  reaches the past; **(c)** an object DELETED from the live tree has no live ACL row, so the
  current-ACL check **fails closed for EVERY principal**, the owner and kernel authority included
  (`DenyNoLiveObject`, traced) — the retained bytes remain on disk but are unreadable through the
  enforced path. **This is FINAL (Peter, 2026-07-17): deleted-object snapshot read is RULED
  fail-closed — not an open question, and NOT softened to the K8 DESIGN doc's weaker "owner-only
  fallback" alternative.** The crate `SnapshotView` carries no policy (read-only is a property of the type,
  not a check); enforcement lives at the verb/ACL seam. No K8a/K8b property is weakened: reads
  mutate nothing (no refcount, no reclaim queue, no root flip).
- **Proof (K8c).** `:: K8c-snapread: … PASS [w=0xff] ::` (uncounted; REQUIREd in
  `pi4-regression.spec`) — set an owner + a read grant (`r`) + a WRITE-ONLY grant (`w`) on a native
  object, retain it, overwrite the live file, then read the SNAPSHOT as several principals: owner and
  read-grantee read the OLD bytes; an impostor is refused from the snapshot by the SAME evaluator that
  refuses the live read; the write-only grantee is refused both live and snapshot (rights-aware);
  dropping the read grant retroactively refuses the previously-permitted grantee; and after the live
  object is deleted even its owner is refused (fail-closed, traced). Self-cleaning. Host twins in
  `unafs/tests/snapshot_read.rs` cover the crate view (old bytes after overwrite AND after delete,
  attrs as-of-snapshot, frozen-to-post-snapshot-changes, reads leave the free ledger / reclaim queue /
  root generation intact + fsck clean, dropped-snapshot view fails closed). Metal rides the next Pi
  sitting.
- **K8b dual-lens review ledger (2026-07-16 — zero surviving must-fix after a THREE-PASS
  fix-verify loop; the loop's history is itself the security story).** Lens A's initial
  should-fix (a NoSpace-failing `snapshot_create` stranded its tree-wide increfs — in-RAM
  mount poisoned, unrepairable in place) was fixed via `thaw()` unwind; lens A's re-verify
  then REFUSED the fix with a reproduced MUST-FIX: entry-snapshot restore composed with the
  K8a write path's un-unwound NoSpace residue put reachable blocks at refcount 0 in BOTH
  views — the allocator legally reallocated live blocks (16 KiB of committed data overwritten
  on disk in the probe; strictly worse than the pre-fix wedge). Final shape (LC ruling,
  FIX-VERIFIED pass 3): **`txn_unwind` reloads root/imap/refmap from the COMMITTED root on
  disk** via `load_committed` (the mount loader itself, factored — validation can never
  drift), so unwind is committed-consistent by construction and HEALS prior write residue as
  a side effect; a reload failure poisons the allocator CLOSED (no allocation, reads fine,
  remount recovers). `RefMap::thaw` is dead code again by design (warning doc). Standing
  NOTEs, each real but bounded:
  - *K8a mutating paths still carry no unwind* (untouched — metal-confirmed code): their
    NoSpace failure mode is the pre-existing safe WEDGE (frozen view blocks further commits;
    remount recovers), now also healed opportunistically by the next failed snapshot txn.
    A uniform txn discipline is future work (K8-batch territory).
  - *fsck(false) is presence-not-magnitude* (doc caveat in fsck.rs): over-refcounts of
    reachable blocks are invisible to the read-only scan; `repair` is the ground-truth rebuild.
  - *fsck(true) on a poison-closed mount mid-inner-txn* is an untested edge (repair would walk
    the mid-txn imap) — remount first is the recovery of record.
  - *Snapshot-time vs current ACL*: a snapshot retains the OLD inode incl. its OLD K6 ACL
    attrs. No snapshot READ path exists today (list/create/drop only), so the question is
    dormant at K8b — **K8c SETTLED it: snapshot-mediated reads re-evaluate the CURRENT ACL**
    (current-ACL, rights-aware, deleted-object fail-closed final — see the K8c bullet above).
  - *Creator stamping contract*: the library accepts an arbitrary creator string; every
    current surface is privileged and hard-codes it. A future ring-3 snapshot surface MUST
    stamp the kernel-authenticated principal (BANDY-STAMP discipline) and actually invoke
    `drop_permitted` — the enforcement hook exists but is vacuous today.
  - *Cap-16 is enforced at create only* (policy, per V1): mount/fsck accept an over-cap
    hostile index (each entry still bounds-checked; O(n) fsck walks bounded by the 4 MiB
    codec ceiling).

### Process & supply chain
- [ ] Adversarial review before metal and before merge on every arc (standing rule, `CLAUDE.md`)
- [ ] Code-signing / "self vs non-self" loader check (design: `dev/OS/04_SECURITY_IMMUNITY/intrusion_detection.md`) — **U2 (x86, 2026-07-02) and M6g (aarch64, 2026-07-03) both landed the loadable-program path but NOT this check**: on both arches the FAT-loaded program is bounded only by size and contained by hardware isolation (ring-3/EL0 + per-page perms + the fault-kill net — see each arch ledger's untrusted-loader item). The gate must cover both loaders. Simple allowlist first, signatures when entropy + crypto land
